| 122 | **Wallet re-architected into one command-centre page — a luminous balance hero, a sliding dashboard sheet, every money action in a dialog (2026-09-29). SUPERSEDES the presentation of Decisions #55 / #60 / #63 (wallet); the finance backend, `/api/wallet/*` and `@projective/types/finance` are kept.** **(A) One page.** The wallet's lane, header band, footer rig and seven deep pages are purged with every presentation file (19 components, 19 islands, 11 stylesheets, 14 core modules, 7 routes). `/wallet` is a bare canvas — `(dashboard)/_layout.tsx` resolves no lane or band for it — holding ONE island, `WalletHome`: a hero pinned `position: sticky` under the top bar and a rounded dashboard sheet that overlaps the hero's lower edge by a negative margin and slides over it in the native window scroll. The hero pins only on viewports at least 36rem tall, because on a shorter one a pinned hero makes its own lower half unreachable, and a control focused under the sheet scrolls the hero back into view. The retired deep pages answer a thin `[section].tsx` with a **308** to a section of the one page, keeping `w` and `display` (`transactions` → `#transactions`, `activity` → `#cash-flow`, `payouts` · `funding` · `invoices` · `access` → `#upcoming`, `methods` → `#methods`); any other segment is a 404. **(B) The hero:** a scope pill (the account and the currency it is HELD in; a popover of real `?w=` links); the total through `MoneyView` at a fluid size floored at `--text-3xl`, its weight overridden to `--fw-medium`; Available · In escrow · Clearing (+ On hold only when non-zero), each explained by a Tooltip and a visually-hidden sentence; four glass pills (Top up · Transfer · Withdraw · Split) with the rest in a More menu. A capability-absent action is ABSENT; a verification- or processor-blocked one is rendered-and-LOCKED and opens an explainer naming the reason (the #60 rule); a single overflow action becomes its own pill rather than a one-item menu. **(C) The sheet:** Accounts rail (personal · tax pot · vaults · the "All accounts" rollup, each tile a real `?w=` link); Cash flow (Week · Month · Quarter · Year mirrored into `?flow=`, the totals as the legend, CSS bars of money in over money out on ONE shared peak with a keyboard ⁄ pointer readout and a visually-hidden table twin, colours run through the dataviz validator); Upcoming (verification · spend approvals with Approve ⁄ Decline · bills · fundable stages · escrow holds · clearing releases with a progress ring · recurring rules · the payout schedule · the smoother, each carrying only the actions the server offered); Transactions (Today · Yesterday · Earlier from the server's own date label; a row opens its detail; Load more; CSV export); Payment methods. Every write is a `Dialog` — a bottom sheet at ≤767px — running compose → review → commit with an idempotency key per attempt, one dialog at a time (a new one replaces, never stacks). **(D) Backend, additive and presentation-only:** `ActivityRange` gains `7d` (Week); the overview's escrow holds are GROUPED PER STAGE and labelled `project · stage` — a stage holds one escrow per funded ticket, so two tickets rendered as two identical rows while the hero said "Held on 1 active stage"; the business `invoicesDueAmount` is summed in the held currency and converted once — per-invoice conversion printed £48,818.89 beside an escrow total of £48,818.90, the rounding class the file's own comment forbids. Registry glyphs `arrow-up-right`, `arrow-down-left` and `repeat` (§B.7.1). **(E) A package defect found by measurement:** under `dir="rtl"` every `MoneyView` scrambled — "£3,414.25" rendered "25.3,414£" and "−£7,086.61" rendered "61.7,086£ −" — because a split figure's sign · symbol · major · minor are flex children that reverse, after which the bidi algorithm reorders the digits. `.ui-money__amount` and `.ui-money__origin` are now isolated left-to-right (the `PaymentCard` card-number technique), recorded as a §A.6 "Figures" row; this corrects every money figure on every surface under RTL. **Defects in this pass's own work, found by measurement and fixed:** the hero printed "£27,036.30" beside a "Personal · USD" pill with nothing reconciling the two (the total is a DERIVED figure and carries no origin) — a "Converted from USD" disclosure with the rate in its Tooltip; the pill's currency measured 4.21:1 and the dark hero's labels 4.46–4.48:1 at 390px (the dark brand teal lifts the glow) — full ink for the currency, a softer dark glow and a firmer soft ink, now ≥4.63:1 in all four states; the phone pill row laid out unevenly because a Tooltip wraps a locked pill in an inline anchor, so only the bare More button took `flex: 1` — an equal-column grid; the payout-schedule dialog always opened on "Monthly" and sent `instant: false`, so Save would have silently rewritten a Threshold schedule — it now opens on the current one; the ledger detail disclosed the original amount twice; zero cash-flow totals read "+£0.00"; Net printed a hyphen beside Money out's true minus; a refused Continue showed one error at a time — every invalid field now marks at once and focus moves to the first. **Verified in a real browser against the live local stack**, four seeded views (a personal seller, a team-vault owner, a business-vault owner, the read-only rollup) at 1440 and 390, light and dark, LTR and RTL: zero horizontal overflow in either direction, zero console errors, hero text ≥4.63:1, pills 44px tall on desktop and an even 84×74 grid on a phone, the phone dialog a full-width sheet docked at the viewport bottom; the locked explainer, More menu, ledger detail, payout schedule and the Split preview (40/30/30 stakes); `?flow=` switching (7 and 12 bars off `range=7d` ⁄ `12m`) and the keyboard readout; the section redirects; and ONE real write — a US$1.00 transfer (Kwame's personal wallet → North Loop) that posted with its idempotency key and refreshed the hero (£4,119.87 → £4,119.08) and a new Today band in place (the local seed keeps it; `deno task db:reset` restores). `deno task check` exit 0, `deno lint` clean over 1,741 files, unit **1,645 passed ⁄ 4 failed** — the four pre-existing `theme-engine.test.ts` failures (#96(a)); the 11 new wallet tests pass. **No DB migration**, no lifecycle change, and no Dev Context axis (the old wallet axes were already removed by the "Remove mocks" commits; layout direction stays global). **FLAGGED — surface, do not silently resolve:** (a) **the brief's hexes (`#288690`, `#3B1262`) were not used** — §3 is token-only, so the gradient runs `--primary` into a `--tertiary`-derived indigo; the brief's own formula `color-mix(in srgb, var(--primary) 70%, #3B1262)` evaluates to a petrol blue rather than a purple, and the theme has no purple ramp. (b) **Two recorded, scoped exceptions need the owner's ratification:** the design language forbids a "purple-blue hero blur", and §B.4.3 allows glass only on pinned bars, floating sheets and marks over photography — the hero and its glass pills are neither, and ship at the brief's direction, scoped to `.wlt-hero` (noted in DESIGN_SYSTEM). (c) **"Exchange" has no backend capability** — the fourth pill is Split where the server offers it, else More. (d) The brief's role and date "badges" are inline meta text and a progress ring — §B.11 forbids a pill for non-actionable metadata. (e) **Capabilities no longer surfaced on the wallet:** the Standing gauge, the business burn-down and spending caps, the access matrix and audit log, statement and invoice history, payout history, and the team split roster (its stakes survive in the Split dialog). Their endpoints remain; bringing any back is a product decision. (f) Escrow rows are converted per stage while the hero converts the sum, so a row can differ from the metric by a penny — inherent to converting at two granularities. (g) `live-wallet.ts` still mints `/wallet/transactions` and `/wallet/payouts` hrefs — harmless through the 308s, but they should name the sections. (h) The "All accounts" rollup carries available cash only (the server's aggregate has no escrow), so it is labelled "Available across N accounts" and hidden from a single-account viewer, where it only repeats Personal. | `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` design language · §A.6 · §B.4.3 · §B.7.1 · `ROUTING.md` · `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Sitemap · `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §Backend Services · `packages/types/finance/wallet.ts` · `packages/backend/services/finance/live-wallet.ts` · `packages/ui/icons/core/paths.tsx` · `packages/ui/display/{styles/money-view.css,components/PaymentCard.tsx}` · `apps/web/features/wallet/**` · `apps/web/routes/(dashboard)/{_layout.tsx,wallet/{index,[section]}.tsx}` · `apps/web/styles/global.css` · `apps/web/utils/storage-keys.ts` · Decisions #54 / #55 / #60 / #63 / #69 / #92 / #96 |
| 123 | **Wallet polish — the hero behind the glass chrome, a header band with the range ruler, the four-state allocation meter, the Finance drawer, and the MoneyView minor-digit baseline (2026-09-29). REFINES Decision #122.** **(A) The hero runs behind the chrome.** `.wlt-hero` pulls itself up by `--wlt-hero-lift` (top bar + header band) and pads its content back down by the same amount, so the glass top bar and band show the aurora while every control starts below them and stays clickable; it stays an in-flow box in the window scroll, sticky at `top: 0` only where the viewport is ≥ 36rem tall. The lift is declared ON the hero, not `:root` — a custom property that reads another resolves where it is declared, so a `:root` lift never saw the band height the frame sets. The two shell frames between the hero and the chrome clip their overflow; they are released on the BLOCK axis only while they hold the wallet (`.ui-shell-frame:has(.wlt-hero)`), keeping the inline clip that contains wide rigs. **(B) A header band returns, from the URL alone.** `walletHeaderFor` (composed into `middleNavHeaderFor`; no lane, no footer) mounts `WalletBand`: a segmented **range ruler** (`7D · 1M · 3M · 6M · 1Y · 5Y · All`, a radiogroup with a sliding thumb positioned from the index alone — no measurement — and mirrored by one sign; the checked cell's own ink carries the state, so a frozen animation clock can never make it lie), a **display-currency trigger** writing the SAME store as the account menu's picker (`commitDisplayCurrency`), an icon link to `/settings`, and the **Finance drawer** toggle. Band and page are two hydration roots joined by client-only signals in `wallet-state.ts` (`walletPeriod` · `walletDrawerOpen` · `walletDisplay`), each root painting its own server-resolved prop until the client says otherwise (the #69 request-race rule). The Cash-flow section lost its own period picker (one control, not two) and names its window in the heading. `ActivityRange` gains `180d` · `5y` · `all` in the Zod SSOT; `activityOf` sizes each window from one table, `all` from the oldest movement read, and `flowSeries` labels a window longer than a year by month AND year. `?flow=` now carries `7d|1m|3m|6m|1y|5y|all`; the pre-ruler names still resolve. **(C) The allocation meter** heads the sheet: one track divided between Available (`--primary`) · Clearing (`--warning`) · In escrow (`--success`) · Reserved (the aurora violet), widths `flex-grow` from the SAME four figures the server summed into the total — set directly, never transitioned — with a 2px surface seam, a glyph + label + share legend (largest-remainder whole percents, `<1%` for a sliver), and a Tooltip per segment carrying figure · share · meaning; hovering one quiets the rest on `opacity` only. One pure `allocationOf` + `fundStateHint` in `wallet-home.ts` feeds the meter AND the hero's metrics, so their order and sentences cannot drift. **(D) The Finance drawer** (package `Drawer`, docked inline-end): the KYC/KYB gate as an `Alert` when one is open; every action the server offers as an icon-first grid (Top up · Transfer · Withdraw · Recurring deposit · Add payment method · Payout schedule, then anything else offered), locked ones rendered-and-explained exactly as in the hero; and records as rows — All transactions and Cash flow scroll to their section and move focus to its heading, and on a business vault **Invoices & statements** opens a new dialog reading `/api/wallet/invoices` (bills due · the accruing month · past statements). No route was added. **(E) Glass and cards.** The scope switcher, the More menu and the currency list are one `wlt-glass-pop` treatment (a `--glass-veil-panel` veil on a `::before` underlay, a hairline, `--radius-xl`, rows hovering as `--radius-md` pills, secondary ink `--on-surface-variant`); the sheet's first `--wlt-sheet-fade` is a blurred veil fading to opaque `--surface`; account tiles take the card contract (`--card-pad` · `--card-radius` · `--surface-1`, no resting border, a `--card-hover-lift` + `--elevation-medium` lift on a link tile only, the rail padded so the lift is not clipped). **(F) MoneyView (package, every surface):** the minor run sits on the major run's BASELINE — no longer raised — sized `var(--minor-size)` and faded `var(--minor-fade)` through per-step `--money-minor-size`/`--money-minor-fade` hooks (hero 0.42em; body and micro unsplit at full ink). Measured: identical baselines at hero/key/body, faded pence ≥ 4.83:1 on the light surface. This reverses the component's documented "never dim the tail" rationale at the owner's direction. **DEFECTS FOUND BY MEASUREMENT, NOT INSPECTION:** (1) the band inherited a column layout because `.wlt-band` already named the ledger's date bands — renamed `.wlt-header`; (2) a drawer action opened its dialog after a fixed 280ms, but the drawer's exit starts a render after the press, so the dialog captured a vanishing drawer button as its return target and focus fell to `<body>` on close — the hand-off now waits for the drawer to leave the DOM and parks focus on the band's toggle first (verified for a pointer-opened drawer too); (3) the sheet's rim and body met at two hard stops on the same offset, leaving an anti-aliased, half-transparent row that read as a dark hairline — the body now starts a step inside the rim; (4) the dataviz validator put the brief's order — brand teal beside success green — at ΔE 14.8, under the 15 a full-colour reader needs, so the states are ordered by how soon the money can be spent (the direction money travels, read backwards), which measures ΔE 19.5, and the hero's metrics follow; (5) the wallet now renders a middle-nav frame, which switched on the top bar's outer frame "L" — and on a phone that L and both fixed frame fills sat at the hidden rail's 64px, the pre-existing Decision #108(e) defect on every framed route: fixed at the root by zeroing `--shell-sidebar-cur-w` on `.ui-app-shell` below `--bp-md` and hiding the outer L there, as the nested seams already were; (6) the owner's `corner-shape: scoop` fillet is one engine's feature — elsewhere it painted a solid block over the content's corner, and it ate pointer events there — redrawn as a radial gradient, logical and mirrored, `pointer-events: none`; (7) the package `Drawer` docked logically but slid physically, so under `dir="rtl"` a closed right drawer rested on-screen — the RTL transforms are mirrored; (8) the gradient commit left `--wlt-hero-glow-*` and `--wlt-hero-midnight` dangling — the glow layers painted nothing (removed) and the lock badge had no ground (re-pointed to `--wlt-hero-deep`); (9) the accruing statement printed "accruing" twice and a past statement's trailing figure was unlabelled. **Verified in-browser** against the live local stack as three seeded personas (a personal seller, a team-vault owner, a business owner) at 1440 · 800 · 390, light + dark, LTR + RTL: zero horizontal overflow everywhere; the ruler by click, arrows and End with the URL and heading following and the thumb landing exactly on the checked cell; a real currency switch through the band (restored to GBP afterwards); four segments at exact proportions (545 · 242 · 363 · 61 of 1,210px); the drawer → dialog → return-focus path; the invoices dialog on live bills; drawer text ≥ 7.77:1 in dark; the detector clean. `deno task check` 0 errors, `deno lint` clean over 1,745 files, unit **1,648 passed ⁄ 4 failed** — the four pre-existing `theme-engine.test.ts` failures (#96(a)); 3 new wallet tests. **No DB migration**, no lifecycle change, no Dev Context axis (the range and the drawer are view state, not a simulatable persona or gate). **FLAGGED — surface, do not silently resolve:** (a) **the brief's hexes (`#288690` · `#268C66` · `#F19C13` · `#A013F1`) were not used** — the meter reads `--primary`/`--success`/`--warning` and the owner's own `--wlt-aurora-violet`; the validator still flags the brand teal's chroma (0.088 < 0.10) and, in dark, the theme's own success/warning as lighter than a categorical band — both pass contrast and both are theme decisions. (b) **"Reserved" is the dispute lockbox only.** The tax pot is a separate sub-wallet and the smoother holds no balance, so neither is in the total; folding them in would make the meter disagree with the figure above it. "On hold" is renamed "Reserved" wallet-wide. (c) **Three exceptions need the owner's ratification** — the translucent sheet rim against the solid-region gate (§3 gate 6), its blur and the glass popovers against functional transparency (gate 9), and the faded pence against MoneyView's prior rationale — each scoped and recorded in `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md`. (d) **No KYC/KYB flow exists**, so the gate banner offers a trigger only where the server names a destination or the next step is a wallet action; an unverified account with neither gets the requirement and no button (gate 11). (e) The band's settings icon links `/settings`, still a placeholder page. (f) The brief's "Financial & Burn-Down Analytics" row is labelled **Cash flow** and lands there: the burn-down is not surfaced (#122(e)). Invoices & statements is business-only because the backend returns nothing for any other wallet, and no statement has a PDF. (g) 5Y and All are read through the same 1,000-row ledger window as 1Y, so a longer history under-reports and All's span starts at the oldest row read. (h) The aurora is literal hex in `wallet-tokens.css` by the owner's own commit, and the commented-out dark overrides stay retired except the sheet shadow. (i) Not verified: the transitions' motion (the pane freezes them; end states were read with reduced motion or a finished animation) and a smooth in-page scroll. | `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` design language · §B.4.2 · §B.4.3 · §C.1 · Part D.3 · `ROUTING.md` · `packages/types/finance/wallet.ts` · `packages/backend/services/finance/{live-wallet,wallet-ledger}.ts` · `packages/ui/display/styles/money-view.css` · `packages/ui/feedback/styles/drawer.css` · `packages/ui/navigation/styles/{app-shell,top-bar}.css` · `apps/web/features/wallet/**` · `apps/web/features/view/styles/entity-view.css` · `apps/web/routes/(dashboard)/_layout.tsx` · `apps/web/routes/api/wallet/activity.ts` · `apps/web/styles/global.css` · Decisions #60 / #69 / #92 / #96 / #108 / #110 / #122 |
| 124 | **Wallet — the Finance drawer becomes the middle-nav lane, the header band becomes hero corner tools, the range ruler pins, and transactions · analytics · invoices get pages (2026-09-29). REFINES Decision #123.** At the product owner's direction. **(A) The drawer is deleted** (`FinanceDrawer`, `wallet-drawer.css`, the drawer→dialog hand-off and its focus race) and its content is a `WalletLane` island on every `/wallet*` route (`walletLaneFor`, composed into `laneFor`): the wallet's page links as real addresses, the verification gate, and every offered money action as an icon grid, with the standard collapsed icon rail. The lane reads the overview itself so its actions paint in the first byte, then follows the overview and cash-flow window the page publishes (`walletOverviewLive` · `walletFlowLive`, client-written only). An action opens its dialog IN PLACE: the one `WalletHome` island renders all four pages and hosts the dialogs on each, so a lane press on `/wallet/analytics` opens Transfer there and focus returns to the lane button on close (verified). **(B) The header band is deleted** (`WalletBand`, `wallet-header-slot`): the display-currency trigger and the settings link are glass buttons (`.wlt-tool`, the pills' own glass) in the hero's top inline-end corner, and the hero lifts by the top bar alone. **(C) The range ruler pins**: a zero-height sticky strip at the head of the page (`.wlt-rulebar`, `top: topbar + --wlt-tool-gap`, above the sheet) holding the ruler on a `--surface-2` chrome-veil glass ground with a hairline and a slight drop shadow, level with the corner tools; the hero reserves that row before its content, and in a narrow content column the ruler steps to the start and leaves the tools' width clear. Only the overview and analytics draw it. Measured at 1440: ruler and tools both at y=60, the ruler still at y=60 with the sheet scrolled 700px under it, and the ruler — not the sheet — at the hit-test point; 390: ruler 16–270, tools 283–374, no overlap. **(D) Pages**: `/wallet/transactions` (the full paged ledger + CSV), `/wallet/analytics` (cash flow, the balance breakdown, and new server-share breakdowns by category and by project), `/wallet/invoices` (the extracted `InvoicesPanel`, read server-side; a non-business wallet is told it is not billed, and the lane lists the page only for a business vault) — each under a compact hero naming the page. The overview keeps its sections but previews six ledger lines with "View all" and links Cash flow to Analytics; the invoices DIALOG is gone. `activity` now 308s to `/wallet/analytics`. Below 767px the lane's links and gate move to the top of the sheet (a strip), never drawn twice. **Verified** in a browser against the live local stack as Kwame (personal) and Priya (business vault), 1440 and 390, light + dark, `dir="rtl"` (lane, tools and ruler mirror; thumb on the checked cell; zero horizontal overflow), ruler clicks updating `?flow=` and the section heading, the 308, zero console errors; `deno task check` exit 0, lint clean, wallet tests 16/16 (2 new, for `walletPageHref` / `viewOfPath`). **FLAGGED — surface, do not silently resolve:** (a) every wallet page now does two overview reads per navigation (the lane's and the page's), because they are separate hydration roots and both must paint in the first byte; (b) sub-pages read the same payment data as the overview so their dialogs work — heavier than a ledger-only page needs; (c) the ruler's glass and the corner tools extend Decision #122's scoped glass exception (recorded in DESIGN_SYSTEM §B.4.3); (d) `/settings`, which the settings tool links to, is still a placeholder page; (e) `.claude/launch.json` was recreated with a `web-wallet` config (port 12974) that re-exports the `.env` service-role key, per the stale-machine-env note. | `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` §B.4.3 / top note · `ROUTING.md` · `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Sitemap · `apps/web/features/wallet/**` · `apps/web/routes/(dashboard)/{_layout.tsx,wallet/*}` · Decisions #63 / #122 / #123 |
| 125 | **Stripe fiat rails, Phase 1 — card payments into the ledger, Connect payout accounts, Identity KYC and the signed webhook (2026-09-29).** Projective stays the ledger of record; Stripe is only the card and payout rail. **(A) SEPARATE CHARGES & TRANSFERS, NOT DESTINATION CHARGES — a conflict with the brief and with the prior `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` blueprint, flagged here rather than silently resolved.** Both named Destination Charges while describing money HELD by the platform and released later. A destination charge transfers to the connected account the moment the payment succeeds, names exactly one destination at charge time and cannot hold escrow — while a stage's payee may not exist when it is funded, a team payout splits across several people (`fn_split_team_payout`), and every escrow in this schema is held FROM a wallet by `projects.fund_stage`. So the card is charged on the PLATFORM account (a `transfer_group`, no `transfer_data` / `on_behalf_of` / `application_fee_amount`), the settled amount becomes an ordinary `topup` credit, and an escrow lock holds the stage from that wallet through the existing `fund_stage` — one escrow engine, not a card-specific second one; payouts leave later as Transfers to an Accounts v2 Express recipient. The blueprint was rewritten accordingly. **CONFIRMED by the product owner 2026-09-29** — Separate Charges & Transfers is the pattern. **(B) SCHEMA, folded in place (§1)** — the brief asked for new additive migrations; §1 governs, so there is no ALTER-on-top file, and no column or FK of `finance.escrows` / `wallets` / `transactions` was touched. New enum `finance.inbound_payment_status`; new table `finance.inbound_payments` (one row per attempt — the twin of `finance.payouts`, needed because `transactions` records only completed movements, so a started or declined payment had nowhere to live) with seven CHECKs; `finance.payout_accounts` edited in place (the five-value owner axis, a status vocabulary equal to what the v2 `stripe_transfers` capability can report, `updated_at`, one active account per owner); an `org.freelancer_profiles.kyc_tier` CHECK (1–3); six indexes including one-row-per-processor-id guards on chargebacks, payouts and verification cases; the `identity_sessions_per_day` param (5); and one new category-1 file, `00001230_functions_finance_stripe.sql`, with 18 definer functions. **(C) TWO DOORS.** User doors (`EXECUTE` → `authenticated`, authorised through `auth.uid()`) open, bind or abandon an attempt and can never say money arrived; processor doors (`EXECUTE` → `service_role` only, explicitly revoked from `authenticated`) apply only what the signature-verified webhook reports; the four internal helpers are executable by no role. An `escrow_lock` amount is NEVER the client's: it is recomputed as the sum `fund_stage` escrows by, and the client's figure is only a check (`PC409` when the stage was re-priced). Settlement runs `fund_stage` AS the payer who authorised it (`request.jwt.claim.sub` = `created_by`), in a subtransaction, so a lock refused at settlement rolls back only the lock and the money stays in the wallet with `lock_error`. Every money-moving call registers a key in `finance.idempotency_keys` AND sends the same key as Stripe's `Idempotency-Key` (`projective:<op>:<id>`); every webhook event is claimed as `stripe:<evt_id>` in the same transaction as its effect. Zero PII: only provider ids and Stripe's own codes are stored. **(D) SERVICES AND ROUTES.** Fat `PaymentBackendService` (`@server/services/finance/`) over `live-payments.ts` (the DB doors), `stripe-rails.ts` (SDK calls from pure parameter builders) and `stripe-webhook.ts` (a pure `planStripeEvent` + dispatch); `packages/backend/core/stripe.ts` (lazy typed config that validates key SHAPE — a placeholder counts as absent — API version `2026-08-26.dahlia` pinned, fetch client, `constructEventAsync` over SubtleCrypto); seven thin routes under `/api/finance/` (`escrow/intent`, `topup/intent`, `connect/{onboarding,refresh,return,status}`, `identity/session`) plus the signed `webhooks/stripe` (raw body, 1 MiB cap, `no-store`, 400 on a bad signature, a livemode mismatch ignored). Zod SSOT `@projective/types/finance/payments.ts`; the finance Zod types were also brought back into line with the tables they mirror (`approve_spend` on `VaultCapability`, nullable `capCents` / `perTransactionCents`, `approvalThresholdCents`, `fundState`, `held`, a signed `amountCents` on subscription events, the five-value `FinanceOwnerType` on methods and billing). **(E) ENV AND A TRACKED-SECRETS FINDING.** `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` / `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, plus a NEW development-only `STRIPE_API_BASE` (stripe-mock; honoured only with a test key and never under `DENO_ENV=production`). The rails are live only with `FINANCE_BACKEND_LIVE=true` AND a well-formed key; otherwise every Stripe route answers 503. **Found while documenting where the keys go:** `.env`, `.env.development` and `.env.production` are TRACKED by git again despite Decision #11 and `.gitignore` (an ignore rule does not apply to a tracked file), and `apps/web/main.ts` loads only `.env.local` then `.env` — so a key written to `.env.development`, as `.env.example`'s header used to advise, is both inert AND committed by the next `git add`. The header, the Stripe block and the SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE contract now say `.env.local`. **VERIFIED BY EXECUTION:** every SQL change dry-run in `BEGIN … ROLLBACK` and then applied to the live local Postgres; every door exercised as real roles (begin / replay / key reuse / re-priced stage / stranger / `authenticated` refused at settle; settle crediting and locking a team stage with the paying business's net balance unchanged; a refused lock keeping the money; replayed and unmatched events; a dispute freezing the escrow it funded; failure → success → late cancel; identity `requires_input` → `verified` → late event; Connect record / sync / `payout_ready`; transfer match and mismatch); the HTTP layer end to end through the real route handlers with minted JWTs against `stripe-mock` (401 / 403 / 422 / 201 / 200-replay; webhook applied / replayed / 400 / ignored / unmatched; 503 without a key); `deno task check` exit 0, lint clean over 1,772 files, unit **1,685 passed / 4 failed** — the four pre-existing `theme-engine.test.ts` failures (#96(a)) — with 35 new tests, one of which reads the migrations and fails if a processor door is ever granted to a client role. **LIVE TEST against the owner's Stripe sandbox (2026-09-29, owner-approved), with real events forwarded by `stripe listen`:** a card top-up (PaymentIntent → test Visa → signed `payment_intent.succeeded` → wallet credited once); a decline then retry on ONE payment (`failed · generic_decline`, no credit → retry `succeeded`, credited once); a disputed charge (`charge.dispute.created` → `opened` chargeback linked to the wallet and ledger credit); a real Accounts v2 Express account + hosted onboarding link; a real Identity session bound to its case, then cancelled (→ `expired`); and a redelivered event (200, no second credit). All six deliveries answered 200. **It found two defects stripe-mock could not:** Stripe REFUSES a v2 recipient account without `contact_email` (`invalid_fields`) — `finance.payout_account_for` now returns the caller's own `auth.users.email`, handed to Stripe and never stored; and onboarding reported `pending_verification` while the same response's capability said `restricted` — a new account's reported status is now synced before the first answer. Local test rows were removed afterwards; the Stripe test objects remain in the sandbox. **NOT VERIFIED, stated:** completing hosted onboarding or a `verified` identity outcome (both are Stripe-hosted forms a person fills in), a full `supabase db reset` of the consolidated chain, and a production build. **(I) BUILT (PHASES 2–4, same day).** The engine emits `--accent` / `--on-accent` from `dsConfig.accentSeed` at the four pinned ratios. `Button` takes `ButtonSeverity = Severity | "accent" | "neutral"`: `accent` is `filled`-only by type (`ButtonAccentTone`; `--accent` as ink on light `--surface` is 2.97:1), its preset `.ui-button--accent` blending hover/press toward `--on-scrim` (`n1.tone(100)` = `#ffffff` in every state) so the label rises — light 5.77 → 6.31 → 6.62, light HC 7.07 → 7.74 → 8.11, dark 7.39 → 7.97 → 8.31, dark HC 11.63 → 12.17 → 12.44; `neutral` is tier 4, `outlined`-only by type (`ButtonNeutralTone`), `--on-surface` ink on a `var(--hairline-strong)` border with the `outlined` tint-soft hover. The shared `Severity` union is untouched. The dev Context Switcher gains a Theme group (`dev-theme.ts`, `DevThemeControls.tsx`) driving both seeds, `radiusScale` and `shadowIntensity` live — a recorded §5 exception: `DevThemeKnobs` + `DEV_THEME_DEFAULTS` (from `DEFAULT_CONFIG`) but no `DevOption` list or `data-dev-*` reflect, since the engine reads `dsConfig` directly; a session-only copy (`pj.session.dev.themeOverrides`) lives only while a knob is off its default. The checkout's commits are all `<Button severity="accent" variant="filled" rounded>`, the basket summary's Proceed to Checkout wears the same classes (it was `warning`), `--checkout-commit-*` and `.cko-commit` are deleted, and `checkout-commit-tokens.test.ts` is replaced by `checkout-commit-tier.test.ts` (markup contract) beside `card-step.ts` (while a card step is pending the rail's Buy Now is not rendered, so one amber Pay shows); the dialog footers pill their commits and escape hatches (#70); the basket footer band's Checkout is tier-5 ghost, and the rig's `CtaTier = "accent" | "ghost"`. The profile rig: Hire amber on a native `<button>`, Add to project monochrome wherever it renders, every other control `severity="neutral" variant="outlined"`; the availability status is unboxed text with an achromatic solid / hollow dot (#96 stands). The wash is gone from `TreeNav`, `LaneIconButton` and every feature rail / row DESIGN_SYSTEM §B.4.2 lists, each now `--surface-2` / `--on-surface` with a 2px `--on-surface` inline-start indicator where it is a row; `Tag` `subtle` is solid `--surface-2` and `outlined` / `subtle` ink is `--tag-ink` (worst 5.00:1). Tier 1 also renders on the wallet movement dialog's commits, `StripeElementPanel`'s Pay, the DM drawer's Fund escrow & confirm seat and the board footer's Checkout N; wallet configuration commits, spend-approval Approve, Accept Submission and Publish stay tier 3. **FLAGGED — needs a human, do NOT silently resolve:** (a) ~~the charge pattern (A)~~ — resolved, confirmed by the product owner 2026-09-29. (b) Individual clients cannot lock escrow by card — `finance.escrows.payer_business_id` is NOT NULL (inherits #56(a)); a top-up works for anyone. (c) Chargeback arbitration has no writer: a dispute records the case and freezes the escrow it funded, but won / lost / refunded and any negative ledger line are undecided, and `finance.chargeback_status` has no member for Stripe's `warning_*` / `needs_response` states. (d) `platform_fee_bp` is still seeded `0` against the SSOT's 500 (inherits #68(b)). (e) Wallet-read drift found, NOT fixed (another session's files): `wallet-scope.ts` can never surface `approve_spend` (`capabilitiesForRole("owner")` omits it, so `manage_members` does not expand to it); `live-buyer.ts` and `live-wallet.ts` read a NULL spending cap — which means uncapped — as 0; the wallet payout projection's status set (`in_transit`, no `cancelled`) disagrees with `finance.payout_status`; `finance.orders.payment_provider` (the processor, default `stripe`) and Zod `PaymentProvider` (the instrument class) name two axes with one word; and `reasonMeta` has no `chargeback` label. (f) No UI calls these routes yet: the wallet's Top up / Withdraw and checkout's card, device-wallet and PayPal options stay refused, `VerificationGate` still links the non-existent `/settings/verification`, and Stripe.js plus its CSP entries are not added. (g) Accounts v2 capability webhooks (thin events) are not subscribed, so a capability change is picked up only at the return URL or on a `status?sync=1` read. (h) Business KYB through Connect is not built. (i) The tracked `.env*` files (E) — untrack them (`git rm --cached`) and keep secrets in `.env.local`. | `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §Integration Blueprints 1 / 1.1 + §Environment Variable Contract · `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` §3.5 · `documentation/database/{finance/{Tables,Functions,Policies},org/Tables}.md` · `packages/types/finance/{payments,common,ledger,vault,methods,billing,plans,mod}.ts` (+ `payments.test.ts`, `payments.contract.test.ts`) · `packages/backend/core/{env,stripe}.ts` (+ `stripe.test.ts`) · `packages/backend/services/finance/{PaymentBackendService,live-payments,stripe-rails,stripe-webhook}.ts` (+ tests) · `packages/backend/mocks/registry.ts` · `apps/web/features/payments/core/respond.ts` · `apps/web/routes/api/finance/**` · `supabase/migrations/{00000004,00000011,00000017,00001230,00002001,00002013,00002510,00002520,00004005,00005001}*` · `deno.json` · `.env.example` · Decisions #2 / #11 / #54 / #55 / #56 / #68 / #96 / #122 |
| 126 | **Stripe fiat rails, Phase 2 — withdrawals, saved cards, recurring deposits, disputes, verification, card checkout and the CSP (2026-09-30). CLOSES Decision #125 flags (b), (d), (e), (f), (g), (h) and most of (c).** Projective stays the ledger of record; Stripe stays the card and payout rail; Separate Charges & Transfers is unchanged. **(A) SCHEMA, FOLDED IN PLACE — the brief asked for new `0400+`/timestamped additive migrations; root §1 governs, so there are none.** The only new file is the category-1 `00001240_functions_finance_money_movement.sql`. Everything else is edited into the consolidated files: `platform_fee_bp` **500** (was seeded `0` against Decision #2, #125(d)); `finance.escrows.payer_business_id` loses `NOT NULL` and gains a sibling `payer_user_id` (FK → `org.users_public`) under `ck_escrows_one_payer CHECK (num_nonnulls(…) = 1)` — **the business FK is untouched**; relaxing a NOT NULL on a protected table is done on the brief's explicit instruction ("handle `payer_business_id` NOT NULL additively without breaking the FK"), which is the human permission §1 asks for. Plus `transactions.created_at DEFAULT clock_timestamp()` (with `now()`, every row of one transaction shared an instant and the ledger ordered them by random uuid, so a top-up and the order it paid could read in the wrong order); `inbound_payments.deposit_rule_id`; `deposit_rules.created_by` (guarded as derived, so a client-inserted rule never runs); `chargebacks.unrecovered_cents` + a resolved-at/status CHECK; the new definer-only `finance.processor_customers` (one Stripe Customer per owner, the `cus_…` id only). **(B) INDIVIDUAL-CLIENT ESCROW (#125(b), #56(a)).** `fn_hold_ticket_escrow`, `projects.fund_stage`, `begin_card_payment` and every release/refund/fair-exit path now take an individual payer: only the project's OWNER may fund it, an individual stage is checked all-or-nothing against their wallet (`PF402` "…pay by card instead"), and money returns to the wallet type the person holds (`fn_escrow_payer_wallet_type`). Releases charge `fn_escrow_fee_bp` (a negotiated rate, else 5%). **(C) THE DOORS** (`00001240`, two-door pattern, processor doors `service_role`-only and pinned by `payments.contract.test.ts`): `begin_payout` (debits FIRST so money in flight cannot be spent twice; KYC `PK403`, KYB `PK403`, destination `PA403`, balance `PF402`) · `complete_payout` · `fail_payout` (credits back, `payout_reversal`); `card_owner_for` · `record_processor_customer` · `record_saved_card`; `create_deposit_rule` · `claim_due_deposit_rules` (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, one charge per period, missed periods skipped, three strikes pause) · `bind_scheduled_payment`; `set_income_smoother`; `my_verification_status`; `ensure_purchase_wallet`; `record_dispute_closed` (won → escrows back to `held`; lost → frozen escrows refunded to the payer, then the disputed amount debited from that wallet as far as it covers, the rest `unrecovered_cents`). `fn_payout_owner` gains a `business` scope and `sync_payout_account` mirrors a business's verified Connect account onto `org.business_profiles.kyb_*` — **Connect onboarding IS the Level-3 KYB check** (#125(h)). **(D) WEBHOOKS:** `charge.dispute.closed`, `account.updated` and `setup_intent.succeeded` on the snapshot endpoint; a SECOND endpoint, `/api/finance/webhooks/stripe-v2` (`STRIPE_THIN_WEBHOOK_SECRET`), for the Accounts v2 thin events, which re-reads the account from Stripe before applying anything (#125(g)). **(E) SURFACES (#125(f)).** `/settings/verification` (the Level-2 identity check through Stripe Identity, the Connect payout account, each business's KYB), which every wallet lock about identity or payouts now links to (the banner pointed at a non-existent `/settings/verification` page before). Wallet: Top up through the Payment Element, Withdraw, Add card (SetupIntent), Recurring deposit, Income Smoother enrolment with its fee previewed. Checkout: the card route is live — a chosen saved card is charged server-side and settles through the webhook into the paying wallet, the order is then placed from that wallet; a bank's 3-D Secure challenge is answered in place by Stripe.js `handleNextAction` (`CardPaymentHandoff.confirmation`); a new card is entered in the Add-card form. `GET /api/finance/{invoices,statements}/[id]/pdf` renders a PDF per request (a dependency-free PDF 1.4 writer). Stripe.js is the versioned `dahlia` build, loaded on demand by `features/payments/core/stripe-js.ts`; the global middleware serves a CSP naming exactly Stripe's script/frame/API origins, the Supabase storage origin and the no-cookie YouTube frame. **(F) #125(e) WALLET DRIFT, FIXED:** owner/admin get `approve_spend`; a NULL spending cap reads as "no cap", never 0; payout status passes through the real `finance.payout_status`; the `chargeback`/`payout_reversal` reasons have labels; a person's wallet total now includes escrow they funded as an individual client. Verified: 0 wallet-vs-last-row mismatches and 0 running-balance mismatches across every wallet after all the flows below. **DEFECTS FOUND BY MEASUREMENT, NOT INSPECTION:** (1) `record_dispute_closed` with `search_path=''` failed inside a ticket trigger that names an unqualified type — a trigger runs with its caller's `search_path` (fixed on the door; the trigger's own fragility is flagged below); (2) the CSP was built at module import, before `.env` loads, and silently blocked every local storage image — now built on the first request; (3) the intra-transaction ledger ordering above; (4) a client with no payout account could open Withdraw and was refused only after the review step — the wallet now locks it up front with "Set up payouts"; (5) the verification banner's "Add a payout method" opened the dialog that saves a card to pay IN with; (6) the Add-card "Name" field was saved nowhere (removed — the brief keeps brand, last4 and the `pm_…` only); (7) **the checkout's saved-card picker was sent to the server and ignored** (the buyer picked a card, then was asked to enter one again) — a §3 gate-11 defect the live card route exposed; (8) the checkout Add-card modal still said Stripe "isn't connected" and was locked; (9) the card-currency rule refused only after Pay — the card option now carries it up front. **VERIFIED BY EXECUTION** against the local stack and a Stripe TEST-mode sandbox (`stripe listen` forwarding): the migrations dry-run in `BEGIN … ROLLBACK` and applied live, with a rolled-back functional suite over every door as real roles; a Payment Element top-up (+$25.00, webhook 200); a SetupIntent card save (Visa 4242, idempotent with the webhook backstop); a recurring deposit set up in the UI and charged by the scheduler once (+$15.00, a second run claimed nothing, the rule advanced a month); a card checkout charging the saved card (+$49.00 top-up then −$49.00 order, order confirmed); a fake saved card refused cleanly; the withdraw lock and its explainer; `GET` invoice PDF 200 (`%PDF-1.4`, xref and stream lengths structurally valid) with 404 to a stranger, 401 signed out; `deno task check` 0 errors, `deno task lint` clean over 1,794 files, `deno task test:unit` 1,706 passed / 4 failed — the four pre-existing `theme-engine.test.ts` failures (#96(a)). **NOT VERIFIED, stated:** a real withdrawal Transfer (no local account has a verified Connect account; the refusal and the debit-first/reversal SQL are proven, the Transfer is not); a real dispute (the won/lost SQL is proven by the rolled-back suite, not by a Stripe event); the v2 thin endpoint with a real event; a 3-D Secure challenge (test card 4242 needs none); a statement PDF (the local seed has no statements); a fresh `supabase db reset` of the whole chain in this pass; a production build. **FLAGGED — needs a human, do NOT silently resolve:** (a) **the brief's "new additive migrations numbered 0400+ or by timestamp" conflicts with root §1** (consolidated, edit-in-place); §1 was followed. (b) **A lost dispute does not claw back escrow already released to a freelancer**, and Stripe's dispute fee is not passed on — both product decisions; `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Escrow #5 now says so as undecided. `under_review`/`refunded`, and Stripe's `warning_*`/`needs_response` states, still have no writer. (c) **The Income Smoother is enrolment only** — nothing buffers releases or pays the monthly figure, so its fee is recorded and never charged. (d) **The Instant Payout fee is still undecided** (#55(c)) and is not charged. (e) **PDFs are rendered per request, never stored** (`pdf_file_id` stays NULL). (f) **The CSP keeps `'unsafe-inline'`** on `script-src`/`style-src` — Fresh 2's inline bootstrap and the theme pre-paint carry no nonce; a nonce-based policy is its own change. (g) **A card is charged in the lines' own currency, so the card option is offered only while the basket is displayed in that currency.** Charging a converted figure the buyer was never shown is not an option; charging the origin total while showing another currency needs a product call. (h) **Checkout shows two currency systems at once:** the checkout's own currency control prices the server session (USD here) while the global display store re-projects every `MoneyView` to the header's choice (AED here), so "Pay US$49.00" sits under "AED 179.80 (~US$49.00)" — pre-existing (#69/#70), made visible by a live card route. (i) **A card checkout's confirmation says "Paid with: Projective wallet"**, because the order IS paid from the wallet the card topped up; naming the card needs the order to link its inbound payment. (j) `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Escrow #5's "an individual client needs no wallet" is read as **no wallet to SET UP** — a card payment passes through a wallet the platform opens automatically; the spec now says so. (k) `projects.fn_enforce_ticket_checkout_desc` names an unqualified type and so depends on its CALLER's `search_path` — latent for any definer with `search_path = ''`. (l) The scheduled run of a payout schedule (`finance.payout_schedules`) is not built — schedules are recorded, withdrawals are manual. (m) The tracked `.env*` files of #125(i) are unchanged — untrack them and keep secrets in `.env.local`. **Env keys used** (all `XXXX-XXXX` in `.env.example`): `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, **new** `STRIPE_THIN_WEBHOOK_SECRET` and `FINANCE_CRON_SECRET`, the optional dev-only `STRIPE_API_BASE`, and `FINANCE_BACKEND_LIVE`. **No new Dev Context axis (§5):** every new gate is server-decided data (KYC/KYB/payout-account state, the card's currency), not a client seam. | `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §Integration Blueprints 1.2 · §Runtime & API Security · §Environment Variable Contract · `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Escrow, Wallets & Finance #5 · `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` §3.5 · `ROUTING.md` · `documentation/database/finance/{Tables,Functions,Policies}.md` · `supabase/migrations/{00000017,00001150,00001200,00001230,00001240,00001830,00002001,00002013,00002510,00002520,00004005,00005001}*` · `packages/types/finance/{payments,wallet,checkout}.ts` (+ tests) · `packages/backend/core/{env,stripe,pdf}.ts` · `packages/backend/services/finance/**` · `apps/web/features/{payments,settings,wallet,checkout}/**` · `apps/web/routes/api/{finance,wallet}/**` · `apps/web/routes/(dashboard)/settings/verification.tsx` · `apps/web/routes/_middleware.ts` · `apps/web/utils/csp.ts` · `.env.example` · Decisions #2 / #54 / #55 / #56 / #68 / #69 / #70 / #122 / #125 |
| 127 | **One avatar rule — uploaded photo → OAuth picture → default picture → initials; `UserAvatar` is the app's one person renderer (2026-09-30). FLAGS a new contradiction for a human (A).** At the product owner's direction ("standardize user profile picture rendering"). **(A) CONTRADICTION — the default picture.** `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` (§C ProgressiveImage/Avatar), `database/files/Functions.md` and `Seed.md` all say a person with no picture renders the *honest absence* — their initials. The brief asks for a stock face instead (`test_images/profile_1.jpg`, "temporarily"). Implemented as asked, but kept reversible and out of the data: projections still answer `null` for "no picture"; only the UI's `UserAvatar` paints `DEFAULT_AVATAR_URL` (`/test_images/profile_1.jpg`, copied to `apps/web/static/test_images/`), with the initials still beneath it. Removing the stand-in is one constant in `packages/types/user/avatar.ts`. **A human should confirm whether the stock face stays, is replaced by a designed default, or reverts to initials.** Entities (team · business · enterprise · group chats) keep initials — a stranger's face is the wrong stand-in for a company. **(B) The ranking lives in one module.** `@projective/types/user` `avatar.ts`: `resolveAvatarUrl({uploaded, oauth})`, `safeOAuthAvatarUrl` (the provider-host allowlist, moved out of `features/auth/core/oauth.ts` so the server applies the same one), `oauthAvatarFromMetadata`, `avatarTierFor`, `DEFAULT_AVATAR_URL`. **(C) The OAuth rung reaches every surface.** Before, the Google picture was never persisted and only the nav's account button (GoTrue `user_metadata`) could show it. `org.get_party_cards` now also returns `oauth_avatar` (raw `avatar_url`/`picture` from `auth.users.raw_user_meta_data`; user-writable, so rendered only after the allowlist), and `party-cards.ts` resolves `avatar`/`avatarLarge`/`avatar_url` through `resolveAvatarUrl` — so every party-card reader (project rosters, messages, contacts, workspaces, `/api/user/me`) shows it. **(D) Size tiers follow the existing tier plan, not the brief's labels.** The brief suggests `lg` for the profile header and settings; `TIER_LONG_EDGE.avatar` puts `lg` at 1024px (the lightbox) and `md` (256px) as the 72–128px disc at 2x, so those surfaces keep `md` — `avatarTierFor` encodes the plan (sm ≤ 48px · md ≤ 128px · lg beyond). **(E) Components.** `Avatar` gains `fallbackImage` (a CSS background layer over the initials, fetched only when shown; covers "no photo" and "photo failed"); `AvatarStack` forwards it; `TreeNav` and `GanttTaskList` draw faces with `Avatar` instead of a raw `<img>` + hand-rolled initials. App: `UserAvatar` / `UserAvatarStack` (`label` required) replace `<Avatar>` at ~50 people-only sites; mixed person/entity sites keep `Avatar` with `personFallbackImage(kind)`; the `/join` passport and the checkout billing chip no longer render raw `<img>`. Three unlabelled avatars (article byline, review author, sponsored owner) now carry the name; the workspace activity feed no longer drops a photo-less actor's avatar. **(F) Deferred, not silently skipped:** (a) the explore / entity-view / catalogue / checkout readers build owner avatars from `avatar_bucket`/`avatar_path` (the full rendition, no tier, no OAuth rung) — fixing them means projecting a media ref from `00003001_views_profiles_business.sql` and the finance KYC functions; (b) the calendar's face renderers (`EventBlock`, `EventPopoverLayer`, the canvas `scene-paint`) are bespoke but receive no data today (nothing sets `attendeeFaces`); (c) sites with no person/entity discriminant keep initials only: the contact popover's seller, the workspace project list's counterparty, file-asset tree nodes; (d) the article comment composer still shows a placeholder "You", not the signed-in user's avatar. | 128 | **Project requests, the hiring handshake, inbox folders, the conversation context panel and safe link previews (2026-10-01). REFINES Decision #116(B) — the intro a client WROTE is now also posted as their own DM message — and #113 — the cooldown refusal names the instant it lifts.** One brief, eight coupled deliverables. **(A) INBOX FOLDERS, folded in place (§1).** `comms.dm_participants.inbox_folder` (`primary` · `requests` · `archived`, CHECK-constrained) on the PARTICIPANT row, because the two people in a DM file it independently; `is_archived` becomes `GENERATED (inbox_folder = 'archived')` so the folder and the old flag cannot disagree (the column is kept, not dropped); indexes `(user_id, inbox_folder)`, `(thread_id, user_id)` and `dm_messages (thread_id, created_at DESC)`. `comms.set_dm_inbox_folder` is the participant's own door (definer, own row only); `comms.fn_promote_thread_on_reply` (`BEFORE INSERT ON dm_messages`) moves the SENDER's own row `requests → primary` — replying is accepting — and touches nobody else's, so a requester's follow-ups can never pull a thread out of the recipient's Requests and a reply into an archived thread leaves it archived. The Zod twins (`InboxFolder`, `folderAfterSend`, `requestRouting`, `folderUnreadCounts`) are pinned to the SQL by `folders.test.ts`; every read projects `ConversationSummary.folder` and `?folder=` partitions the list. **(B) THE HIRING HANDSHAKE.** `comms.send_request_message(recipient, body, project)` posts a request's opening message into the pair's DM — and REFUSES (`42501`) unless an open invitation or a pending application on that project stands between the two, so it is not a cold-message door; only when it OPENS the thread (created, empty, or restored from the recipient's deletion) does it file it — the recipient's row in `requests` unless the two follow each other, the sender's in `primary`; an existing conversation is never re-filed. The projects service calls it after the request is recorded: `hire` posts the client's intro (none written → no thread, so nothing is ever posted that the client did not write — the principle #116(B) protected); the new `projects.apply_to_project` (stage · optional staffing role · cover note; refusals for the owner's own project, an unlisted-or-inactive project, no freelancer profile, a person already on the stage and a second pending application per target) records a `pending` application ("Awaiting approval"), notifies the owner (`application.received`, an existing catalog event) and posts the note into the owner's Requests; `projects.respond_to_project_invitation` is finally reached by an application surface — `POST /api/projects/invites/respond` answers every invitation of one request together; `projects.assign_from_application` was rewritten to take any target (stage, role, open seat), refuse a non-pending application, fill a seat and mark its other pending applicants `rejected`, and notify `application.accepted`. `invite_to_project`'s cooldown refusal now carries `DETAIL = 'reopens_at=<ISO UTC>'`, surfaced by the fat service as `details.reopensAt` on its 422 (and the 10-per-10-minutes limit as `details.retryAt` on its 429) — `ServiceResult` gained an optional `details` record for exactly this. **(C) THE CONTACT FILTER COVERS DMs.** `comms.tg_mask_dm_message_pii` applies `comms.mask_pii` to a DM between two people negotiating or working on a protected project (an open or accepted invitation or application between them, or a protected project both are engaged in — `projects.fn_engaged_projects`), writing `pii_masked` / `pii_categories`; `invite_to_project` masks the intro the same way and the stub path mirrors both with `maskPii` (`packages/types/comms/pii.ts`, pinned to the SQL patterns by `pii.contract.test.ts`). The helper functions are executable by no client role. **(D) THE CONTEXT PANEL.** `projects.get_engagement_context(counterpart)` (definer; only rows the caller is a party to) feeds `MessagingBackendService.context`, whose `context-model.ts` DERIVES the rig server-side: a freelancer gets Accept request / Decline for every pending invitation of the latest request; a client gets "Fund escrow & confirm seat" for a pending applicant (POST accept, then the fund page) or for a confirmed seat awaiting funding. From 1280px the panel stands in the flow, sticky between the header and footer bands (the composer's band is MEASURED, because it grows), collapsible from a Details toggle remembered per viewer; below 1280px it is a right `Drawer`. **(E) THE INBOX LANE.** Folder tabs (`LaneTabs` gained a `dot` + spoken `hint`, and arrow/Home/End roving per the ARIA tabs pattern); rows end in a 48px slot whose compact time (`lastActivityShort`: now/5m/3h/2d/4w/1y) and unread dot hand over to a horizontal `…` on hover/focus/open by opacity alone; coarse pointers keep the menu visible; the row's link stretches over the row; one `ConversationMenu` serves the lane and phone rows, and folder moves are optimistic with rollback (`folder-moves.ts`). "New message" and "Create project" now share the new `LaneCreateButton` (`.proj-lane__create*` deleted). `Ctrl`/`Cmd+B` toggles the global rail except while typing (`shortcuts.ts`); the composer takes focus on arrival on a fine pointer only. **(F) LINKS.** The link scanner's live path is built (`link-guards` · `link-fetch` · `link-reputation` · `link-favicon` · `link-html`): DNS first with every answer checked (rebinding), the checked address pinned and TLS started against the hostname, redirects re-validated (max 2), a 5 s deadline, a stream-enforced 512 KiB cap, Google Safe Browsing (`LINK_SAFETY_API_KEY`), favicons re-hosted — all still behind `FILES_BACKEND_LIVE`. `LinkPreviewBackendService` turns a message's links into cards (internal: profile, project, listing, read under the reader's identity; external: the verdict, metadata only when `safe`); the bubble renders fixed-shape rows so a verdict never moves the feed, and any external link not known safe goes through the new `/exit` interstitial, which never redirects, needs an explicit "I understand" for anything unchecked or suspicious, offers no way on for a blocked link, and never lets a guest trigger a scan (a guest sees a remembered verdict plus the URL's own shape). `exit` is a reserved handle (TS + `org.fn_is_reserved_handle`). **(G) THREE DEFECTS FOUND BY MEASUREMENT, ALL MINE:** (1) the stub context read `Date.now()` while the conversation corpus is frozen at 17 Jul 2026, so every fixture invitation had "expired" and the rig rendered nothing — the stub now dates everything against `CORPUS_NOW`; (2) the warning badge as the filled `--warning` / `--on-warning` pair measured **3.2:1** at 12px in light mode — it is now a solid warning tint with `--on-surface` text, **11.2:1**; (3) adding `exit` to `RESERVED_HANDLES` without its SQL twin failed `workspace.contract.test.ts` until the migration list was edited. **VERIFIED BY EXECUTION:** every new RPC, trigger and grant inside `BEGIN … ROLLBACK` as real roles (routing with and without a mutual follow, no re-filing, restore, reply promotion for the sender only, the folder door's refusals, the cold-message refusal, apply/accept/seat-reject, the cooldown DETAIL, masking in a protected pair's DM and none in an unrelated one); in a driven headless Chromium on `deno task dev:mock`: the tabs and the Requests dot ("Requests, 1 unread" spoken), the row unchanged at 244×62 through the hover swap, a 48px slot and a 24px menu, Move to Primary moving the row and the dot, Ctrl+B toggling and inert in a field and in the composer, the coarse-pointer row (menu beneath the time, no overlap), the composer focused on a fine pointer and not on touch, the panel sticky at 96px and sized to the measured 90px footer, the toggle widening the chat 776 → 1096px, the 384px drawer at 1100px with Escape, Accept request → Accepted and the thread in Primary, safe links direct and flagged ones through `/exit`, every `/exit` state (blocked, suspicious, unchecked, internal path collapse `//evil.com` → `/evil.com`, a `javascript:` refusal at 422) with `noindex` + `no-referrer`, and the no-JavaScript consent round trip. `deno task check` 0 errors, `deno task lint` clean (1,848 files), **1,799 unit tests** with only the four pre-existing `theme-engine` failures (#96(a)). **No new Dev Context axis (§5)**: folders, requests and verdicts are server data, and pointer precision is a device capability the browser's own emulation already simulates. **FLAGGED — surface, do not silently resolve:** (a) the brief says a reply "moves the thread to Primary"; folders are per participant, so it moves the REPLIER's copy — a requester's follow-up never promotes the recipient's; (b) Archived is a third folder tab — the brief named Primary and Requests, but archive is a folder in the data model and the old All/Starred/Archived partition would have contradicted it; (c) the brief asked for an unread BADGE on Requests; the lane renders a dot with the count spoken, because §D.1 and `LaneTabs` forbid printed counts in lane chrome; (d) applications notify through the existing `application.received` event, not `stage.invite` as the brief wrote — the catalog already had the dedicated event, with its own digest window; (e) "Decline / Block" is rendered "Decline": there is no block feature, and the 48-day lockout is the only block that exists (per project) — a person-level block would be new product; (f) "Fund escrow & confirm seat" lands on `/wallet#upcoming` (or the project, for a seat parked at `pending_funding`) because no per-stage funding deep link exists; (g) the brief specified in-flow from 1280px and a drawer below 1024px; 1024–1279px uses the drawer too; (h) Safe Browsing's Lookup API sends the full URL to Google (the Update API would send hash prefixes only) — a privacy trade-off for a human; (i) `projective.io` / `www.projective.io` are ASSUMED to be the product's canonical hosts (`CANONICAL_HOSTS`); (j) the Explore "Apply to project" control is still the pre-existing client stub — the endpoint exists, the stage/role/cover-note picker does not; (k) the local database was NOT migrated: `deno task db:reset` rebuilds with these edited migrations but wipes local data (it holds the owner's own account and projects), so it needs the owner's go-ahead; (l) the phone inbox's row menu was `display: none` below 768px — phones had no row actions at all; it is now visible on coarse pointers; (m) the theme engine's filled `--warning` pair failing 4.5:1 in light mode is pre-existing and touches gate 12 — worth a human look beyond this badge. | 129 | **The middle-nav frame gains a full-height, drag-resizable right PANEL; the canvas becomes a four-corner card between the lane and the panel (2026-10-01). REFINES Decision #128(D) — the conversation context panel leaves the page body for the shell.** **(A) THE SLOT.** `MiddleNav` takes an optional `panel` — a third grid column (`auto minmax(0, 1fr) auto`) spanning all three rows on the inline-end side, the lane's mirror: `sticky` at `--shell-topbar-h`, `max-block-size` as a cap (never a definite height — the lane's measured reason, a spanning item must not bid the `1fr` row up), `stretch`. The footer band stays in column 2, so it runs ONLY between the lane and the panel, and the panel cuts through the footer's row to the viewport bottom. The header band spans columns 2–3 (`2 / -1`) and the panel starts beneath it (`grid-row: 2 / -1`, pinned at top bar + `--shell-midnav-header-h`), so the header's trailing actions hold one position with the panel open or closed (measured: `.chan-header__actions` at x=1083 both ways on a channel, x=979 on a conversation). It docks from 1280px only (the #128(D) breakpoint); below that `.ui-middle-nav__panel` is `display: none` and the surface keeps its right `Drawer`. **(B) RESIZE.** New `MiddleNavPanel` (`@projective/ui/navigation`): `useSplitter` gained `edge: "start"` (handle on the leading edge, drag inverted) and `onKeyDown` (Arrow by `step`, Home/End to the clamp) — the lane does not wire the latter yet. Clamped 280–520px, default 320px (= new token `--shell-panel-w`), persisted under `CONTEXT_PANEL_WIDTH`, hoisted onto `.ui-middle-nav` as `--shell-panel-w`. Body is its own `overscroll-behavior: contain` scroll container; mid-drag it takes no pointer events. Drag-to-close: dragging more than 80px past `min` arms a close (body at `--minor-fade`) and releasing closes the panel through the shell's `closeContextPanel` (preference `0`), so the Details toggle goes inactive; the pre-drag width is kept for the next open (verified: a 130px drag from 360 clamps to 280 and stays open; a further 100px drag closes it — grid `280px 1090px 0px`, toggle without `data-on`, `aria-expanded="false"`; reopening restores 280px; a real mouse drag on a Task channel does the same). **(C) THE CARD.** `.ui-page-canvas::before` subtracts `--shell-panel-cur-w` (0 unless a panel is docked open — keyed on the panel's `data-open` + the breakpoint) and, with the panel docked, gains the `--hairline` end seam and end radii mirroring the start radii (top always; bottom when a footer band is present). **(D) CONSUMERS.** `middleNavPanelFor` in the dashboard layout (fourth parallel slot): `conversationPanelFor` (one-to-one conversation, Chat tab — the route body no longer renders the panel, the `.msg-thread` wrapper and the measured `--msg-ctx-footer-h` sync are gone) and `channelPanelFor` (a project channel's Chat view — bare and `/chat`). The Details state moved from `features/messaging/core/context-panel-state.ts` to `features/shell/core/context-panel-state.ts`: ONE shell preference for every surface (like the lane width); storage key `CONVERSATION_CONTEXT_PANEL` → `CONTEXT_PANEL_DOCKED` (`pj.local.shell.contextPanel`), so a viewer's remembered closed state resets to open once. `ChannelDetailBody` moved out of the `ChannelHeader` island into `components/ChannelDetailBody.tsx` (+ `ChannelDetailsPanel`) so the server slot can render it; the header's Details control toggles the docked panel on the Chat view from 1280px and opens the drawer elsewhere; its "Channel info" menu item only ever opens. The progress meter and member roster had NO CSS at all (pre-existing) — they now have token-only rules in `channel-header.css`, because docking puts them on screen by default. **VERIFIED** on `deno task dev:mock` (preview pane, fabricated session) at 1440×900: `/messages/dm-mara` grid `280px 770px 320px`, header + footer at x 344→1114, panel x 1114→1434 at y 48→900 through the footer row, canvas surface 770px ending at the panel with all four corners at 27.28px and a 1px end seam, panel pinned at y=48 at scrollY 1442; a 100px drag → 421px with header/footer/canvas tracking and the width persisted; End/Home → 520/280, Arrow steps 16px; the Details toggle closes it (`280px 1090px 0px`, end radius 0, preference `0`) and reopens it; `/projects/prj-cujw52gg3p/stg-9768fe95yr` (a Task) docks "Task details" at the persisted 328px; the panel is registered on exactly the two Chat views (not on channel Members/Details, project board, `/messages/{id}/files`, a group conversation, `/messages`, `/wallet`); at 1100px the panel is removed (`280px 750px 0px`) and the control opens the drawer; at 375px no panel, no horizontal overflow. `deno task check` 0 errors; `deno lint` clean on the changed files; **1,799 unit tests** with only the four pre-existing `theme-engine` failures (#96(a)). **No new Dev Context axis (§5)**: docked/width are viewer preferences, not personas, roles, capabilities or lifecycle states. **FLAGGED for a human:** (a) the brief named the Task details view; the details control is shared by every channel kind, so the panel docks on every channel's Chat view (Task · Stage · Channel details) — one control must not behave two ways by kind; (b) the panel docks on the Chat views only — the board, timeline, files and submissions views keep the full canvas width and the drawer; (c) one shared open/closed preference across messages and channels rather than one per surface; (d) the panel slot adds one more parallel `ProjectBackendService.detail` read per channel navigation (each slot resolver already reads it independently — there is no request cache). | `packages/ui/navigation/{components/MiddleNav.tsx,islands/MiddleNavPanel.tsx,hooks/useSplitter.ts,styles/{middle-nav,middle-nav-panel,page-canvas}.css,mod.ts}` · `packages/ui/styles/index.css` · `apps/web/features/shell/{components/UserShell.tsx,core/context-panel-state.ts,islands/MiddleNavPanel.island.tsx}` · `apps/web/features/messaging/{core/conversation-panel-slot.tsx,islands/{ConversationContextPanel,ConversationHeader}.island.tsx,styles/conversation-context.css}` · `apps/web/features/projects/{components/ChannelDetailBody.tsx,core/channel-header-slot.tsx,islands/ChannelHeader.island.tsx,styles/channel-header.css}` · `apps/web/routes/(dashboard)/{_layout.tsx,messages/[conversationId]/index.tsx}` · `apps/web/utils/storage-keys.ts` · `documentation/design-system/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` §C.1/§D.2/§D.4 · Decisions #29 / #31 / #128 |
| 130 | **Group conversation photos; a started conversation lists for its starter at once; the group picture fallback (2026-10-01).** **(A) THE LANE.** The inbox LIST's visibility rule (`messageCount > 0`) now also admits a thread the VIEWER started (`dm_threads.created_by_user_id`), empty — a conversation that stayed hidden until its starter wrote in it read as a create that had failed. Everyone it was started with still sees it only once it holds a message. Live: `fetchConversations` (via a new unfiltered `fetchParticipatingConversations`, which `fetchConversation` also uses — a direct read is gated on membership only, the fix for the picker's "Conversation not found"); fixture: `overlayCreatedConversations` drops its `messageCount` gate (its set is already the viewer's own creations). **(B) GROUP PHOTOS.** `comms.dm_threads.photo_file_id` (FK `files.items`, `ON DELETE SET NULL`, edited in place in `00000016`) is set only by the new definer door `comms.set_group_photo(thread, file)` (`00001300`; grants `00002510`): any undeleted participant; refuses a stranger (42501), a non-group (22023) and any file that is not the CALLER's processed public `avatars` rendition (22023, the `set_profile_avatar` proof); `NULL` clears; the replaced photo is soft-deleted. The rendition is cut server-side from the caller's own library still by the profile's `writeRendition` (now exported, with a display-name override) — `live-group-photo.ts` (`renderGroupPhoto`, shared by the live + fixture paths; `setLiveGroupPhoto` → the door, retiring the rendition on refusal). Reads resolve every group photo in one `files.get_public_media` round trip (`sm` tier) into `ConversationSummary.avatar`. Zod: `GroupPhotoInputSchema` `{sourceAssetId, crop?}`, `SetGroupPhotoSchema` `{photo | null}`, `GroupPhotoSetSchema`; `CreateConversationSchema.photo?` and `CreatedConversation.photoApplied?`. Route `POST /api/messaging/conversations/[id]/photo` → `MessagingBackendService.setGroupPhoto`. The create applies a photo AFTER the group exists, so a photo failure costs only the photo: the conversation opens with `?notice=group-photo-failed`, and the header toasts once and strips it with `fClientNav: false`. UI: the profile's `MediaCropModal` gained `onSave` / `title` / `saveLabel` (and imports its own stylesheet), so the picker's new photo control (beside the group name, `.msg-picker__identity`) and the header's Add / Change / Remove group photo menu items reuse the library + upload + 1:1 crop dialog. The fixture store mints new groups with NO photo (was a stock cover) and keeps an owner-blind per-process photo map (`setStubGroupPhoto` / `withStubPhoto`). **(C) FALLBACK.** `CONVERSATION_PICTURE_FALLBACK_URL = "/test_images/banner_5.jpg"` (TEMPORARY, `@projective/types/messaging`; the file copied into `apps/web/static/test_images/`), applied through one helper `conversationFallbackImage(kind)` at every conversation-picture site (lane row, inbox row, messages rail, header, picker): a GROUP falls back to it; a DM keeps the person default (`DEFAULT_AVATAR_URL`, Decision #127), because a DM's picture is a person. **VERIFIED BY EXECUTION:** the door inside `BEGIN … ROLLBACK` as real roles (set · replace with the previous soft-deleted · another member clears · someone else's file, a DM and a stranger refused · no `anon` EXECUTE). Then, after applying the additive delta to the local DB, end to end through the real services as seeded users: a library upload through the media pipeline → a group created with it (`photoApplied: true`) → theo's lane lists the EMPTY group with its photo, which serves `200 image/webp` → priya's lane does not list it yet, but she opens it directly and sees the photo → priya clears it (`avatar: null`) → priya is refused theo's library asset (404). Every row and storage object it made was removed afterwards (0 left). In the preview (mock mode): a new empty group appears in the lane at once; group pictures compute `banner_5.jpg` in lane + header while a DM computes `profile_1.jpg`; the header menu offers Add group photo and opens the 1:1 "Group photo" dialog; the picker shows the photo control on one row with the name (a class collision with the contact list's `.msg-picker__group` tier wrapper was found by measurement and renamed). `deno task check` clean; lint clean on the changed files; `conversation-store.test.ts` updated to the new rule, plus a photo test. **No new Dev Context axis (§5).** **FLAGGED for a human:** (a) an empty thread lists for its STARTER only — the people it was started with see it once it holds a message, so nobody receives an empty thread from a stranger; (b) ANY member may set, change or remove a group's photo (the common chat-app default); (c) the rendition is stored under the UPLOADER's id in `avatars` and owned by them (`files.owner_kind` has no thread owner) — it stays when they leave the group; (d) the picker's thumbnail previews the whole chosen picture centred, not the exact crop — the server publishes the crop; (e) "no conversation picture" was read as a GROUP with no photo — a DM keeps the person default; (f) the LOCAL database got the additive delta (column, function, grants) applied directly rather than via `deno task db:reset`, which would wipe local data; (g) fixture-created groups no longer carry a stock cover; | 131 | **Messaging — rich text (four marks) stored as plain text + Quill Delta, replies, highlight mode with multi-select and the unfocused-composer keyboard, and the touch gestures with the mobile header action bar (2026-10-03).** **(A) RICH TEXT.** The composer field is a Quill editor on the shared `@projective/ui/editor` loader (now exported: `loadQuill` / `loadedQuill` / `warmQuill`) accepting exactly Bold · Italic · Underline · Strikethrough; highlighting text raises a glass bubble directly above the selection (also Ctrl+B/I/U, Ctrl+Shift+X). A message is stored twice: the plain `body` (search, notifications, previews, the PII mask) and `body_delta jsonb`. The two must agree — `sendDeltaAgrees` refuses a send whose Delta does not spell `text`, and a READ shows the Delta only when it spells the exact body shown (`messageDeltaFor`), so a clamped or masked body renders plain rather than showing words the body lacks. Both PII mask triggers null `body_delta` when they rewrite the body (masking run by run is not attempted: a contact detail can span runs). The feed renders runs as four fixed elements around linkified text — no markup is ever taken from a message. **(B) REPLIES.** `reply_to_id uuid` (self-FK per table) on `comms.project_messages` and `comms.dm_messages`; a guard trigger (INSERT and UPDATE of the reply/room columns) refuses an original in another room, and the services pre-check with the same sentence — 422 "That message can't be replied to here." (`replyToId: not_allowed`), live and mock alike. `ChatMessage.replyTo` carries the quote (author, one-line excerpt, media label, `available`); an unreadable or soft-deleted original renders "Original message unavailable". Reply was a stub before this; the feed hands a reply to its composer on `MESSAGE_REPLY_EVENT`, addressed by channel AND surface (page vs pop-out), and the composer shows a strip until sent or cancelled. A quote jumps to the original, paging up to 8 earlier pages to find it. **(C) LAYOUT.** The message actions are a ghost toolbar in a side rail literally beside the bubble (no fill/border/shadow), with the viewer's own sent time directly beneath; the line wraps the rail under the bubble only where there is no room (the pop-out), so the rail never widens a scroller. **(D) HIGHLIGHT MODE + KEYBOARD.** `useMessageSelection` (shared by `ChatFeed` and `PopoutChat`): right-click (browser menu suppressed), Up/Down with the composer unfocused, or Ctrl/Shift-click enters it — others blur (`filter`, not glass), the chosen lift with a ring + `--elevation-high`, the cursor's message shows a portalled context menu (one message menu on the page at a time, across islands, via `MESSAGE_MENU_OPEN_EVENT`), Shift+hover un-blurs without selecting, a count pill shows "N selected". A plain click never highlights; it sets where the next arrow starts. ←/→ reply to exactly one message (several is refused out loud), Ctrl+C copies the selection (several as a who/when transcript), Ctrl+↑/↓ scroll, Escape leaves; typing with the composer unfocused focuses it and appends at the end. Key ownership is `chat-keyboard.ts`: never a field, a composite widget, a dialog, a Space meant for a focused button, or the other chat surface — judged by the keydown's target. **(E) TOUCH.** Swipe either way to reply (resistance, threshold, cue); long-press selects, raises a glass reaction bubble and enters tap-to-toggle selection; below `--bp-md` a solid action bar replaces the site header at its height and tone. **VERIFIED** in the mock dev server (1280×800 and 375×812): format bubble + Ctrl+Shift+X → send → `{text, delta}` echoed and rendered; reply via toolbar and → → `replyTo` quote; right-click spotlight + menu; ↓ / Shift+↓ / Ctrl-click → "2 selected", Reply disabled "One at a time", Copy 2; Ctrl+C transcript; Shift+hover un-blur; Ctrl+↑ scrolled 288px; type-anywhere appends at the end; pop-out keys stay in the pop-out. Long-press, reaction and swipe were driven with synthetic touch `PointerEvent`s (the pane delivers mouse). A collision with the context panel's `.msg-ctx` block was found by measurement and the menu renamed `.msg-cmenu`; an in-flow rail widening the pop-out was fixed by wrapping. `deno task check` / `lint` clean; `test:unit` 1833 passed, the 4 known theme-engine failures; new `message-rich.test.ts`, `message-selection.test.ts` and mock reply tests. **No new Dev Context axis (§5).** **FLAGGED for a human:** (a) the two glass bubbles extend §B.4.3 at the owner's direction (the request asked for glassmorphic); the context menu, pill and header bar are solid; (b) the migrations are edited in place but were NOT run here — the local database needs `deno task db:reset` (wipes local data) or the additive delta applied by hand; (c) a PII-masked message loses its formatting by design; (d) sends allow 8,000 characters but `ChatMessage.text` caps at 4,000, so a longer message renders plain (pre-existing cap); (e) a reply's media label comes from `has_attachments` / `is_audio`, which nothing keeps current — approximate;
