| 152 | **Wallet Phase 3 — the hero's live aurora, a server-computed balance meter, a keyset-paged and windowed ledger with counterparty context and URL filters, an uncarded analytics grid on the viewer's calendar, card-first Add payment method, and a row-based lane (2026-10-07). REFINES Decisions #122 / #123 / #124; the meter's colours REVERSE #123(C) at the brief's direction.** **(A) Hero aurora.** `HeroAurora` (`apps/web/features/wallet/core/aurora-{engine,field,render,runtime}.ts`) lays an `aria-hidden`, pointer-inert `<canvas class="wlt-hero__canvas">` at `z-index:-1` in the hero's own stacking context — the hero already reaches up behind the glass top bar, so the canvas does too; a `<canvas>` cannot live in the brief's `::before`, so it occupies that underlay layer. 3D simplex noise, lightly domain-warped into long silk folds, at ~30 FPS: WebGL2 at ≤ 480 px on the long edge, else a ~96×54 Canvas2D field CSS upscales. Colours are the five `--wlt-aurora-*` tokens resolved at runtime (any CSS colour syntax, through a 1×1 canvas); the static five-layer CSS gradient stays the resting state and the no-JS picture. Every pixel's luminance is clamped, hue kept, to a ceiling solved from `--wlt-hero-ink` / `--wlt-hero-ink-soft` / `--wlt-glass-fill` (0.1149 for the default inks — 4.7:1 with headroom for 8-bit rounding, so text keeps ≥ 4.5:1). It never mounts under `prefers-reduced-motion` or `:root[data-motion="reduced"]`, Save-Data, `deviceMemory < 4` or `hardwareConcurrency ≤ 4`, or while hidden (it starts once visible), and pauses off-screen; the watchdog tears it down for the page view when the p95 of the first 90 frames exceeds 22 ms or rAF is silent > 1 s, or the context is lost. State on `data-aurora` (`static`·`webgl2`·`canvas2d`·`stopped`) + `data-aurora-reason` + `data-aurora-p95`; Dev Context Switcher axis `walletAurora: auto · static · animated` (`data-dev-wallet-aurora`, all §5 touch points; `animated` bypasses the hardware gates and the watchdog, never reduced motion). **(B) Balance meter.** The overview carries `allocation: AllocationSlice[]` computed SERVER-side by `allocationSlices()` (`@projective/types/finance`) from the same four figures summed into the total: true `shareBp`, drawn `widthBp` (a share under 1.5% is a SLIVER, drawn at 1.5% with the difference taken from the largest slice, so the track still sums to 10000), the legend's largest-remainder percent. Segments are `flex: 0 0 var(--seg)`, never grown, shrunk or transitioned; the seam is a padding inside each later basis. The legend is a `<dl>` (term · figure · share; a sliver gets a pip); a segment's hover and a term's focus open a detail popover (the package Popover's surface tokens on a rich `Tooltip`) with the figure, share, meaning and the stages holding escrow or the releases clearing. Colours follow the brief and the payments-dashboard balance bar: available `--primary` teal, clearing the SAME teal hatched over its soft tint (a second, non-colour channel), escrow `--warning` amber, reserved the aurora violet. **(C) Ledger.** `LedgerLine` gains `counterpartyKind` (user · team · business) + `counterpartyAvatar`, `kind` (`LedgerKind`, one map `LEDGER_KIND_REASONS` between families and `reason` codes), `subject` (the stage · order · service/product · top-up · payout · transfer it is about, with its address), `instrument` (card brand + last four where the platform recorded which card paid: an order's saved card, a recurring deposit's source, a seeded payment-method top-up), `settlement` (cleared · pending · disputed — from the escrow's status, the release window, an open dispute or card chargeback), `receiptHref` (`/api/finance/invoices/[id]/pdf` for the stage's newest issued invoice the reader may read) and `group` (Today · Yesterday · the month, on the viewer's calendar). Paging is KEYSET through `finance.list_ledger` (SECURITY INVOKER; `k:<base64url(created_at|id)>`; index `(wallet_id, created_at DESC, id DESC)`), filtered in the database by direction, families, the ruler's window and a search over what the reader may read. `/wallet/transactions` draws a windowed feed (`useVirtualScroll` over the window's scroll + an `useIntersectionObserver` sentinel 900 px ahead; tail placeholders while a page is in flight) and a URL-synced filter bar — search · All/In/Out · category · the pinned ruler (now on the ledger too; each page omits its OWN default window, `all` for the ledger) — with `useOfflineStall`'s notice and Retry at the tail. The CSV export takes the same filters and is fetched, not linked: only a `text/csv` answer becomes a file; a refusal is JSON shown as an alert. **(D) Analytics.** An uncarded 12-column grid (`.wlt-an__grid`, one level inside the `wlt-an` container, which a container query cannot style itself): KPI strip (In · Out · Net · Locked capital · Projected income — escrow on the seller's stages plus releases still clearing) · cash flow (8) beside category volume by line family (4) · the release schedule (8: releases clearing on known dates, escrow projected a safety window after its stage's `file_due_date`, escrow with no date listed as such) beside top projects by allocation (4: escrow held + clearing, then what moved). Regions separate by `--space-7` above a header, `--space-3` below and ONE hairline per boundary. The cash flow is a diverging `d3-shape` area (in above zero, out below, net as a 2px line drawn beneath both, ONE y-scale) in a stretched viewBox with HTML axis labels, a keyboard / pointer crosshair with a polite readout, and a visually hidden table. Buckets are summed in the database per calendar day / ISO week / month IN THE VIEWER'S ZONE (`finance.ledger_flow`: `date_trunc(grain, created_at AT TIME ZONE tz)`; zone = the browser's `?tz=` → the profile's `timezone` → UTC), so 5Y and All no longer read through 1,000 lines (closes #123(g)); `finance.ledger_first_at` starts All. **(E) Card-first setup** (wallet + checkout): Stripe Elements mounts at once in deferred setup mode (`GET /api/finance/cards/setup` → `{ publishableKey, mode }`; `allowedPaymentMethodTypes: ['card']`, Stripe's current name for the brief's `paymentMethodTypes`), the interstitial is gone, "Save card" sits in the Dialog footer (`useStripeCardSetup` + `StripeElementMount`), and only Save runs `elements.submit()` → the wallet `add_method` action / `POST …/cards/setup` → `confirmSetup({ elements, clientSecret })` → `POST …/cards/confirm`. The server SetupIntent names `allowed_payment_method_types: ['card']` (`CARD_SETUP_PAYMENT_METHOD_TYPES`; mutually exclusive with `automatic_payment_methods`). The new card is selected (checkout) or marked and preselected (wallet `walletAddedCardId`); every `return_url` is the page in view with its query intact, and a 3-D Secure return records the card for the wallet in view and strips only Stripe's params. **(F) Lane.** Actions are icon + label button rows like the page links; the verification gate is a presence pip + one line + optional link (DESIGN_SYSTEM §B.6 rule 5). **DEFECTS FOUND BY MEASUREMENT AND FIXED:** (1) the watchdog sampled through island hydration and tore a healthy canvas down on `/wallet/transactions` (a 94 ms task → p95 33.4) — it now samples only after `load` + one idle period (`AURORA_SETTLE_TIMEOUT_MS`); (2) the analytics grid kept twelve tracks at every width, whose eleven gaps forced every region to 528 px inside a 358 px phone column — one column until the container is ≥ 52rem; (3) a legend popover opened below its term and covered the figure it explains — it opens above; (4) the net line hid the in/out strokes on one-direction days — drawn first; (5) the window's offset table was rebuilt every render (unstable callbacks) — memoised on the lines. **Verified** in headless Chrome (an isolated Playwright run; the Playwright MCP browser was held by another session) against the live local stack after the user-approved `db:reset`, as Kwame (personal seller + North Loop), Maris (escrow), Priya (Helia vault), in dark (the meter also in light), with analytics at 1440 · 900 · 390: zero console errors; keyset paging over the server equals one read in order with no duplicates (7 lines, 4 pages of 2); 160 synthetic lines scroll through ≤ 24 DOM slots with ZERO layout shift while pages arrive (the only shift is the dev server's CSS injection at load, on every wallet page alike); the offline tail shows the one offline notice with no retry loop and resumes on reconnect; filters write `?q=&dir=&kind=` and survive a reload; a CSV downloads (header + 7 lines) and a 503 shows as an alert with no file; analytics has no four-sided box around static content at any width, collapses to one column below the container breakpoint, and draws its three paths; the meter's segments measure exactly their `--seg` (126 + 874 of 1,000 px) with no width transition; the aurora runs (pixels change) on every page, goes `static` for reduced motion (and back), Save-Data, low memory, few cores and a hidden start, obeys the dev axis, and stops (`watchdog-p95`, 33.5) on a janky main thread; Add payment method shows `.pay-element[data-mode="setup"]` at once, Stripe's iframe ready by 6 s, Save disabled until complete, and only `GET /api/finance/cards/setup` before Save. `deno task check` exit 0, lint clean, unit **2,432 passed ⁄ 4 failed** — the four pre-existing `theme-engine.test.ts` failures (#96(a)); new tests: `wallet-ledger.test.ts` (9), `chart-geometry.test.ts` (5), `aurora-engine.test.ts` (33), `stripe-flow.test.ts` (4), `payments-card-setup.test.ts` (2), wallet-home (4 new). **Migrations (edit in place):** `00001210` §14 (`list_ledger`, `ledger_flow`, `ledger_first_at`), `00002510` grants (authenticated; revoked from PUBLIC/anon), `00004005` index `+ id DESC`. No table, lifecycle or business-rule change. **FLAGGED — surface, do not silently resolve:** (a) the brief cites "Phase 3 Architecture Rulings" — no such document or decision exists in the repo; this row is the record. (b) The meter's colour mapping (escrow amber, clearing hatched teal) reverses #123(C)'s (escrow success-green, clearing warning-amber), at the brief's direction. (c) The STATIC hero gradient (which predates this pass) is under 4.5:1 for the soft hero ink in ~4% of its area — the cyan top-left, mostly behind the top bar; the canvas is clamped everywhere, so it reads darker there than the fallback. (d) A seller cannot see what was bought on a sale line — the buyer's `order_lines` are buyer-readable only — so a sale's subject and card stay empty; a definer door would be a product decision. (e) A one-off card top-up records no instrument (`finance.inbound_payments` holds no card reference); showing it needs the settle door to persist the PaymentIntent's card brand/last four — a money-path schema change for the owner. (f) Stripe's docs name the option `allowedPaymentMethodTypes`, not the brief's `paymentMethodTypes`; stripe-mock may predate `allowed_payment_method_types` (2026-07-29.dahlia) — real test mode is fine. (g) Checkout has no 3-D Secure setup-return handler (the webhook still records the card; nothing selects it) — the checkout session's. (h) The sticky hero counts as on-screen while the sheet covers it, so the canvas keeps drawing; the glass over the hero re-blurs each canvas frame — worth a production perf trace. (i) `TransactionPage.total` is now `null` under keyset paging (counting would be a second scan). (j) The release schedule projects escrow from `file_due_date` + the 7-day window — an estimate, labelled "Projected from the stage's due date". | `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` §B.4.3 · §B.6 · `ROUTING.md` · `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §Charts · §1.2 · `documentation/database/finance/Functions.md` §The wallet ledger read · `packages/types/finance/{wallet,payments}.ts` · `packages/backend/services/finance/{wallet-ledger,live-wallet,wallet-scope,WalletBackendService,PaymentBackendService,stripe-rails}.ts` · `supabase/migrations/{00001210,00002510,00004005}` · `apps/web/features/{wallet,payments}/**` · `apps/web/features/checkout/islands/AddPaymentMethodModal.island.tsx` · `apps/web/features/devtools/**` · `apps/web/utils/dev-seam.ts` · `apps/web/routes/api/{wallet/{transactions,export},finance/cards/setup}.ts` · Decisions #54 / #55 / #60 / #96 / #110 / #122 / #123 / #124 / #125 / #126 |
| 153 | **Checkout steps 2–3 stabilised — the Details grid fixed and sized to a 900px window with Enter traversal, the commit CTA's own amber token pair, Stripe's Express Checkout Element in place of the hand-made wallet buttons, cards charged in the listing's own currency with a dual display, and the card checkout's order placed by the settling webhook (2026-10-07). RESOLVES Decision #126 flag (g); AMENDS DESIGN_SYSTEM §A.7 (the hint reserve) and §B.8.4 (the checkout exception) for this flow.** At the product owner's direction (the checkout stabilisation brief). **(A) THE DETAILS GRID (blocker D1).** The markup asked for `span="wide"`/`"narrow"` while `checkout-details.css` only knew `full`/`half`/`third`; an unmatched item auto-places into ONE track of a twelve-track grid, and the address line rendered **44px** wide (measured). A field's span is now a track count (`DetailsFieldSpan = 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 8 | 12`, `data-span="8"`) and every member has a rule: below a 34rem form column one column, 34–40rem every non-full row halves (a triple wraps its last field rather than shrinking a postcode), from 40rem the spans as written. Rows: Delivery `3 + 3 + 6` (first · last · email); personal billing `4 + 4 + 4` (name · email · mobile), `8 + 4` (address · apt), `5 + 3 + 4` (city · postcode · country); company `8 + 4`, `6 + 6` (CRN · VAT, keeping their two disambiguating hints), `8 + 4` (invoice email · department), then the address. **(B) 900px.** Section rhythm `--space-4` (was `--space-6`/`-7`), section purposes ride on their headings as secondary text, the lede is gone (the save-status line says it), the switcher is one row, and the note row renders ONLY when it has text — an owner-directed exception to the `--fld-hint-min-h` reservation, scoped to this step, because the blank 20px band under every control was the largest single reason the step scrolled. Measured at 1440×900: **1685px → 900px** (personal identity, with and without the State row); a COMPANY identity measures **1022px** (13 fields). **(C) COUNTRY AND STATE.** Country is a filtered `Select` over the full ISO 3166-1 list (`@projective/types/finance` `COUNTRIES`, 249 entries, `countryCodeOf` normalises a code, a name or an alias and never guesses); a record saved as free text ("Brazil") SHOWS as `BR` and is never rewritten until the buyer picks. State / province renders only where `countryNeedsRegion` says addresses carry one, or when a value is saved; it stays optional, as the SSOT says. **(D) KEYBOARD.** The fields are `<form id="cko-details" noValidate>` and the rail's Continue is `type="submit" form="cko-details"`. Enter walks the form (`checkout/core/details-keys.ts`, capture phase): a text field moves on, a FILLED picker is stepped over, an empty required picker opens, an open list keeps Enter, and Enter on the last field submits — the one deliberate difference from `/join`'s enter-chain, where a select is only ever a destination, because this form ENDS on a picker. `InputText` gains a typed `enterKeyHint` (§C.1). Busy is `readOnly` + `aria-busy`, never `disabled`, so focus is not dropped to `<body>` mid-save. Verified by a Playwright run-through: Enter from First name through State reached submit, saved once (mocked) and navigated to `/checkout/payment`; Shift+Tab walks back. **(E) THE COMMIT TOKEN.** `--checkout-commit-bg` `hsl(38 92% 50%)` / `--checkout-commit-fg` `#1a1a1a` / `--checkout-commit-pole` (`checkout-shared.css`), mode-invariant like `--primary`, **8.09:1** (AAA), hover/press blend toward a LIGHT pole so label contrast rises with pressure — pinned by `checkout-commit-tokens.test.ts`, which reads the sheet. `.ui-button.cko-commit` replaces `severity="warning"` as the stand-in on Continue, Buy Now, the confirm-and-pay, Save details and the Add-card actions, so "commit" and "needs attention" are no longer one colour (§B.8.3). The pill stays the §B.8.4 checkout exception. **(F) FOCUS RING — A CONTRADICTION, FLAGGED.** The brief asks for `--focus-ring` with a 2px offset; `apps/web/styles/global.css` carries the owner's "NO VISIBLE FOCUS INDICATOR" directive (commit `152a27a`), which neutralises the `--focus-ring-*` tokens with `!important`. Implemented as a scoped exception for the two focus steps only (`.ckod`, `.cko-pstep`): private `--cko-ring*` tokens derived as the engine derives `--focus-ring` (`--secondary`, 1px / 2px under high contrast, 2px offset), one outline mechanism for controls and composite fields. Measured: `solid 1px rgb(74, 99, 102)`, offset 2px on a field, a chip and the commit. One region; deleting it restores the directive. **(G) EXPRESS CHECKOUT.** `PaymentProvider` becomes `card · wallet · express · invoice` — the per-vendor `google_pay`/`apple_pay`/`paypal` routes (refused in every environment, drawn from a quarantined `--xpay-*` hex block) are gone with their buttons, and the browser capability sniffing (`googlePay`/`applePay`/`paypal` query flags, `deviceWallets()`) with them; the Zod enum has no database mirror (`finance.card_brand`'s `apple_pay`/`google_pay` are wallet WRAPPERS, a different axis). `express` is offered to an individual and refused to an entity (an individual instrument, rule 2 of `availableProviders`). `ExpressCheckoutElement` mounts Stripe's Element in deferred-intent mode (`mode: "payment"`, `amount` = the session's `charge`, `currency`); on `confirm`: `elements.submit()` → `create({ provider: "express" })` (the server re-prices and opens the PaymentIntent for the same charge) → `confirmPayment({ redirect: "if_required" })`. A redirect-based wallet returns with `?pay=<payment>&attempt=<key>` and the page RESUMES that payment (params stripped first, both validated). Buy Now (the panel) shows `express` refused with a pointer to the full checkout. The three additive Stripe types live in the wallet session's deferred layer (`stripe-flow.ts` `amount`; `stripe-js.ts` `create("expressCheckout")`, `confirmPayment.clientSecret`), by agreement. **(H) CURRENCY — #126(g) RESOLVED.** The card no longer requires the basket to be DISPLAYED in its own currency. The session gains `charge: MoneyView | null` — the order total in the listings' own currency, computed by `chargeFor()`: the SAME basket re-read with `display` = that currency and totalled by the same `toTotals`, so the figure shown and the figure charged come from one function (`create()` charges exactly it). The rail prints `AED 840.27 AED (~US$229.00 USD charged)` with `1 USD = 3.6693 AED · locked <stamp>` and "your bank converts the charge at its own rate" — the platform's one estimate grammar (`MoneyView`'s `£78.50 (~€90.00 EUR)`), with an accessible sentence naming the charge as exact; `Amount pinned` prints the charge in its own currency instead of re-projecting it. The banner says the order is priced and TAKEN in the listing currency (true of a wallet order too). Measured for `noor`: AED 840.27 total, US$229.00 charged, 229 × 3.6693 = 840.27. **(I) ADD CARD.** The new card is selected (the wallet session's modal now calls `selectPaymentMethod`; the screen does too), and "Add new card" stays under a REFUSED card route — the refusal ("add a business card") is the one a card answers. `finance.record_saved_card` now records a card saved to a team / business / enterprise as that entity's business card: it can only arrive through `card_owner_for` (manage_billing on the entity), and without it every card a business added at checkout was refused by the business-card rule. **(J) THE WEBHOOK PLACES THE ORDER.** `finance.inbound_payments` gains `checkout jsonb` (`{basket_id, item_ids, currency, units, promo_code, order_key}`), `order_id` and `order_error`, with two CHECKs (checkout only on a top-up; an order outcome only with a checkout) — edited in place in `00000017`. New user door `finance.attach_checkout_order(uuid, jsonb)` (payer only, unpaid only, `fn_can_manage_basket` as the caller, write-once: same → no-op, different → `PX409`), called after `begin_card_payment` and BEFORE any intent exists (a saved card confirms at creation, so the webhook must already know). `settle_card_payment` then places the order AS THE PAYER (`request.jwt.claim.sub` = `created_by`, in a subtransaction — the escrow-lock pattern) under the attempt's OWN key; the browser threads that key (`chargeKey`, the redirect `attempt` param) instead of minting a fresh one, so whichever arrives second REPLAYS. A refusal rolls back only the order: the money stays in the wallet, `order_error` says why, and the buyer is notified either way. The app degrades on a database without the door (`PGRST202` → the browser still places the order). **VERIFIED BY EXECUTION:** in rolled-back transactions against the live local DB — attach (stored, normalised), same-again no-op, different `PX409`, stranger `P0002`; settle → order `PJ-…` for US$80.10 placed as `theo`, both lines consumed, claim restored; the browser's placement under the same key replayed (one order); a redelivered event replayed (one order); a moved price → payment `succeeded`, wallet +US$80.10, no order, `order_error` = the catalogue's refusal, lines still open; a business's saved card `is_business_card = true`, a person's `false`. In the browser (Playwright, writes blocked): the Details measurements above; the payment step with no USD banner and no verbose express copy, card + express offered to `noor`, the dual line, the Element mounting (`ready`, Stripe.js `dahlia`), Buy Now `rgb(245,159,10)` on `rgb(26,26,26)` in light and dark, Add card present. `deno task check` exit 0; `deno task lint` clean over 2,111 files; unit 2,432 passed / 4 failed — the four pre-existing `theme-engine.test.ts` failures (#96(a)) — with new tests for the countries list, Enter traversal, the commit contrast, the provider offer and the door/CHECK contract. **NOT VERIFIED, stated:** an actual wallet payment through the Element — over `http://localhost` Stripe shows no Apple Pay or Google Pay (HTTPS required; Apple Pay also needs the payment-method domain registered) and Link / Amazon Pay are not activated on the sandbox; a PayPal redirect round trip; the webhook path end to end with `stripe listen` (the SQL is proven by the probes, not by a Stripe event); a production build. **FLAGGED — needs a human, do NOT silently resolve:** (a) **the GBP seed default is NOT done** — "set default account/seed processing to GBP so UK accounts settle penny-for-penny" needs a product call on scope (every seeded product and wallet is USD; re-pricing UK sellers' listings and UK buyers' wallets means a seed regeneration and a reset of the shared DB). (b) **The focus-ring exception (F)** contradicts the owner's global directive — keep or drop. (c) **This change's four migration edits went live through ANOTHER session's user-approved `db:reset`** (2026-10-07) before this session had asked for approval; they applied cleanly and were then verified against the live DB, but the user approved that reset, not these edits. (d) `appearanceFromTokens` (payments) passes `calc(8px * 1)` as a radius, which Stripe rejects with a warning — pre-existing, the wallet session's helper. (e) `create()` refuses any processing contribution on every route while the session can still display one — pre-existing, untouched here. (f) The business-card rule is now ownership-based (a card on the entity's own account); Stripe offers no reliable commercial-card flag. (g) The company form measures 1022px at 1440×900 — over the 900px target by its own extra rows. | `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` §A.7 · §B.8.4 · §C.1 · §D.6 · `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Escrow, Wallets & Finance · `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §Integration Blueprints 1.2 · `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` §3.5 · `documentation/database/finance/{Tables,Functions}.md` · `supabase/migrations/{00000017,00001230,00001240,00002510}*` · `packages/types/finance/{checkout,payments,countries}.ts` (+ tests) · `packages/backend/services/finance/{CheckoutBackendService,PaymentBackendService,live-payments,basket-query}.ts` · `packages/ui/fields/islands/InputText.tsx` · `apps/web/features/checkout/**` · `apps/web/features/payments/core/{stripe-js,stripe-flow}.ts` · Decisions #70 / #125 / #126 / #69 |
| 154 | **The proposal allowance gets its surfaces — the account popover's meter, the lane's cost disclosure, an apply modal with a pre-flight gate, team applications, withdrawal with a refund, and "Your proposals" on `/projects` (2026-10-07). EXTENDS Decision #58; REFINES #149 (the popover) and #128 (the application handshake).** **(A) THE GATE HONOURS THE FAIL-OPEN SWITCH (product owner, 2026-10-06).** The brief asked `POST /api/projects/apply` to 422 and the modal to disable submit when headroom is out; #58 shipped `proposal_allowance_enforced = false` (meter, never block, flip only after tuning). The owner chose one switch for everything: the METERED reasons (`weekly_exhausted`, `buffer_exhausted`) refuse only while the param is on — in the app gate and the insert trigger alike — and the ELIGIBILITY reasons (`team_too_small`, `missing_permission`) always refuse. While the switch is off the meter, the countdown and the notice still render, and the notice says the application will still go through. The rule lives once, in `@projective/types/finance` (`resolveProposalAllowance` · `refusesApplication` · `allowanceNotice`), and the server's 422 message and the modal's notice are the same sentence. **(B) ONE CALLER-SCOPED DOOR.** `finance.get_proposal_allowance(p_team_id)` (authenticated) derives its subject from `auth.uid()` — the person, or a team the caller is an active member of — so `finance.fn_current_allowance`, which takes any subject id, stays service-role only. It returns the period, the drip dials, the plan and its Pro upgrade target (from the catalogue, never a hard-coded 150), the earned rung, the switch, and for a team the active head-count and `can_bind_seat`. A pre-flight refusal happens before the insert, so the metering trigger never writes its `entitlement.denied`; `finance.record_proposal_denial` writes it for the caller's own subject (the `analytics` schema is not exposed to PostgREST). The read is `GET /api/user/allowance[?team= | ?as=self]` through `WalletBackendService.getProposalAllowanceStatus` (logic in `finance/proposal-allowance.ts`, with an in-memory twin for `dev:mock`). **(C) TWO METERING BUGS FIXED (both latent since #58).** (1) THE DRIP CLOCK: a spend from a FULL buffer did not restart `buffer_refreshed_at`, so a buffer that had sat full for ten hours refilled the instant after its first spend and no countdown could ever appear; and a refill set the clock to `now()`, so every late read silently stretched the drip. `fn_consume_allowance` now restarts the clock when a spend takes the buffer below its cap, and `fn_current_allowance` advances it by the whole windows it paid out (verified: a read 17 h in pays one window and leaves 7 h on the clock). (2) THE PERSON'S RUNG: a person is metered as `user` but earns Standing as `freelancer`, and no recompute writes a `user` row, so `fn_subject_standing_level('user', …)` pinned every individual at rung 1 and the weekly bonus (+10…+40) never applied. `user` now reads the freelancer rung — which also corrects any other standing-scaled entitlement resolved for a person. **(D) TEAM APPLICATIONS.** `projects.apply_to_project(…, p_team_id)` applies on a team's behalf: the caller must hold `bind_seat` (`org.is_team_lead`), the team needs two active members, and it may not apply to its own project; the duplicate and already-on-stage checks are applicant-aware, so a lead may hold one pending application as themselves and one for the team. Metered against the team's pool by the existing trigger. **(E) WITHDRAWAL.** `projects.withdraw_application` (the filer, or a `bind_seat` member of the applying team; pending only) flips the status, and the existing trigger refunds one WEEKLY unit. The anti-burst BUFFER token is deliberately NOT returned: an apply → withdraw loop that refilled it would let a sender notify the same client without limit. So the action reads "Withdraw proposal (refunds 1 proposal)", not the brief's "1 token". `projects.list_my_applications(p_project)` backs the sent list (`GET /api/projects/applications/mine`); `POST /api/projects/applications/withdraw` is the write. **(F) THE SURFACES.** The popover's meter sits under the completion block for anyone who sends proposals (a freelancer, or a team context): `Proposals: W/G weekly · B/C ready` (the buffer as units of CAP — `12/12` — where the brief's example showed the drip, `3/3`), a refill line, a portal tooltip naming the drip and the earned bonus, and `Accelerate volume with Pro →` at ≤ 5 weekly or an empty buffer on a free plan. The shared `ProjectCtaRig` carries `1 proposal token · N ready` under Apply in the lane and the mobile bar alike (§D.7.4). `ProjectApplyModal` (stage · role · apply as · cover note) replaces the optimistic `projectApplied` stub, which is now derived from the viewer's real pending proposals; pressing "Applied" opens it to manage or withdraw them. `/projects` lists "Your proposals". All four read one client store (`proposals/core/allowance-state.ts`), so an application or a withdrawal moves every meter on the page with no reload, and every countdown runs on the SERVER's clock (the skew is carried with the status). **(G) DEV AXIS.** `proposalAllowance` (auto · healthy · low · paced · paced (unenforced) · week spent · team < 2) substitutes the STATUS client-side; the server gate is untouched. **FLAGS for a human:** (a) `/settings/billing` holds saved cards only — no plan can be bought there yet, so the upgrade link lands where Pro is not on sale. (b) The seed note on `team_free` says a free team "draws on its members' allowances rather than a dedicated pool", but the trigger meters a dedicated 50/week team pool, as the brief describes — the seed note or the trigger must change. (c) The modal defaults to "Yourself" even in a team context. (d) The enforced 422 path is pinned by unit tests of the rule and the gate's code path, not end to end — flipping `proposal_allowance_enforced` on the shared database is the owner's call. (e) `projects.apply_to_seat` (posted seats) has no UI and no pre-flight; only `apply_to_project` is wired. |
| 155 | **Gamified onboarding without points — a server-computed setup score, verification crests per authority, earned trust signals, and in-place milestone celebrations (2026-10-07). REVERSES Decision #149(A) (the TypeScript completeness rule) at the product owner's direction; AMENDS DESIGN_SYSTEM §B.7.8 (three crests replace `verified`) and §C.1.** At the product owner's direction (the gamified onboarding brief), with four rulings taken before building. **(A) THE SCORE MOVES TO SQL (ruling 1).** `org.fn_compute_profile_setup_progress(p_user_id)` (00001010 §2, caller-own or service role, `42501` otherwise) is the ONE completeness rule: account 20 · confirmed email 10 · skills/interests 10 (the 40 a finished sign-up reads) · uploaded photo 20 · headline + story 20 (`org.fn_bio_has_text`, the SQL twin of `bioText`) · published hours with a band 20, and one `next_suggested_action` (an unconfirmed email first, then a seller's earning gate — identity, payout — then the steps by weight, a buyer's `become_partner` last). `calculateProfileCompleteness`, `GO_LIVE_MIN_SKILLS` and the facts schema are deleted with their test; `AccountSetup` now carries `{ handle, seller, progress, verificationStamp, hours, standing }` (`ProfileSetupProgressSchema`, `@projective/types/org/onboarding.ts`, strict enums for keys and actions). `UserBackendService.getSetupProgress(actor)` (`services/user/setup-progress.ts`) is the read — it takes the `ReadActor`, not a bare id, because the function runs in the caller's JWT (the #150 precedent) — and `setup()` composes it with `org.get_profile_view`. **(B) STAMPS (rulings 2 + 3).** `org.entity_standing` gains `verification_stamp` (CHECKed) and `active_adornments` (CHECKed against the eighteen slugs), edited in place in `00000011` — not the brief's appended `20261008000000` ALTER file, which root `CLAUDE.md` §1 forbids. A person's stamp (`org.fn_verification_stamp`) is `vault_verified` only with KYC AND a verified payout account (the brief said `payout_ready` alone; the No-Forever-Escrow pair is stricter), else `id_verified` on KYC; `trg_freelancer_profiles_verification_stamp` (00001830) and every Standing recompute keep it current. `corporate_verified` lives on BUSINESS PROFILES ONLY: `org.get_profile_view` derives it from `kyb_status` (and an enterprise's `verification_level = 'verified'`) — a business has no Standing row and a person never inherits it. Three registry glyphs replace `verified` everywhere (`seal-check` · `shield-check` · `keystone-check`, one knockout check each), rendered by the new `VerificationStampBadge` (`@projective/ui/display`) in `--primary` · `--tertiary` · `--on-surface-variant` — the brief's `#007680` IS `--primary`; "Royal Navy" and "Deep Slate" were mapped to the nearest role tokens, never hex — so silhouette, not hue, carries the claim. Ten call sites migrated; `TIER_META` (its only reader was the crest) is deleted. **(C) TRUST SIGNALS.** `org.fn_refresh_active_adornments` (00001030 §9, called at the end of `org.fn_recompute_standing`) derives six dimensions × three tiers from the delivery record — median first response (`search.talent_signals`, evidenced by a `fast_response` streak ≥ 5), claim → accepted-submission turnaround, revision-free stages, dispute streaks and disputed escrows, returning-client share and long Pipelines, review averages with volume floors, CREATE share, team splits, the Architect designation — and stores the highest tier per dimension; `rankAdornments` (pure, tested) ranks by tier then client weight and `mapProfileView` caps at three. They render on the profile hero through the new `TrustAdornments` (Meta register, middot siblings, a portal tooltip per claim, no fill). A `user` subject earns none (buyers are not gamified). **(D) CELEBRATION (ruling 4).** No modal. `MilestoneCelebration.island.tsx` (mounted by `UserShell`) compares the popover's published setup (`accountSetupSnapshot`) with the device's last mark (`LocalKeys.SEEN_MILESTONES`), and on a raise plays a one-shot `transform: scale()` stamp on `--spring-snappy` (new CSS token, `cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1)`; none under either reduced-motion channel) on the header ring and the popover crest, plus ONE transient toast that states only what the milestone unlocks — "Payout account verified — Clients can now hire you onto escrow-backed stages, and released escrow is paid out to you." The brief's "+15% Search Boost" is NOT shipped: no such rule exists and discovery weight is Standing's alone. A first sighting records the baseline and celebrates nothing. **(E) THE POPOVER.** The setup block is extracted to `AccountSetupNudge`: below 100% the score, the bar and the six-step checklist; at 100% the verification status replaces the percentage; beneath either, ONE filled call to action for the suggested step (an `<a>` in `ui-button` classes), with a reason line for a seller's earning gate. The identity block carries the crest. **(F) AI ROADMAP (documentation only).** `comms.project_messages.actor_kind` (`human` · `agent_assisted` · `autonomous_agent`) with a nullable `actor_plugin_id`, agents as `integrations.plugins` rows with `runtime = 'headless'` — written into `database/comms/Tables.md` and `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §3.5; nothing migrated or built. **(G) Dev Context Switcher:** `profileSetup` now substitutes the RPC's ANSWER (40 · 80 · 100); new `verificationStamp` (auto · none · identity · payout · corporate — raising it fires the celebration) and `trustAdornments` (auto · none · earned · top tier) axes, `data-dev-verification-stamp` / `data-dev-trust-adornments`, set and deleted in `reflect()`. **Verified:** `deno task check` clean; `deno lint` clean (2,125 files); unit suite 2,457 passed / 4 failed — the four pre-existing `theme-engine.test.ts` failures (#96(a)); 18 new tests (trust signals, onboarding parsing, milestones). The six changed migration files parse under the real Postgres parser (`libpg_query` 17, PL/pgSQL bodies included). **NOT verified against a database** — the Docker engine returned 500s for the whole session, so the rolled-back probe (setup scores for seeded personas, the 42501 refusal, trigger transitions, CHECK refusals, `get_profile_view` stamps) has not run and `deno task db:reset` is required before the live path works. **Flagged (surface, do not silently resolve):** (a) The go-live milestone (`GO_LIVE_MIN_SKILLS = 3`, "to go live") is retired with the TS rule; the SQL rule counts any skill or interest, and payout leaves the percentage for the next-action CTA — still no server-side go-live gate (#149(c)). (b) The brief says "20 tiered thresholds" but enumerates 18 (6 × 3); 18 are built. (c) Volume floors (≥ 5 / ≥ 10 reviews, ≥ 5 delivered tickets, ≥ 5 engagements, ≥ 5 stages in a CREATE category) and the `fast_response` streak-as-evidence are additions the brief did not specify. (d) "Persistent elevation" on the identity container is not built — a raised block inside the elevated popover breaks the anti-card gate (§3 rule 6). (e) The toast is informational: `Toast` has no action slot, so the "actionable" part lives in the popover's CTA. (f) The brief's `ui-separator` class became BEM `ui-trust__sep` (the layout `Separator` is a hairline; one name for two shapes). (g) Explore cards, listings, reviews and articles know only `verified` + kind, so they draw identity or corporate via `stampForVerifiedOwner` and never the payout crest; `org.profiles_index` and the explore readers need the stamp to show it. (h) The entity view's TS-derived `sellerBadges` ("Top rated", "Fast replies") now overlap the SQL trust signals — two derivations of similar claims; unify. (i) Certifications "Verified by Projective" render `seal-check`, the identity crest's silhouette — confirm a platform-checked credential and an identity check may share it. (j) `ProfileView.verified` / `tier` / `verifications` (the L1–L3 ladder) remain in the SSOT with no renderer; retire or re-home. (k) Adornments render on the profile hero only; listings and cards do not show them yet. | 156 | **The Settings hub, the account lifecycle and localisation — a searchable card hub with attention dot badges and a profile completeness tracker, the `@handle` change policy, freelancer → client and account deletion as scheduled, cancellable erasures, connected sign-in accounts, a plan placeholder, the date format and `auto` direction by script, contextual away replies the database now sends, and one `CurrencySelect` with circular flags (2026-10-08). EXTENDS #151; AMENDS DESIGN_SYSTEM §C.1 (Select `panelWidth` · `panelClass` · option `description`; new `Flag`, `CurrencyLabel`, `CurrencySelect`); keeps #149(C) (no hand-set presence).** At the product owner's direction (the `/settings` refactor brief). **(A) UI primitives.** `Select` gains `panelWidth` (`trigger` · `auto` · a CSS length; the positioner measures the rendered box) and `panelClass`, and `Option.description` draws a second meta-register line the filter searches. `Flag` (`@projective/ui/display`) draws circular flags inline from `flag-art.tsx` — 30 territories, the flags' own colours as content like a brand mark, a letter fallback — because regional-indicator emoji render as two letters on Windows. `CurrencyLabel` (flag · `[symbol] [code]` over the issuing country) and `CurrencySelect` (a `Select` drawing it in the trigger and every option) replace every currency picker: Settings → Language & region, the checkout band, the project budget, the paid-call fee, and the wallet hero's list rows (its trigger stays a one-line glass pill by the tool contract). `DisplayCurrencyOption.flag` (emoji) becomes `country` + `countryName`; the checkout dial codes draw `Flag` too. **(B) The hub.** `/settings` = search (`searchSettings`; `/` focuses it) · Needs your attention · the profile tracker (`org.fn_compute_profile_setup_progress` score + ONE call to action, routed by the extracted, DOM-free `features/shell/core/setup-actions.ts`) · every section as an interactive card (the whole card opens it; at rest its settings as middot meta text, while searching the matching settings as links). Attention FACTS gain `profile` · `billing` (`cardExpiryCounts`, plan state) · `security` (sign-in methods, whether another provider can be added, scheduled erasures) and items for profile steps, expired / expiring cards, a failed plan payment, a lone sign-in method and a scheduled erasure. The lane, the hub's cards and the modal's tree draw one dot badge per section (shape per tone: dot · ring · square; worded for AT); the modal reads `GET /api/settings/attention` through `useAttention`. **(C) Handles.** `org.change_username` (00001060): two changes inside three days, then a 90-day lock after the window's second change (`org.fn_handle_change_policy`, `HANDLE_POLICY`); `org.handle_changes` is the clock and the 90-day hold that `org.fn_handle_taken` now honours (only the previous owner may take a held handle back). Settings states the policy before and after each change and renews the session. **(D) Freelancer → client and account deletion.** `org.deletion_requests` (`scheduled → cancelled | completed`, PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT §3.5): `org.schedule_freelancer_removal` (typed `CONFIRM`; drops the persona at once, pauses the person's own listings and services, erasure in 90 days) and `org.schedule_account_deletion` (hides the profile, pauses listings, erasure in 30 days), both refused while `org.fn_account_blockers` reports held escrow, live work, wallet money (and, for the account, running projects or owned workspaces); `org.cancel_deletion_request` / becoming a freelancer again restores exactly what was paused; `org.fn_purge_due_deletions` (service role via `security.purge_due_account_deletions`, `POST /api/user/cron/erasures`, bearer `ACCOUNT_CRON_SECRET`) erases what is due and DEFERS a request whose blockers came back. The UI is a high-friction `RemovalDialog` (what goes, what stays, what blocks it, the confirm off until typed). **(E) Connected accounts and plan.** `IdentitiesBackendService`: GoTrue identities, providers offered read live from GoTrue `/settings`, link = PKCE `linkIdentity` (only the verifier leaves, as a cookie; `/api/auth/callback` returns failures to Settings), unlink refused for the email identity and the last method. `PlanBackendService` + `PlanBlock`: the held plan or the free default, renewal and the individual offers — read-only, said so. **(F) Localisation.** `org.user_preferences.date_format` (`dmy` · `mdy` · `ymd`; `NULL` follows the locale) rides the token (`active_context.dateFormat`, `UserContext.dateFormat`); `formatDateValue` / `localeDateOrder` (`@projective/types/org`). `auto` direction resolves by script (`localeDirection`), server-side (`a11yRootAttributes(overlays, locale)`) and on the device (`applyDirection(dir, locale)`). The language list gains flags, English names and he · ur · hi · zh · ko · es-MX · pl · sv · tr. **(G) Away replies.** `comms.auto_responses` gains `status` (`away` · `busy` · `out_of_hours` · `holiday`, all DERIVED — notifications paused, a calendar event or blackout, outside published hours, the rule's dates) and `project_invitation` triggers, an optional window (a holiday needs both ends), and `service`/`product` rules with no id answering every inquiry of their kind. `comms.tg_dm_auto_reply` SENDS them (most specific first, once per rule per thread per day, marked `dm_messages.auto_response_id`, never answering itself or accepting a request; best effort). The editor becomes one grouped `When` picker plus dates. **(H) Dev Context Switcher:** new `accountLifecycle` axis (`auto · handle-window · handle-locked · removal-scheduled · deletion-scheduled`, `data-dev-account-lifecycle`) substituting the handle policy / lifecycle answers; `settingsAttention` `clear`/`all` cover the new facts. **Verified:** `deno task check` clean; `deno lint` clean; unit tests (new: date format + direction, attention rules, card expiry, refusal mapping, bearer check, account simulation, a11y root, auto-reply write planning) — the four pre-existing `theme-engine.test.ts` failures only (#96(a)); the whole SQL delta in a rolled-back transaction against the local database as real users (58 probes: the handle window, lock length 90 days, hold and own-hold, refusals, RLS, grants, the removal and deletion lifecycles with cancel and re-enable, pause/restore, the sweep's erasure, the token claim, the CHECKs, and the auto-reply engine's keyword, dedupe, away, service-inquiry and request-folder cases), then applied additively to the local database; live service reads and refusals with a minted JWT; Playwright on the local stack (hub, search, account, removal dialog, language and currency panels, billing, the auto-reply picker, the modal's marks, the wallet list, a 390px Arabic `auto` layout) with zero console errors. **Flagged (surface, do not silently resolve):** (a) **CONFLICT — the brief's "permanent, unrecoverable deletion" vs PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT §5.4 "nothing is hard-deleted" and PRODUCT_SPEC's 7-year retention.** Built as an anonymising erasure that keeps tombstone rows and money/evidence records; it does hard-delete GoTrue `auth.identities` and `auth.sessions`, sets the DOB to `1900-01-01`, and does NOT yet erase portfolios, showcase items, certifications, education, experience or stored files. A human decides the erasure's scope. (b) The account window (30 days) was chosen here; the brief named only the freelancer's 90. (c) The handle rule read literally: one change followed by three quiet days never locks; and links to an old `/@handle` 404 after a change (held, not redirected). (d) Sign-up still allows usernames the namespace rule refuses (`.` · `_` · capitals; `provision_user_profile` checks neither reservation nor the shared namespace). (e) Linking needs `[auth] enable_manual_linking = true` (set in `config.toml`; a local `supabase stop && supabase start` and the production GoTrue setting are required); only Google is configured; Amazon has no GoTrue provider and always shows "Not available yet". (f) The brief's Away/Busy read as chosen statuses; they are derived instead, keeping #149(C). (g) Product rules match nothing (an inquiry carries no product) and named-service rules match only messages carrying `service_id`, which no writer sets; the old editor's free-text service/product names never saved (a 422) and are gone. (h) No scheduler calls the erasure sweep yet, and nothing tells the person before an erasure (no email transport, #57(f)). (i) The date format is honoured in Settings only (birth date, previews); ~118 ad-hoc date sites and `DatePicker`'s `mm/dd/yy` default do not read it yet. (j) Plans are read-only — no plan checkout, no entitlement comparison. (k) During the 30-day grace a person can still buy as a client; the sweep defers rather than forces. (l) The schema delta was applied to the shared local database for verification; `deno task db:reset` reproduces it from the migrations. | `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Settings + Sitemap · `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` §3.5 (Scheduled removal · Handle change window) · `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §The Settings engine · §The Notification Engine · §Layout direction · Environment Variable Contract · `ROUTING.md` (settings root, §Settings engine API) · `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` §C.1 · `database/org/{Tables,Functions,Policies}.md` · `database/comms/{Tables,Functions}.md` · `database/security/Functions.md` · `supabase/migrations/{00000011,00000016,00001020,00001060,00001300,00001700,00001840,00002001,00002010,00002510,00002520,00004001}` · `supabase/config.toml` · `packages/types/{org/{account-lifecycle,date-format,preferences}.ts,auth/{identities,user-context}.ts,settings/sections.ts,messaging/settings.ts,finance/{fx,plans}.ts}` · `packages/backend/{core/bearer.ts,services/{user/{AccountLifecycleBackendService,account-lifecycle-refusals,UserBackendService}.ts,auth/IdentitiesBackendService.ts,finance/PlanBackendService.ts,messaging/live-settings{,-write}.ts}}` · `packages/ui/{display/{components/{Flag,CurrencyLabel}.tsx,core/flag-art.tsx},fields/{islands/{Select,CurrencySelect}.tsx,types/mod.ts}}` · `apps/web/features/settings/**` · `apps/web/features/shell/core/setup-actions.ts` · `apps/web/features/messaging/components/AutoResponseEditor.tsx` · `apps/web/routes/api/{settings/attention.ts,user/{handle,lifecycle}.ts,user/identities/**,user/cron/erasures.ts,auth/callback.ts}` · `apps/web/{utils/a11y-context.ts,routes/_app.tsx}` · devtools `accountLifecycle` axis |
| 157 | **The global Accent Amber pair and the Button Interaction Matrix — one amber seed becomes `--accent` / `--on-accent` for every terminal financial / conversion commit, buttons are tiered by what they commit the person to, and translucent primary washes leave active rails, middle-nav lists and tags (2026-10-09). AMENDS Decision #153 (the checkout's private commit pair is retired for the global one; #70's pill unchanged) and DESIGN_SYSTEM §B.8.1 / §B.8.2 (the matrix; the one sanctioned two-fill rig becomes hue-ranked amber + monochrome) / §D.7.7 (the profile rig: Hire amber, Add to project monochrome); EXTENDS §A.1 / §A.2 / §B.4.2 / §B.11 / §B.12 / Part E 8.** At the product owner's direction (the "Accent Amber" directive). This entry is PHASE 1, the contracts; the engine, the `accent` severity, the checkout and profile migrations follow and land against them (PHASES 2–4, below). **(A) THE TOKEN.** `ACCENT_SEED = #D98216` → `CorePalette.of(seed).a1` (`A`); the ink comes from the BRAND seed's `n1` (`N`), the palette `--surface` / `--on-surface` resolve from. Light `--accent = A.tone(62)` = `#d88115` (≈ the seed) on `--on-accent = N.tone(10)` = `#191c1d` (= light `--on-surface`) — **5.77:1**; dark, the tonal lift, `A.tone(66)` = `#e58b22` (≈ `#E58A18`) on `N.tone(4)` (= dark `--surface`) — **7.39:1**, meeting the ≥ 7:1 dark rule. **(B) HIGH CONTRAST AND FIXED POLARITY.** The pair widens by `HC_DELTA` (12): the INK always moves away from the fill, `max(0, t − 12)` in BOTH modes (light 10 → 0, dark 4 → 0); the FILL moves up 12 in dark only (66 → 78 = `#ffb061`) and HOLDS at 62 in light, because raising it loses page visibility and lowering it closes the gap with the ink — light HC **7.07:1**, dark HC **11.63:1**; the pair never narrows. Polarity is fixed (light fill, dark ink, every mode) like `--primary` and unlike the mode-adaptive roles, so `fg()` / `on()` are bypassed: written `fg(62)` / `on(10)` the light branch converges to 50 over 22 under the overlay — the brand-pair trap of §A.1.1. **(C) THE PRESSURE POLE.** `--btn-shade` (`--on-surface`) is the accent's own ink side in light mode, so hover and press blend toward a mode-invariant near-white (`N.tone(100)`, what `--on-scrim` already resolves to) and label contrast RISES with pressure (§B.12.4) — #153's `--checkout-commit-pole` mechanism, promoted to the token layer. **(D) THE MATRIX (§B.8.1).** 1 · Terminal financial / conversion — `severity="accent"`, solid `--accent` / `--on-accent`, pill: every checkout commit (`.cko-commit` today), the profile hero's Hire, Fund Escrow. 2 · Primary structural commitment — monochrome inverted (`--btn-accent: var(--on-surface)`; `--btn-on: var(--surface)`, the §D.7.7 idiom): Add to project, primary creation triggers. 3 · Brand primary — solid `--primary` / mode-invariant `--on-primary`, reserved for major navigation active states and core workflow commits (Save photo, Attach Selected). 4 · Neutral outlined — border `var(--hairline-strong)`, `--on-surface` ink, MANDATORY for utility actions (the asset picker's Upload, profile media upload). 5 · Ghost / bare — secondary toolbar tools, escape hatches. **(E) TWO FILLS.** Legal in one decision region ONLY when ranked by hue — amber terminal against monochrome structural, the profile rig; two fills of the same colour remain a finding, and the §B.8 gate now also refuses a fill from outside its tier. **(F) SURFACE POLICY (§B.4.2, §B.11.6).** Semi-transparent primary washes (`rgba(primary, .15)`, `color-mix(… var(--primary) …, transparent)`) are PROHIBITED on active navigation rails, middle-nav lists and tags; the active rail is `background: var(--surface-2); color: var(--on-surface)` with an optional 2px inline-start indicator reserved `transparent` at rest. **(G) THE GATE (§B.12.2, Part E 8).** `--accent` / `--on-accent` joins the machine-checked set on the full terms — ≥ 4.5:1 in all four mode/contrast states, ≥ 7:1 dark, never narrowing — pinned in `packages/ui/system/core/theme-engine.test.ts`, which also takes over from `checkout-commit-tokens.test.ts`. **(H) THE KNOB (§A.2).** The Colors knob has two seeds, `dsConfig.seed` and `dsConfig.accentSeed`, both live from the dev theme controls through `applyConfig` → `:root` `style.setProperty`. **FLAGGED — needs a human, do NOT silently resolve:** (a) **Commit and warning are one hue again — in BOTH modes.** Light `--accent` `#d88115` against light `--warning` `#ca8100` (engine warning seed `#F19C13`, `a1` tone 60) measures **1.06:1**; dark `--accent` `#e58b22` against dark `--warning` `#ed990d` (tone 70) measures **1.14:1**. "Commit" and "needs attention" read alike in either theme — the exact §B.8.3 failure #153 fixed. Recommend retuning the warning ramp or the accent hue; a human picks which. (b) **Under the house 3:1 fill floor on every light ground, and lower under pressure.** The light fill rests at **2.97 / 2.84 / 2.69 / 2.55 / 2.42:1** on `--surface` / `--bg` / `--surface-1` / `--surface-2` / `--surface-3`; hover and press toward the light pole (8% / 12%) take it to **2.71 / 2.59:1** on `--surface` and **2.21 / 2.11:1** on `--surface-3`, the worst case; high contrast changes none of these (the light fill holds at 62, surfaces never widen). The label carries identification, so WCAG 1.4.11 is not failed, and #153's amber had the same property, but it deviates from the §B.12 gate's 3:1 clause and waives §B.12.4's "do not shave a floor" rule; recorded in §A.1.2, §B.12.4 and the §B.12 gate as a deviation, not a precedent. The alternative for a human: give the light branch the brand's treatment (`--btn-mix-hover` / `--btn-mix-active: 100%` plus the border-channel hover) and keep the light pole for dark only. (c) **Warning seed drift (pre-existing).** DESIGN_SYSTEM §A.1 and PRODUCT_SPEC §Visual Identity list the warning seed as `#D98216`; the engine uses `#F19C13`. The accent now owns `#D98216`; the warning rows in BOTH documents carry a drift marker, the values are not changed. (d) **`--text-main` is not a token.** The directive named it; ink is `--on-surface` (see the `checkout-stepper.css` note), and the docs map one to the other. (e) **`--hairline-strong` is a border shorthand** (`1px solid var(--hairline-strong-color)`, `packages/ui/styles/index.css`), used as-is for tier 4 — recorded so it is not re-mixed or misused as a colour. (f) **The entity-view lane is not reconciled.** §D.7.2 item 3's money verbs (Buy now · Book session · Reserve seat · Fund Stage 1) are tier 1 by the matrix, but an amber primary beside the lane's teal Add to basket would be two fills that are not the amber + monochrome pair, and the matrix reserves teal for navigation and workflow commits, which a basket is neither. The lane is held as shipped (monochrome primary, teal basket), scoped to that lane; the options are an amber primary with the basket demoted (to tier 2 or 4), or a scoped lane exception. (g) **"Accent" vocabulary collides.** Prose elsewhere in DESIGN_SYSTEM uses "the accent" loosely for the brand teal (§B.6, §B.9, §B.11.4), and the components' internal pair is named `--btn-accent` / `--x-accent`; neither is the new global `--accent`. The PROSE is corrected — §B.6, §B.9.8 and §B.11.4 now say "the brand (`--primary`)", and §B.8.3 calls the component block "the severity pair block" — so no sentence reads as licensing amber outside tier 1; the CODE names (`--btn-accent`, `--x-accent`, `.ui-lane-iconbtn--accent`) are left unrenamed. (h) **The tier-1 pill leaves the checkout.** §B.8.4 reserves `rounded` for chip-like and floating controls with one checkout exception (#70); the matrix makes the amber terminal commit a pill wherever it renders (Hire, Fund Escrow). Recorded in §B.8.4 as the directive's; not extended to any other tier. (i) **Contracts ahead of code, and shipped code the new gates now fail.** _Closed by Phases 2–4 except as noted at the end of this flag: every "not built yet" item and the TreeNav / LaneIconButton washes below are built (I); what remains open is `LaneCreateButton` and the held `NavItem`._ Not built yet at Phase 1: `severity="accent"` on a Button-only `ButtonSeverity` type (the shared `Severity` union in `packages/ui/fields/types/mod.ts`, read by `Badge` / `Tag` / `Alert` / `Message` / `Toast`, does NOT gain `accent` — §B.8.3), the neutral tier-4 `outlined` override (`--btn-accent: var(--on-surface)`, border `var(--hairline-strong)`; today `outlined` draws border and ink in `--btn-accent`, teal by default, `button.css` `.ui-button--outlined`), `dsConfig.accentSeed`, the four pinned ratios and the retirement of `--checkout-commit-*`; until Phase 2 lands the docs describe a target, and the four pre-existing `theme-engine.test.ts` failures (#96(a)) still stand. Shipped and now non-compliant, to migrate in Phase 2 or be ruled on: the translucent `--primary` washes on `TreeNav`'s active row (`packages/ui/navigation/styles/tree-nav.css` `.ui-treenav__row--active`) and on `LaneIconButton`'s engaged state and `--accent` modifier hover (`lane.css` `.ui-lane-iconbtn[data-on="true"]`, `.ui-lane-iconbtn--accent:hover`) — §B.4.2 / §B.11.6; `NavItem`'s solid `--primary` active (`nav.css`), held pending (k); and `LaneCreateButton`'s teal pill (`lane.css` `.ui-lane-create`), which by the matrix's "primary creation triggers" is tier 2 monochrome for Create project — whether New message, a messaging verb, follows it is open here. (j) **Tier-1 scope.** _Phase 4 adds a surface under the "act that starts the flow" reading: the basket summary's Proceed to Checkout is amber; and `/wallet`'s Top up / Withdraw now sit in the hero's glass action-pill row (`.wlt-pill`), neither filled, the amber on each dialog's commit — the two-amber case below does not arise while they stay unfilled (Part F F.3)._ The directive puts "every checkout commit that today uses `.cko-commit`" in tier 1 (Continue, Save details and the Add-card actions move no money) and Hire (starts a hire, moves none); PRODUCT_SPEC's first draft said "only (Pay, Hire, Fund Escrow)". The docs now state ONE test — a commit inside a financial or conversion flow, or the act that starts one (§B.8.1) — and use it in §B.8.1, §B.8.4, the §B.8 gate and PRODUCT_SPEC. Open for a human: whether only the money-moving commit (Pay / Place Order, Fund Escrow) is amber and the flow's other commits drop to tier 2. Same flag: the `/wallet` footer's Top up and Withdraw both move money, so the matrix makes them two tier-1 amber fills — the same-colour case the cap forbids (Part F F.3). (k) **Which active navigation is teal and which is surface-2.** The directive reserves tier-3 teal for "major navigation active states" AND names the active rail `--surface-2` / `--on-surface`, without saying which surfaces are which; shipped, `NavItem` (global `ShellSidebar` rail, `BottomNav`, and the Project Details lane's `ProjectNavSection`, §D.4) is a SOLID `--primary` / `--on-primary` active. Docs reading until ruled (§B.4.2): the wash is banned everywhere it was named; solid-teal `NavItem` actives are held as shipped as tier 3; washed rows (TreeNav, LaneIconButton) move to `--surface-2`. The ruling: do the middle-nav `NavItem` rows (ProjectNavSection) join the surface-2 set, and is the global rail tier 3 or surface-2? Since Phase 3 it also asks for an order between hover and active: on `LaneIconButton` and the indicator rails hover equals the active `--surface-2`, and on `TreeNav` and the asset picker the `--tint-subtle` hover is about as heavy as active, so only the indicator or the glyph fill tells them apart — an active at `--surface-3` (as `.msg-conv`) or a lighter hover. (l) **What `outlined` means now.** Tier 4 mandates a neutral `outlined` for utility actions, while the §B.8.1 weight table keeps `outlined` for "a real alternative / a secondary create". Docs reading until ruled: utility actions take the neutral form, other `outlined` buttons keep their severity's ink; the alternative is that `outlined` turns neutral for every severity. (m) **Dark `--primary` engine drift (pre-existing, outside the accent).** `theme-engine.ts` emits dark `--primary` as `core.a1.tone(55)` (`#00929e`), not `tone(45)` (`#007680`) as §A.1.1, §A.2 step 3, §B.12.2 and the code's own comments say, so the brand pair is not byte-identical across modes in code. Logged, not changed; Phase 2 must not copy the "like `--primary`" invariance claim into the accent tests without checking it. (n) **Guardrail texts lag.** `guardrails.md` §3 items 6 / 7 / 12 and `pr-checklist.md` now carry the accent, wash-ban and matrix clauses, and `guardrails.md` gains item 13 (the matrix); root `CLAUDE.md` §3 (items 6, 7, 12) and its "all 12 UI gates" reference line are guardrail files a human edits — they do not yet mention `--accent` / `--on-accent`, the light pole, the matrix or the wash ban. (o) **`ConfirmDialog` / `ConfirmPopup` cannot host an amber commit.** Both type `acceptSeverity` as the shared `Severity`, so _Approve and release_ (`ApproveStageDialog`, the release-funds commit) cannot be tier 1, and its opener _Approve Stage_ (`SubmissionActionBar`) is held beside it for consistency; the fix is a `packages/ui` change — widen to `ButtonSeverity` and add an accept-`rounded` hook. (p) **`danger` vs tier 1 on an irreversible financial commit.** §B.8.3 says an irreversible action always takes `danger`, while the matrix makes irreversible money movements (Approve and release, Withdraw, Transfer) tier-1 amber, and the wallet's ship amber; proposed reading: `danger` is for destructive or loss actions, an intended irreversible financial commit is tier 1. (q) **The schedule's _Book this slot_** (`EventModal` on `/view/[entity]/schedule`), the body counterpart of the lane's held _Book session_, is left non-amber pending (f). | `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` §A.1 (seeds table, drift note) · §A.1.1 (engine-drift note) · §A.1.2 · §A.2 (pipeline, code sample, Colors knob) · §B.4.2 · §B.6 · §B.8 (B.8.1 matrix + tier-1 test + tier-4 mechanism, B.8.2 two-fill note, B.8.3 Button-only `accent`, B.8.4 Decision #153 note and tier-1 pill, gate) · §B.9.8 · §B.11.4 · §B.11.6 + gate · §B.12.2 · §B.12.4 + gate · §C.1 (Button, Tag, LaneCreateButton) · §D.4 · §D.7.2 item 3 · §D.7.7 · Part E 8 · Part F F.2 / F.3 · `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Visual Identity · `guardrails.md` §3 · `pr-checklist.md` · Decisions #70 / #96 / #153 · `checkout-commit-tier.test.ts` · `checkout/core/card-step.ts` · `devtools/core/dev-theme.ts` |
| 158 | **The File Picker becomes the one picture picker — profile photo, showcase slots and group photos are chosen and framed in its media mode; the avatar is clamped to its circle; the sign-in picture enters through the pipeline; the photo is editable from Preview (2026-10-09).** AMENDS Decision #119 (B) (Preview is no longer edit-free: the owner's photo carries an edit overlay) and the `crop.ts` rule recorded with #119 (D) that a rotated avatar stays FILLED (the avatar is now clamped to its visible circle), both at the product owner's direction. **(A) ONE PICKER.** `MediaCropModal` and its "Your images" tray are deleted; `AssetPicker` gains a `media` mode (`MediaPickConfig`, `files/core/media/media-pick.ts`) whose rail is Upload from device · Projective Library (the `/files` listing of `files.items`) · Sign-in account, and whose `.apk-main` switches to the Crop & Adjust stage (`files/components/media-crop/`: `CropStage`, `CropControls`, `RotationRuler`, `MediaCropWorkspace`, `SignInPictureSource`) once a source is chosen, dropped or imported; the nav and inspect panes leave so the stage takes the pane. State lives in `useMediaPick` (`files/hooks/`); hosts mount `MediaCropPicker` and receive `{ sourceAssetId, crop, alt, previewUrl }` through `onSave` — `MediaSection` (`/[handle]/edit`), `OwnerAvatarEdit` (`/[handle]` hero), `ContactPicker` and `ConversationHeader` (group photos). A library row is a crop candidate only when the server cut would accept it (`candidateFromAsset`: own storage, `uploaded`, still or allowed video, known dimensions), refused at the click with a sentence. Uploads in media mode use the strict `/api/media/upload-*` door, not `/api/files/*`. `MediaService` moved to `files/core/`. **(B) THE STAGE.** Fixed size in every state (`stageRatioFor(target)`, a container-sized square for the avatar, 1.476 for 16:10); empty, it is a dashed drop zone whose crop outline fades in on drag-over; the controls bar always renders and is disabled (`--fld-disabled-mix`) until a still loads; rule-of-thirds guides inside the frame at 0.15 opacity, 0.7 while the picture or the ruler is dragged (both reduced-motion channels drop the transition); the rotation control is an infinite ruler (`files/core/media/rotation-ruler.ts`: offset `(θ mod 360) × 6px`, ticks generated for the visible window, labels wrapped into (-180, 180], drag · wheel · Arrow ±1° · Shift/PageUp ±15° · Home, one `role="slider"`); Reset returns zoom 1.0× and 0°. **(C) THE CIRCLE.** `crop.ts` gains `CropShape` and `RENDITION_SHAPE` (`avatar: "circle"`, `showcase: "rect"`), threaded through `fitHeight` · `cropBox` · `centreBounds` · `clampCrop` · `stageTransform` · `dragCentre` · `sourcePointFor`, client and server alike: a circle's fit is the short side at every angle and its legal centres are the picture shrunk by its radius, so the circle reaches every edge at any rotation and zoom (before, the hidden square's corners stopped it short, and the minimum scale climbed √2 at 45°). The stored square may overhang the picture; `renderCrop` fills those corners from the nearest edge pixel (the 0° shortcut now falls through to the resampling pass when the box leaves the picture, instead of clipping and stretching). Note: for a circle the spec's s_min(θ) is constant by construction. **(D) THE SIGN-IN PICTURE.** `GET/POST /api/media/oauth-avatar-sync`: `UserBackendService.oauthAvatar` reads provider + allowlisted URL from the VERIFIED identity (`isOAuthProvider`, `oauthProviderLabel`, `OAuthAvatarSourceSchema` in `@projective/types/user`); `services/media/oauth-avatar.ts` fetches it with `guardedFetch` (8 MiB cap), re-checks the final host after redirects, sniffs it, then declares a quarantine upload under the caller's session, writes the bytes to the declared object as the service role (`quarantinePath`, now shared with `initLibraryUpload`) and completes it through `completeLibraryUpload` — the browser never touches the provider's bytes, so no canvas is tainted. **(E) PROPAGATION.** `applyProfileMedia` (`profile/core/media-apply.ts`) applies the choice and broadcasts `pj:avatar-changed` for a person's own photo; the hero disc updates in place and `ProfileStickyHeader` now listens too. Ownership for the overlay is `profile.viewer.isOwner`. **(F) FIXED ON THE WAY.** the picker footer's container query targeted its own container and never fired (a phone footer squeezed the tray to one letter per line) — the footer now always wraps; `activeSources` was a `useComputed` that froze the first render's config (a showcase slot inherited the avatar's sign-in source; the attach mode's `scope` had the same latent bug). **(G) VERIFIED.** `deno task check` 0 errors; `deno lint` clean on every touched file; `deno task test:unit` 2,526 passed with only the four pre-existing `theme-engine.test.ts` failures (#96(a)); new tests: circle fit/bounds/invariant swept 0°–360° in 7.5° steps × 4 aspect ratios × 4 zooms × 4 centres × 32 rim points, the edge-reach and drag round-trip, the overhanging-corner cut, the ruler model, the candidate rules, the OAuth acceptance rule. In a browser against the live local stack, signed in as `chloewinters`: hover overlay; three sources; empty stage 397×397 = loaded stage; disabled controls; drag-over outline; thirds 0.15 → 0.7; ruler 3×Shift+Right = 45, wraps to 180; at 45° / 2.14× pushed to the edge the circle met the image boundary (measured 171.8px against a 170px radius); Reset; focus held in the dialog across 25 Tabs; `role="dialog"` `aria-modal` labelled; Save & apply cut the rendition, fired `pj:avatar-changed` and replaced the hero disc; Escape closes; Library pick → stage; the OAuth sync (a temporary allowlisted `avatar_url` on the persona, restored afterwards along with the avatar) imported to `personal/users/{id}/library/` and opened in the stage; showcase slot 1.476 with its description field; 390px wide: 358×358 stage, no horizontal overflow. **No new Dev Context axis (§5):** ownership is server-decided data. **FLAGGED — surface, do not silently resolve:** (a) the spec's crop descriptor `{ x, y, width, height, rotate, scale }` was NOT adopted — the existing normalized `CropState { zoom, rotation, cx, cy }` travels, because it is the one model both sides clamp with; (b) the spec's `users/[user_id]/avatar.webp` path was NOT adopted — renditions stay `avatars/{owner}/avatar/{rendition}/full.webp` + `sm`/`md`/`lg` (immutable, RLS anchor first); (c) "update `org.users_public` and the `get_party_cards` cache" — there is no cache: the sync only adds a library asset, and Save runs `org.set_profile_avatar`, which sets `avatar_file_id` that every party read resolves live; (d) `pj:avatar-changed` is dispatched by the client host after the apply (the server cannot reach a window) and carries the `md` URL while `avatar-sync.ts` says "small tier" — pre-existing mismatch; (e) a TEAM profile's new photo is not broadcast (the event is keyed by user id) — the hero updates, the sticky band does not until reload; (f) `/api/media/library` has no client caller any more (the picker browses `/api/files/list`) and is left in place; (g) in a team acting context the Library source lists the team's library, and a teammate's file is refused at Save (404 from `readLibrarySource`); (h) `--outline-variant`, used by `.apk-drop`, is defined nowhere — pre-existing, untouched; (i) `AssetPicker`'s render block carries pre-existing `deno fmt` debt (640 lines at HEAD) and was not reformatted; (j) a server-side OAuth ingest whose storage write fails leaves a `pending_upload` row, as an abandoned browser upload does; (k) the crop parts are feature components (`files/components/media-crop/`), not `@projective/ui`, so §C.1 is unchanged — `RotationRuler` is a promotion candidate; (l) the stage and ruler show no focus ring, following the product-wide removal (152a27a). | root `CLAUDE.md` §1/§2/§3/§5/§8 · `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §The Public Profile + §Sitemap · `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §The Public Profile (media pipeline 5–6) · `ROUTING.md` (owner views, media API) · `database/files/Storage.md` §The upload pipeline · `packages/types/files/{crop,variants}.ts` + `crop.test.ts` · `packages/types/user/{avatar,mod}.ts` · `packages/backend/services/media/{image-codec,image-jobs,library,oauth-avatar,MediaBackendService}.ts` + tests · `packages/backend/services/user/UserBackendService.ts` · `apps/web/routes/api/media/oauth-avatar-sync.ts` · `apps/web/features/files/{islands/AssetPicker.island.tsx,components/media-crop/*,core/MediaService.ts,core/media/{media-pick,rotation-ruler}.ts,hooks/use-media-pick.ts,styles/{media-crop,asset-picker}.css}` · `apps/web/features/profile/{components/{OwnerAvatarEdit,edit/MediaSection}.tsx,core/media-apply.ts,islands/{ProfileHero,ProfileStickyHeader}.island.tsx,styles/{profile,profile-hero}.css}` · `apps/web/features/messaging/{components/ContactPicker.tsx,islands/ConversationHeader.island.tsx}` · Decisions #119 / #127 / #130 / #138 |
| 159 | **The crop stage's precision controls and two-finger gestures — icon-only ghost back / Reset / steppers, scrubbable readouts, a Slider that drags from the track and answers Ctrl + wheel on hover, and pan · pinch · twist at once on touch (2026-10-09).** EXTENDS Decision #158 at the product owner's direction; RE-INTRODUCES a focus indicator on four controls against the product-wide removal of commit `152a27a` (flag (a)). **(A) BUTTONS.** `.apk-main__bar`'s "Choose another" becomes an icon-only, rounded, `text`-variant `Button` (`arrow-left`, `aria-label="Back to file selection"`, a portalled `Tooltip`, `.ui-hit` floor; 32px). Reset becomes the same shape with the `refresh` glyph and now also re-centres the picture (`cx = cy = 0`). The sources toggle `.apk__act--toggle` takes the `panel-right` glyph and the `.ui-shell-sidebar__toggle` treatment (base radius, `--tint-soft` hover, muted when pressed). In media mode `.apk__why` moves into the footer's left (`.apk-tray`) slot. **(B) ZOOM.** `−` / `+` `HoldStepButton`s flank the slider (`useHoldRepeat`: one step per press, a ramp after 380ms that ends on release or at a bound, Enter/Space step once); a step is 0.1× (0.01× with Ctrl/Cmd held at press). The zoom and rotation readouts are `ScrubValue`s: `useScrub` (Pointer Lock where granted) feeding `scrubDelta` from `fields/core/number-field.ts`, `ew-resize` cursor, Ctrl/Cmd read live per sample for the fine step (0.001× / 0.1°). **(C) SLIDER (`@projective/ui`).** A press on the bare track jumps the nearest handle AND keeps dragging it (pointer capture on the track) until release; Ctrl/Cmd + wheel now steps on hover (the first handle when nothing is focused), the plain wheel stays focus-gated — `NumberInput`'s two-gate rule (§C.1 roster updated). **(D) GESTURES.** `CropStage` keeps a pointer map; one pointer pans, two pointers drive `pinch-gesture.ts` (`pinchFrame`: centroid, Euclidean spread, `atan2` bearing; `pinchDelta`: centroid travel, spread ratio, short-way-round angle change) and one combined `onChange({ zoom, rotation, cx, cy })`, which the owner clamps through `clampCrop` with the circle shape — so a pinch can never expose the void; lifting a finger re-baselines on the remaining one. `touch-action: none` on the stage. **(E) VERIFIED.** `deno task check` 0 errors; lint clean; `deno task test:unit` 2,530 passed, only the four `theme-engine.test.ts` failures (#96(a)); `pinch-gesture.test.ts` (frame, combined delta, seam wrap, degenerate spread). Browser, live stack, owner `chloewinters`: back button 32×32 with its glyph, its tooltip, first in the tab order after Close, glow drawn under keyboard focus; `panel-right` toggle; why-text on the left; `+` click 1.00→1.10×, Enter 1.20×, Ctrl+click 1.21×, a 1s hold ramped to 2.30× and stopped on release, `−` disabled at 1×; track press at 20% → 2.00×, dragged to 60% without release → 4.00×; plain wheel over the unfocused slider ignored, Ctrl+wheel stepped; readout scrub +60px 4.01→4.17×, −30px 0→−6°; Reset → 1.00× 0° and disables; 300 rapid synthetic pan moves ≈ 5ms each, 0 long tasks; a real CDP two-finger touch (390px, `hasTouch`) pinched out ×2.8 while twisting 36° and panning → 2.80× / 36°, and a hard pinch-in clamped at 1.00× with the circle inside the picture. **FLAGGED — surface, do not silently resolve:** (a) **focus rings** — commit `152a27a` removed them product-wide; the spec asked for a `:focus-visible` ring on these controls, so `.apk-main__back` and `.pf-media__iconbtn` (steppers, Reset) are added to `global.css`'s `--fld-focus-glow` exemption block; the stage, ruler, slider handle and the other picker controls keep the no-ring convention, so the modal is now mixed — choose one rule; (b) the stepper step is 0.1×, not the slider's 0.01 step the spec's "1 step" would literally mean (a 0.01 ramp crawls); (c) a pinch zooms and twists about the crop centre, not about the fingers' midpoint — pan follows the centroid; (d) the icon registry has no undo/reset glyph, so Reset uses `refresh`; (e) the `.apk__act--toggle` restyle applies to the attach picker on every host too, while the `.apk__why` move is media-mode only; (f) on a RANGE slider an unfocused Ctrl + wheel steps the first handle; (g) the scrubbable readouts are `aria-hidden` — their keyboard equivalents are the slider and the ruler beside them; (h) the stage still re-renders per pointer sample (no rAF batching); measured cost above; (i) the stage's hint line does not mention two-finger gestures on touch. | root `CLAUDE.md` §3/§8 · `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` §C.1 (Slider) · `apps/web/styles/global.css` (focus exemption) · `packages/ui/fields/islands/Slider.tsx` · `apps/web/features/files/{components/media-crop/{CropStage,CropControls,HoldStepButton,ScrubValue}.tsx,core/media/pinch-gesture.ts + test,islands/AssetPicker.island.tsx,styles/{media-crop,asset-picker}.css}` · Decisions #138 / #158 |
| 160 | **The showcase picker chooses many, frames each, and writes nothing until Save & Apply — the selection ceiling, Browse → Continue → Crop, the carousel, staged uploads, and the drawer's corner controls (2026-10-09).** EXTENDS Decisions #158 / #159 at the product owner's direction. **(A) SELECTION.** `MediaPickConfig` gains `max` and `positions`; `MediaSection` opens an EMPTY slot with every empty slot (the pressed one first), so the ceiling is the empty-slot count, and a filled slot's Replace with that one slot; the photo and group targets stay at one, where a second click REPLACES the choice instead of refusing. A chosen card or tray chip toggles off (`toggleSelection`); at the ceiling the other cards are blocked with "You have N empty slots — remove one to choose another." Selected cards hide `.fh-asset__flags` so the tick never sits on the privacy mark. Slot 1 still takes only a still: `assignSlots` moves the first still into it and refuses a selection with none. **(B) TWO STEPS — FOR MULTI-SELECT ONLY.** A single-pick target (the photo, a group photo, a showcase Replace) has no Continue: choosing a library card, dropping a file or using the sign-in picture goes straight to Crop, and Back there lets the choice go so the next click picks again (owner direction, same day). A multi-select Browse never advances by itself — not even when a drop fills the ceiling. Browse's footer is Cancel + **Continue (n)** (disabled with its reason on the LEFT, in the tray slot, at zero); Continue enters Crop, whose footer is **Back** + **Save & apply**. Each item keeps its own crop and description (`PickItem`; the working crop is committed on every switch). The tray marks the active item (`data-active`, `aria-current`) and a chip click frames that item; muted ghost chevrons flank the stage and cycle circularly (`cycleIndex`); the bar reads "name · 2 of 3". **(C) STAGED UNTIL SAVE.** A dropped or chosen device file becomes a `staged` candidate — an object URL plus its decoded size — and the sign-in picture a `signin` candidate previewed from the provider URL; neither touches the server until Save & Apply, which uploads each staged file through `/api/media/upload-*`, syncs the sign-in picture, carries the crop over to the size the server decoded (`rescale`), then hands the host library ids.
