| 184 | **The shared media lightbox becomes a full-viewport embed — fit, zoom and pan measured against the viewport, a bottom thumbnail carousel, and one modal for `/view/[id]`, the `/[handle]` showcase and multi-image chat messages (2026-10-13).** At the product owner's direction. **(A) STAGE.** The old panel scaled the `<img>` by a fixed `scale(2.2)` inside an `overflow: hidden` `.vw-lightbox__frame` in a 1100 × 880 box, so "zoom" cropped inside the frame. The picture is now laid out at natural size on a stage filling the viewport above the tray and drawn by one transform from the inspector's pure `core/viewport.ts`: `fit` with a 24px gutter (a bitmap is never upscaled, zoom never goes below the fit, so the fitted picture is always wholly visible); a click toggles fit ↔ max(100%, 2.5 × fit) about the click point; wheel and pinch zoom about the pointer; a drag pans an enlarged picture and swipes the gallery while fitted; `+` / `-` / `0` zoom from the keyboard; a click on the empty stage closes. **(B) STRUCTURE.** Of `fx-modal` only the embed container survives: `.vw-lightbox__panel` takes `.ins-embed`'s box (full size, `min-*: 0`, `overflow: clip`, `isolation`, solid `--bg`) as its own rules — `inspector.css` is not imported. The header, counter-in-header, Download control, floating stage arrows and `.vw-lightbox__frame` are gone, and the `.fx-modal*` rules `view.css` declared for the lightbox are deleted, which removes the shared-class collision #162 flag (n) recorded. Styles move to `features/view/styles/lightbox.css`, imported by `MediaLightbox.tsx`, so every island rendering it carries them. Close is the one floating control (solid `--surface-1`, no `backdrop-filter`). **(C) TRAY.** Previous · a scrolling thumbnail strip (`aria-current`, a 2px inset `--primary` ring, the active one kept in view) · Next · `n / m` (`tabular-nums`, a polite live region); drawn only for two or more items (gate 11). **(D) COMPATIBILITY.** `MediaLightbox` keeps its path, its props (`open`, `active`, `gallery`, `title`, `onClose`; `open` widened to accept a `ReadonlySignal`, `gallery` to `LightboxMedia`, which `EntityMedia` satisfies) and the `vw-lightbox` root class, so `EntityCanvas` is unchanged. **(E) TRIGGERS.** `/[handle]`: an image slide of `ProfileShowcase` is a button opening the lightbox on that slide (gallery = every slide index for index, videos play in place, the widest `srcset` candidate is drawn, auto-advance pauses while open). `/messages/[conversation]`, project rooms and the popout: a message whose attachments are two or more images and nothing else opens the lightbox (`attachmentGallery` — proxy `lg`, a vector's original, `sm` thumbnails); a single image or a mixed message keeps the file modal. FLAGS (a) a lone chat image still opens the file modal, so one image and two images open different surfaces — a human should decide whether a single image joins the lightbox; (b) the profile Work tab's tiles still navigate to the raw file (`href: full`) rather than opening the lightbox; (c) the lightbox draws one `src`, never a `srcset`, because a width-described candidate rescales the decoded natural size the zoom reads. | `apps/web/features/view/{components/{MediaLightbox,lightbox/*},hooks/useLightboxViewport,core/lightbox-model,types/lightbox-types,styles/{lightbox,view}.css}` · `apps/web/features/profile/{components/ProfileShowcase,core/showcase-model,styles/profile-showcase.css}` · `apps/web/features/projects/{components/{ChatAttachmentPreview,ChatImageLightbox},core/chat-attachments}` · `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` §D.8.0 |
| 185 | **`/api/auth/verification-status` stops being an email-enumeration oracle; the `/verify` poll is bound to the browser that joined (2026-10-13).** **(A) THE DEFECT.** The route was unauthenticated, took `?email=`, and `AuthBackendService.getVerificationStatus` answered `{ verified }` from a service-role read of `org.user_emails` for that address, so anyone could learn whether an address had a confirmed account. `/api/auth/resend` and `/api/auth/forgot-password` already avoided this. The route JSDoc also claimed the poll "auto-logs the user in"; it never minted a session. **(B) THE BINDING.** `POST /api/auth/join` (individual email/password and enterprise owner), and a password sign-in that GoTrue refuses only for an unconfirmed email, set `pj_pending_verification`: HttpOnly, `SameSite=Strict`, `Path=/api/auth`, one hour (GoTrue's OTP lifetime), value `v1.<userId>.<expSeconds>.<base64url HMAC-SHA256>` (`packages/backend/core/pending-verification.ts`). The HMAC key is derived from `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` under a fixed label, so no new env key; with no service key no cookie is minted and every poll is uniform. The token travels on the `ServiceResult.pendingVerification` envelope field and `toAuthResponse` mints it, the same way `session` stays out of the JSON body. **(C) THE POLL.** `services/auth/verification-status.ts` `resolveVerificationStatus` (pure, injected deps): it takes no email (any `?email=` is ignored); it answers only for the cookie's user id, from GoTrue `email_confirmed_at` via `auth.admin.getUserById` rather than `org.user_emails` (an address can also be someone else's secondary). No cookie, a forged / foreign-key / swapped-id / expired cookie, and a still-pending account all get the identical `200 { verified: false }`. Every poll goes through a `SlidingWindowLimiter` (30 / 60s, keyed `pending:<id>` when the cookie is valid, else `client:<remote address>`; 429 with `retryAt`). A failed lookup is a 503, never a false pending. `cache-control: no-store`. **(D) THE UI.** A confirmed answer carries `signedIn` (whether the request holds `sb-access-token`). `true` continues in as before (OTP entered, or the link opened in this browser, which set the session). `false` (link opened on another device) shows `EmailConfirmedPanel` ("Email confirmed" + Sign in → `/login?notice=confirmed`, which shows a success banner) instead of navigating into a guarded route that bounced to `/login`. New dev axis `emailConfirmation` (Server · Pending · Confirmed · Signed in) substitutes the poll answer client-side, since the stub backend never confirms. **(E) TESTS.** `pending-verification.test.ts` (round trip, foreign key, swapped id, expiry, malformed), `verification-status.test.ts` (email-only caller, unbound = pending body, mismatched cookies, per-account and per-address rate limits, both success paths, 503), `verify-poll.test.ts`, `respond.test.ts`. Probe `supabase/probes/verification_status_oracle.ts` compares the answers for a seeded and an unknown address over HTTP. FLAGS (a) **pre-existing, not fixed here:** at HEAD `service_role` has no `USAGE` on schema `org` (`00002500` grants `org` to `anon`, `authenticated` only; `has_schema_privilege('service_role','org','USAGE')` is false on the shared DB), so every service-role `org.*` read fails. The old poll swallowed that error and answered `false`, which hid the oracle on the live stack (it would reopen the moment the grant lands). The same failure breaks username sign-in (`resolveUsernameEmail`) and this change's unconfirmed-sign-in user-id lookup (`resolveSignInUserId`, which then sets no cookie; the 6-digit code still works). A human should decide whether `service_role` gets `USAGE` on `org`. (b) The limiter is per process, like every limiter in `core/rate-limit.ts`. (c) The fallback key is `ctx.info.remoteAddr`, the proxy's address behind a reverse proxy; unbound callers learn nothing either way. (d) The cookie is not cleared on confirmation; it expires within the hour and only ever reveals its own account's state. (e) `documentation/architecture/MVP_FLOW_AUDIT.md`, cited by the task brief, does not exist in the repo. | `packages/backend/{core/pending-verification.ts,services/{ServiceResult.ts,auth/{AuthBackendService,verification-status}.ts}}` · `apps/web/{routes/api/auth/verification-status.ts,utils/{auth-cookies,dev-seam}.ts,features/auth/{core/{respond,AuthService,verify-poll}.ts,components/EmailConfirmedPanel.tsx,hooks/useVerifyPollSimulation.ts,islands/{VerifyForm,LoginForm}.island.tsx,types/mod.ts},features/devtools/{core/dev-context.ts,components/DevContextPanel.tsx}}` · `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §Authentication · `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Authentication surfaces · `database/org/Tables.md` |
| 186 | **The ledger is append-only and reconciled nightly (2026-10-13).** MVP audit 3.3 / §2.5, work order M3. `finance.wallets.balance_cents` is a cache written beside `finance.transactions` by `fn_wallet_credit` / `fn_wallet_debit`; append-only was a convention held by grants (the service role held `GRANT ALL`), `wallet_id` was `ON DELETE CASCADE`, both primitives returned silently on a missing wallet, and nothing compared the cache with the ledger. Reproduced first: `supabase/probes/ledger_immutability.sql` failed every check on the unfixed schema (UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE and a service-role UPDATE all succeeded; a wallet delete cascaded; credit and debit to a missing wallet returned). **(A) IMMUTABLE.** `finance.fn_transactions_append_only()` raises `PL403` unconditionally, bound `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` per row and `BEFORE TRUNCATE` per statement (TRUNCATE skips row triggers), so the guarantee holds for the table owner and the service role too. No function updated or deleted ledger rows (grep), so there was nothing to refactor into compensating inserts; the two direct INSERTs in `simulate_wallet_transaction` are appends with consistent balances. **(B) RESTRICT.** `transactions.wallet_id` is `ON DELETE RESTRICT`, edited in place in `00000017`; no code path deletes a wallet. **(C) LOUD PRIMITIVES.** A missing wallet raises `PW404` from both primitives. Every credit caller already ran `fn_ensure_wallet` first, and every debit caller pre-checks the wallet (`PF402 no_wallet` / balance check) or holds it by id; the one path that relied on the silence, `settle_card_payment`, credits the wallet its `inbound_payments` row names (`NOT NULL … ON DELETE RESTRICT`), so a raise now rolls back the settlement with its `fn_claim_stripe_event` claim, the webhook answers 500 and Stripe redelivers — a succeeded payment with a NULL `transaction_id` is no longer reachable. **(D) RECONCILIATION.** `finance.fn_reconcile_wallets()` (definer, service role only) compares each cache with the ledger sum (the existing `v_wallet_reconciliation`) and the newest `balance_after_cents` in one statement, writes one `ops.log_entries` `error` row per drifted wallet (`scope = 'finance.reconcile_wallets'`), raises a WARNING and returns the count; it never corrects. `ops.log_entries` is reused rather than a new ops table. Scheduled nightly at 03:40 UTC as the guarded pg_cron job `finance-reconcile-wallets` (R2 has not landed). The local DB reconciled cleanly at the time (22 wallets, zero drift). FLAGS (a) the scheduler is the guarded `cron.schedule` block, like `finance-billing-cycle`; R2 should adopt the job and make a skipped registration loud. (b) Drift lands in `ops.log_entries` (admin-readable) plus a WARNING, with no page or email; that table's future retention sweep may delete old rows (a persisting drift is re-reported nightly). A real alert channel is an ops follow-up. (c) `live-payments.ts` maps the new codes to a generic logged 500, but `wallet-actions.ts` maps unknown SQLSTATEs to 422 with the raw message, so an internal `PW404` sentence could reach a user if a future caller skips `fn_ensure_wallet`. (d) The primitives still match currency exactly (case-sensitive); all stored currencies are upper-case today, and a lower-case caller now raises instead of vanishing. (e) Beyond the brief: the service role's grant on `finance.transactions` is narrowed from `ALL` to `SELECT, INSERT`, so the grant no longer contradicts the trigger. (f) R4's remainder (a `settled_unapplied` state, a payer notification, an ops view of succeeded payments without a ledger row) is not built here. | `supabase/migrations/{00000017_tables_finance,00001200_functions_finance_core,00001230_functions_finance_stripe,00001830_triggers_finance,00002510_permissions_function_grants,00002520_permissions_table_grants}.sql` · `supabase/probes/ledger_immutability.sql` · `documentation/database/{finance/{Tables,Functions,Policies},ops/Tables}.md` · `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §Integration Blueprints → Stripe · `finance-model.md` |
| 187 | **Every platform fee reads the payer's effective rate and truncates (2026-10-13).** CLOSES #68(b) and MVP flow audit row 3.10 / roadmap 0.9 (fee single source). The source of truth is `security.platform_params.platform_fee_bp = 500`, resolved per payer by `finance.fn_effective_platform_fee_bp` (an active `finance.negotiated_rates` row wins). **(A) SQL.** `finance.place_wallet_order` drops its hardcoded `c_fee_bp := 500` and charges `fn_effective_platform_fee_bp(basket owner_type, owner_id)`, the same resolver an escrow release reaches through `fn_escrow_fee_bp`, and records that rate in `orders.platform_fee_bp`; its stale "param is seeded 0" header is rewritten. `finance.preview_team_split` reads `fn_escrow_fee_bp(payer_business_id, payer_user_id)` of the escrow it projects instead of the bare param, and returns `fee_bp`. `finance.get_purchase_owner` (now plpgsql, since the resolver lives in a later file) adds `platform_fee_bp`, but only for a caller who passes `fn_owner_visible`, because a negotiated rate is a commercial term. **(B) ONE ROUNDING RULE — TRUNCATION.** `(amount × bp) / 10000` in integer arithmetic, as releases, fair exits and session settlement already did; `place_wallet_order`'s half-up `+ 5000` is removed. `finance-model.md` only prescribes integer division (the §5 split remainder), so this contradicts no source doc; §16.4 now states the rule. **(C) TS.** `platformFeeFor` truncates (`Math.floor`). `CheckoutBackendService` previews with `ResolvedOwner.platformFeeBp` (from `get_purchase_owner`). `live-wallet`'s team-split preview shows the released escrow's recorded `platform_fee_cents` instead of recomputing half-up. `PLATFORM_FEE_BP` stays a display fallback only (the standing card; an owner whose rate the caller cannot see), pinned to the seed by `platform-fee.test.ts`. The stale `CheckoutBackendService` re-export is removed. **(D) FIXTURES.** `board-fixtures` and the seed generator use `platformFeeFor` + `PLATFORM_FEE_BP` instead of `0.05` / a local `500`; `07_finance.sql` regenerates byte-identical (no seeded fee falls on a half cent). Probe: `supabase/probes/platform_fee_single_source.sql`. **FLAGS:** (a) checkout `totals.platformFee` still previews a fee on ticket/seat/revision lines, which `place_wallet_order` does not charge at purchase (it is charged at release) — pre-existing, not changed here. (b) The platform's Fee Collection wallet is still not materialised (#54(i)). | `supabase/migrations/00001210_functions_finance_kyc_wallet.sql` · `packages/types/finance/{checkout,platform-fee.test}.ts` · `packages/backend/services/finance/{CheckoutBackendService,commerce-owner,live-wallet}.ts` · `packages/backend/services/workspace/live-money.ts` · `packages/backend/services/projects/{board-fixtures,board-fees.test}.ts` · `supabase/seeds/gen/emit-finance.ts` · `supabase/probes/platform_fee_single_source.sql` · `database/finance/Functions.md` · `finance-model.md` §16.4 · `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` checkout note |
| 188 | **Escrow settlement is idempotent under concurrency, and the stage doors settle only an unsettled stage (2026-10-13).** **(A) THE DEFECT.** `finance.fn_release_ticket_escrow` looped `SELECT … WHERE ticket_id = … AND status = 'held'` with no row lock and flipped each row with `UPDATE … SET status = 'released' WHERE id = r.id`, never re-checking `status`. Under READ COMMITTED a second caller read the row as held, waited on the first caller's row lock, then updated it anyway once that committed, and credited the payee again. `fn_fair_exit_release` and `fn_refund_ticket_escrow` had the same shape. `projects.approve_stage` and `projects.cancel_stage_fair_exit` neither locked nor checked the stage, so two concurrent approvals both paid the payee, and any stage with held escrow could be approved or cancelled, even one already `paid` or `cancelled`. Reproduced with `supabase/probes/escrow_release_concurrency.sql` against a disposable copy of the database: 11 of 11 scenarios paid twice or settled a settled stage (e.g. two `approve_stage` calls: the first moved 8 ledger rows, the second added 4). **(B) THE FIX.** Each of the three finance functions locks the ticket row, then its held escrows `FOR UPDATE` in id order, and flips each one with `… WHERE id = r.id AND status = 'held' RETURNING id`, crediting nothing when no row comes back. Both stage doors take the stage row `FOR UPDATE` before settling and refuse with `55000` outside the unsettled set. Lock order is stage → ticket → escrow → wallet. After the fix, all 11 scenarios pass; `escrow_settlement_authority`, `single_ticket_purchase`, `paid_revisions`, `cohort_session_isolation` and `comms_column_grants` still pass. **(C) THE UNSETTLED SET (a rule this row CHOOSES, see flag (a)).** `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` had no stage lifecycle. Unsettled = `open` · `assigned` · `in_progress` · `submitted` · `revisions`. `approve_stage` also admits `approved` (approved but not yet paid → `paid`), while `cancel_stage_fair_exit` does not, because work already approved is not fair-exited. `paid` and `cancelled` are terminal. `open` and `assigned` stay settleable because `fund_ticket` and a Pipeline claim hold escrow without moving the stage on, so refusing them would strand held money. Recorded as the Stage settlement row in `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` §3.5, plus rule (6) in §3.6. FLAGS (a) a human should confirm the unsettled set, especially `approved` admitted for approval only and `open` / `assigned` admitted at all. No function writes `approved`, `submitted` or `revisions` today, and the other stage guards (`fund_ticket`, the Stripe lock, membership) treat `approved` as finished; (b) `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` §3.4 says the platform derives stage state from its tickets ("source of truth lives at the leaf"), but `projects.project_stages.status` is stored and written directly by `fund_stage`, `approve_stage` and `cancel_stage_fair_exit`. This is a pre-existing doc contradiction and was NOT resolved here; (c) the user-requested `documentation/architecture/MVP_FLOW_AUDIT.md` does not exist in the tree; (d) `deno task db:reset` was NOT run, because the local DB is shared by parallel sessions. The probe ran against a `pg_dump` copy (`escrow_probe`) with the five functions loaded. The shared dev DB still runs the OLD bodies until a `db:reset` or an applied `CREATE OR REPLACE`; (e) the probe commits by design (a race needs two commits), so it refuses the `postgres` database unless `probe.allow_commit=on`; (f) `fn_refund_ticket_escrow` was outside the brief and fixed for the same defect. | `supabase/migrations/00001200_functions_finance_core.sql` (`fn_release_ticket_escrow`, `fn_fair_exit_release`, `fn_refund_ticket_escrow`) · `supabase/migrations/00001150_functions_projects_stage_funding_submissions.sql` (`approve_stage`, `cancel_stage_fair_exit`) · `supabase/probes/escrow_release_concurrency.sql` · `database/{finance,projects}/Functions.md` · `api/projects-settlement.md` · `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` §3.5 / §3.6 |
| 189 | **One held base escrow per ticket, enforced by the database (2026-10-13).** **(A) THE DEFECT.** `finance.fn_hold_ticket_escrow` checked for a held escrow, then inserted one, and `idx_escrows_ticket` was not unique. Under READ COMMITTED a concurrent hold cannot see a sibling's uncommitted row, so two holds on one ticket both passed the check, both inserted, and the second debited the payer once its wallet-row wait cleared. `projects.fund_stage` read its candidate tickets without locking them, so two concurrent `fund_stage` calls (a double-submitted Fund all, a webhook replay racing the button) reached that path. Reproduced on HEAD: two held escrows and 20,000 debited for one 10,000 ticket, and a second `fund_stage` re-funding a ticket the first had already funded. **(B) A DISCRIMINATOR WAS MISSING.** A paid revision round's escrow (#167) is a second held row on the same ticket, and it was written with exactly the base escrow's shape: no kind or revision column existed, so no index could tell the two apart. `finance.escrows.revision_id uuid REFERENCES projects.stage_revision_requests (id) ON DELETE RESTRICT` (nullable, folded into `CREATE TABLE`) names the round; `fn_hold_revision_escrow` writes it; `NULL` is the base escrow. **(C) THE INDEXES** (`00004005`). `uq_escrows_ticket_base_held` UNIQUE `(ticket_id) WHERE status = 'held' AND ticket_id IS NOT NULL AND revision_id IS NULL`, so a revision round sits beside its base escrow and a seat escrow (no ticket) is untouched. `uq_escrows_revision` UNIQUE `(revision_id) WHERE revision_id IS NOT NULL`: a round is held once, whatever became of its escrow. `idx_escrows_ticket` stays for all-status reads. **(D) THE HOLD.** `fn_hold_ticket_escrow`'s early check now ignores revision rows (it and the index agree on what "held" means). The INSERT runs in a subtransaction: a `unique_violation` on `uq_escrows_ticket_base_held` means a concurrent hold committed first, read as "already held" and answered `NULL` before anything is debited or notified; a violation of any other constraint is re-raised. **(E) FUND_STAGE.** The fundable tickets are locked `FOR UPDATE` in id order before the individual's wallet check and before any hold, and only that locked set is held. A concurrent caller waits, re-reads the rows as `escrow_funded` and holds nothing (`P0001`, nothing fundable). Ticket locks always precede the payer's wallet-row lock, the same order `fund_ticket` (row lock, then hold) and a claim already follow; the auto-fund drain uses `SKIP LOCKED`. The index is the backstop for every other door. **(F) VERIFIED** on an isolated stack (HEAD plus only these edits, own `project_id`/ports): migrations and every seed apply under both indexes (the seed writes one escrow per ticket); the new `supabase/probes/escrow_uniqueness.sql` races two real transactions over `dblink` and passes, as do `paid_revisions`, `single_ticket_purchase`, `escrow_settlement_authority` and `cohort_session_isolation`. FLAGS (a) **The lost race returns `NULL`, not the existing escrow id** as the brief asked. `NULL` is #165(A)'s documented "already held" answer, and an id would be read as "this call held it": `fund_ticket` would report success for an escrow it did not make and `settle_card_payment` would write it into `locked_escrow_ids` (a chargeback on that card payment would then freeze someone else's capital); `fund_stage` would count it as funded; the auto-fund drain would mark it `auto_funded`. A human should confirm, since returning the id is a one-line change. (b) The brief cited `documentation/architecture/MVP_FLOW_AUDIT.md`, which does not exist in the repo. (c) The brief expected an existing escrow kind/revision column; there was none, so `revision_id` was added (additive, nullable). A database holding a pre-#189 revision escrow beside a held base escrow would refuse the index until that row's `revision_id` is backfilled; a consolidated `db:reset` never has one. (d) The shared local DB was not reset at this change: several sessions use it and a reset wipes everyone's data. The sessions agreed to one shared reset once all SQL had landed. `escrow_uniqueness.sql` COMMITS its fixture (a race needs two committed transactions) and refuses to run without `-v disposable=1`. (e) `EscrowSchema` already lagged the table (`payerBusinessId` non-nullable; no `payerUserId`, `cohortMembershipId`, `releasedAt`, `autoFunded`); only `revisionId` was added. (f) Other "anything held?" reads (the work gate on `trg_ticket_escrow_sync`, `fund_ticket`'s `PC409`, `begin_card_payment`) still count a revision row; a revision escrow only exists beside a held base escrow, so this does not change behaviour. | `supabase/migrations/{00000017_tables_finance,00001200_functions_finance_core,00001150_functions_projects_stage_funding_submissions,00004005_indexes_finance}.sql` · `supabase/probes/escrow_uniqueness.sql` · `packages/types/finance/ledger.ts` · `documentation/database/finance/{Tables,Functions}.md` · `documentation/database/projects/Functions.md` |
| 190 | **Age guardrails are enforced in Postgres, and the 13–17 restriction is derived from the date of birth, never stored (2026-10-13).** PRODUCT_SPEC §Age guardrails was held only by `AuthBackendService.provisionAccount`: a direct GoTrue sign-up (`enable_signup = true` → `handle_new_user`) or a direct `public.complete_onboarding` call provisioned a 12-year-old, and the `restricted` flag the wizard computed was stripped by Zod and never stored or read. **(A) FLOOR.** `public.provision_user_profile` runs `org.fn_check_dob` before any write: `22023` missing / future / before 1900, `PM422` under 13 — every provisioning path, the GoTrue trigger included, passes through it. `trg_users_public_dob_guard` (BEFORE INSERT / UPDATE OF `dob`) runs the same rule on any later write, and `users_public_dob_check` (`dob >= 1900-01-01`, the erasure sentinel) is the CHECK; the date-relative half lives in the trigger because a CHECK must not read today's date. **(B) RESTRICTION, DERIVED.** `org.fn_is_minor(uid)` reads `dob` at every call (no stored flag, so it lifts on the 18th birthday with nothing to run) and `public.custom_access_token_hook` stamps it as `active_context.isRestricted` (`UserContext.restricted`, chrome only; only an explicit `true` restricts). PRODUCT_SPEC's "persisted on the individual profile" is reworded to "re-derived at every check" at the owner's direction. **(C) ONE GATE, ONE SQLSTATE.** `org.fn_assert_adult(uid)` raises `PM403` in `org.enable_freelancer_profile`, `projects.apply_to_project`, `projects.assign_from_application` (the applicant), `projects.fn_assign_ticket_core` (the assignee — claim, manual, round-robin, parallel), `projects.fund_stage` / `fund_ticket` / `fund_paid_revision`, `finance.place_wallet_order` (so `place_on_account_order`), `finance.begin_card_payment` and `finance.begin_payout`, and `catalogue.trg_listings_publish_age_gate` refuses any move to `published` (a trigger, so `set_listing_status` stays an invoker and no write path publishes past it). The helpers are org functions under deny-by-default, so no client can ask whether somebody else is a minor. **(D) API MIRROR.** `refuseIfRestricted` (`apps/web/utils/age-gate.ts`) answers 403 before the service on `/api/user/freelancer`, `/api/projects/apply`, `/api/catalogue/status` (publish only), `/api/checkout/create`, `/api/finance/{escrow,topup}/intent`, `/api/wallet/payouts` and `/api/wallet/action` (`top_up` · `withdraw` · `fund_escrow`); the services map `PM403` through `packages/backend/core/age-gate.ts`; both speak `RESTRICTED_REFUSAL` with reason `age_restricted` (checkout as a `not_authorised` blocker). `AuthBackendService` maps `PM422` to the dob field. **(E) SSOT.** The `/join` body (`JoinSchema`, the email / username / phone patterns, the employee-tier and industry values) moves to `@projective/types/auth/join.ts`, which refuses an under-13 or implausible `dob` with the form's copy; `features/auth/core/schema.ts` re-exports it and the app's option lists are typed and tested against its values; the wizard no longer sends `restricted`. `AGE_SQLSTATE`, `RESTRICTED_REFUSAL`, `UNDER_MINIMUM_REFUSAL`, `DOB_FLOOR` join `age.ts`. **(F) DEV AXIS.** Dev Context Switcher **Age** (`ageRestriction`: Token · Minor (13–17) · Adult) — `DevOverrides` + `DEV_DEFAULTS`, `DEV_AGE_RESTRICTIONS`, the panel field, `data-dev-age-restriction` set and deleted in `reflect()`, folded into `restricted` by `applyDevContext` and `deriveEffectiveContext`, and mirrored to the `pj.dev.age` cookie the route mirror honours only when `DENO_ENV` is `development`; Adult skips the mirror so a real minor meets the database's own `PM403`. Probe: `supabase/probes/age_guardrails.sql` (12-year-old refused by `complete_onboarding` and by a direct GoTrue insert; a 16-year-old refused all ten doors; 17-and-a-day-before vs 18-today at the boundary; the claim; plausibility). FLAGS (a) the database ages against UTC `CURRENT_DATE` while the form ages in the browser's local date, so the two can disagree for a few hours either side of a birthday; (b) no UI reads `restricted` yet — Become a Partner, Pay and Withdraw still render for a minor and answer with the 403 copy on press, so a human should decide whether those controls explain themselves up front (gate 11); (c) a minor may still create a project and accept applicants (only funding is gated) — whether hiring without paying is "buying" is a product call; (d) a minor may still be a team or business member whose team is paid and distributes to them, and `transfer` / `distribute` / `new_recurring` are not gated; (e) there is no seeded minor persona — the probe builds its own in a rolled-back transaction. | `supabase/migrations/{00000011,00001010,00001020,00001130,00001140,00001150,00001170,00001210,00001230,00001240,00001700,00001810}` · `supabase/probes/age_guardrails.sql` · `packages/types/auth/{age,join,user-context}.ts` · `packages/backend/core/age-gate.ts` · `packages/backend/services/{auth/AuthBackendService,user/freelancer-unlock,projects/live-applications,catalogue/live-catalogue,finance/{live-payments,wallet-actions,CheckoutBackendService}}.ts` · `apps/web/utils/{age-gate,dev-seam}.ts` · `apps/web/features/{auth/core/*,devtools/*,shell/core/effective-context.ts}` · eight `apps/web/routes/api/*` routes · `documentation/database/{org,projects,finance,catalogue,security}/*` · SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE §Authentication & Identity · PRODUCT_SPEC §Age guardrails |
| 191 | **Earned credits clear a 7-day pending window before they are withdrawable or spendable (2026-10-13).** MVP audit work order M4. **(A) THE DEFECT.** `finance.pending_releases` (00000017) and `security.platform_params.pending_release_days = 7` existed but nothing wrote or read them: `fn_release_ticket_escrow`, `fn_fair_exit_release`, `fn_split_team_payout` (team shares, finder fee, vault cut), `fn_release_seat_escrow` and `place_wallet_order` (`product_sale`) all credited `balance_cents` at once, and `begin_payout` let all of it leave the same minute. Reproduced first: `supabase/probes/pending_window.sql` showed a ticket release moving the payee's Available balance 433,610 → 2,523,610 immediately, with all five doors crediting Available. **(B) THE HUMAN RULINGS (2026-10-13, asked per §8 before any code):** (1) **top-ups are NOT held**: finance-model §7 holds only releases, and PRODUCT_SPEC §5 spends a card payment the moment it settles (`settle_card_payment` credits `topup` then runs `fund_stage` / `fund_ticket` / `place_wallet_order` in the same transaction), so holding it would break card checkout; refunds, transfers and returned payouts stay immediate too. (2) **Pending money cannot be spent on the platform**: Available only. (3) **A lost chargeback recovers from the PAYER's wallet only** — its pending rows first, then Available; a freelancer's earnings are not clawed back, so Decision #126's flag stays open. (4) The sweep is a **guarded pg_cron block**, not a cron route. **(C) THE MODEL — derived, not a column.** `balance_cents` stays the Available balance and Pending is the sum of `amount_cents − recovered_cents` over a wallet's `state = 'pending'` rows, which is how `live-wallet.ts` already built the hero's "Clearing" figure. A held credit writes a `pending_releases` row and NO ledger line; the sweep writes the ledger line (original `reason`, original `escrows` / `orders` reference) when it matures. So `balance_after_cents` keeps meaning Available, `list_ledger` / `ledger_flow` / the Income Smoother see exactly one credit per earning (7 days later), #186's append-only ledger is never updated, and `fn_reconcile_wallets` stays clean. Because every spend door (`place_wallet_order`, `fund_stage`, `fund_ticket`, `fund_paid_revision`, transfers) and `begin_payout` already debit `balance_cents`, rule (2) needed no change to any of them. A `pending` ledger line at release time was rejected: `ledger_flow` sums by reason, so it and the maturing credit would double-count earnings, and its `balance_after_cents` would not move. **(D) SCHEMA (00000017, edited in place, moved below `finance.orders` for its FK).** `escrow_id` nullable + `order_id` (exactly one, `ck_pending_releases_one_source`), `reason`, `recovered_cents` (`0 ≤ … ≤ amount_cents`), `matured_at`, `transaction_id`; all FKs `ON DELETE RESTRICT` (were CASCADE), `available_at ≥ released_at`, and `state = 'available'` ⇔ `matured_at` set. **(E) FUNCTIONS (00001240 §7).** `fn_wallet_credit_pending` (fn_wallet_credit's arguments, so each door swapped one name; a 0-day window credits at once; `PW404` on a missing wallet), `fn_mature_pending_releases(p_limit)` (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, idempotent, pg_cron `finance-mature-pending-releases` hourly at :05) and `fn_recover_from_pending` (soonest-to-clear first; an emptied row closes with no credit), wired into `record_dispute_closed`'s lost branch, whose outcome now also reports `recovered_pending_cents`. Grants in 00002510. **(F) TYPES & UI.** `PendingReleaseSchema` gains `orderId`, `reason`, `recoveredCents`, `maturedAt`, `transactionId` with one-source and recovery refinements; `services/finance/pending-window.ts` (`stillClearing`, `clearingKindLabel`, tested) normalises the overview's read; a sale reads "Sale clearing"; the Pending tooltip says the money cannot be spent or withdrawn yet. **FLAGS for a human:** (a) the shared local DB has NOT been migrated — applying the DDL was refused by the session's permission classifier, and `live-wallet.ts` now selects `order_id` / `recovered_cents`, so `/wallet` (live) errors until `deno task db:reset` runs; (b) `on_hold` (a platform dispute freezing a release mid-window, finance-model §7's diagram) is not wired; (c) the ledger's clearing marker in `wallet-ledger.ts` (`facts.clearing`) can no longer match, since a release has no ledger line until it matures — harmless, left for a cleanup; (d) a matured earning is dated in the ledger and analytics on its maturity day, not its release day; (e) `supabase/probes/escrow_release_concurrency.sql` (#188) counts `finance.transactions` rows per escrow — a release now writes a `pending_releases` row instead, so that probe should count both. | |
| 192 | **Restricted (13–17) accounts see only what they can use; their projects are personal; ages are counted in the person's own time zone; guardian-consented selling is specified and disabled for the MVP (2026-10-13, at the owner's direction).** RESOLVES #190 flags (a) (the age disparity), (b) (no warning UI) and (c) (a minor creating and running projects). **(A) ONE CLOCK.** An age is whole years on today's date in the person's IANA zone — `computeAge(dob, timeZone)` (`todayIn`, `isTimeZone`, `browserTimeZone`, `AGE_FALLBACK_TIME_ZONE = UTC`) and `org.fn_age_years(dob, zone)` (`fn_today_in`, `fn_valid_time_zone`) apply one rule. The join form sends the browser's zone (`JoinSchema.timeZone`, an unknown one dropped), the server validates against it, and `provision_user_profile` stores it as `users_public.timezone`; `fn_check_dob`, `trg_users_public_dob_guard` and `fn_is_minor` read it. A 29 February birthday turns over on 1 March, as `age()` does. **(B) HIDDEN, NOT REFUSED.** `isRestrictedPath` (`features/shell/core/restricted-surfaces.ts`) names Wallet, Businesses, basket/checkout, Catalogue, Services/Products, Teams, Analytics, Become a Freelancer and Settings → Billing / Verification: `globalNav` and `createMenuOptions` drop them, the account popover drops Become a Freelancer, Wallet, the Teams/Businesses switcher sections and their create links, the header drops the basket, Settings gates Billing, Verification & payouts and the Businesses entry (`adultOnly`), and the `(dashboard)` middleware's gate 3 sends a direct visit home with a 303 (a thrown 404 would drop rotated session cookies). **(C) THE WARNING UI.** One unboxed `InlineNotice` in the meta register replaces each in-page transaction: the `/view` lane and its below-breakpoint block (`RestrictedLaneNotice`, `RESTRICTED_REFUSAL`; a session's slots stay visible with `viewerCanBook` false), the project create modal (`PERSONAL_PROJECT_NOTE` instead of currency and baseline price) and Settings → Account ("Limited account"). The profile rig loses Hire and Add-to-project (Message leads), and Explore withholds the "Become a Freelancer" promo (`viewerRestricted`). Islands follow the switcher's Age axis through `useEffectiveContext` / the new `useRestricted`. **(D) PERSONAL PROJECTS.** `projects.projects.is_personal` is set on insert when the owner is a minor and is permanent. Table triggers (`00001130` / `00001820`, because the app writes these tables directly under RLS): `trg_projects_personal_guard` (one owner — no business, team or enterprise; visibility and publish visibility pinned to `invite_only`; no sessions, budget, auto-fund or deadline bonuses), `trg_stages_personal_money_guard` and `trg_tickets_personal_guard` (no prices, no hiring, only the owner holds a ticket), `trg_personal_project_closed` (no participants, assignments, seats, roles, budget rules, maintenance contracts, invite links, applications, invitations or cohorts). `fn_ticket_escrow_sync` / `fn_ticket_delete_protocol` skip a personal project, so work moves with no escrow — a deliberate exception to #165, which exists to protect a payee a personal project cannot have. The reads stamp `personal` on `ProjectDetail`, `ProjectSetup` (no Budget / Roles / Terms & visibility sections, no pricing / roles / rules / publish rungs, no price, skills, capacity or NDA fields), `ProjectWorkspace` (no pricing or applications actions, no Ready-to-start, People, Terms or Invite people) and `BoardCard` (`funding` null, no Finances tab); Members and Preview redirect and leave the navigation, and the setup rig drops Publish. **(E) OUT OF OTHER PEOPLE'S PROJECTS.** `trg_project_joiner_adult` refuses an invitation addressed to, an application from, and a participant or assignment row naming a minor — every join path including `apply_to_seat`, invite links and invitation acceptance. A minor who chose "I want to work" at sign-up starts as a client, and the setup progress never suggests `become_partner` to one. **(F) GUARDIAN CONSENT — DISABLED.** PRODUCT_SPEC §Age guardrails records the post-MVP rule: an older minor may sell with consent from an adult aged 21+ who passes an ID check, gives personal details and signs a consent form, with the minor's messages and attachments monitored, including inside project stages. Nothing of it is built. Probes: `supabase/probes/personal_projects.sql` (19 checks) and `age_guardrails.sql`. FLAGS (a) published working hours and discovery calls are not gated, so an adult can still book a call with a minor who publishes hours — a human should decide whether scheduling joins the restricted set; (b) Teams are hidden but `org.create_team` and workspace-invitation acceptance are not refused for a minor in the database; (c) a personal project has no Publish and so reads as a draft in the feed for its whole life — a "Personal" label would read better; (d) messaging between minors and adults is unrestricted until the guardian feature's monitoring exists; (e) a person who edits their profile time zone moves their birthday by at most a day; (f) the guardian feature's age band ("slightly older users") is undecided; (g) the `isRestricted` claim refreshes with the token, so the chrome can trail an 18th birthday by up to one token lifetime while the database has already lifted the restriction. | `supabase/migrations/{00000015,00001010,00001110,00001130,00001140,00001820}` · `supabase/probes/personal_projects.sql` · `packages/types/auth/{age,join}.ts` · `packages/types/projects/{detail,setup,workspace,board}.ts` · `packages/backend/services/{auth/AuthBackendService,projects/{live-detail,live-writes,live-board}}.ts` · `apps/web/features/{shell/{core/{restricted-surfaces,nav-model,actions-model},hooks/useRestricted,islands/UserActions.island},explore/{core/promo-model,islands/PromoGate.island,components/{ExploreHome,ExploreScreen}},view/{components/{EntityViewPage,RestrictedLaneNotice},core/booking-ssr,styles/entity-view.css},profile/*,settings/*,projects/*,auth/*}` · `apps/web/routes/{(dashboard)/_middleware,(dashboard)/_layout,(dashboard)/projects/[projectSlug]/{_middleware,preview},(public)/explore,(public)/view/[entity]/index,[handle]/_layout,[handle]/view/[item]/index,api/auth/join}` · PRODUCT_SPEC §Age guardrails · PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT §3.5 · SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE §Authentication & Identity · DESIGN_SYSTEM §D.7.4 / promo slots · `documentation/database/{org,projects}/*` |
| 193 | **Claim-changing mutations re-issue the session server-side; a transient refresh failure never signs anyone out (2026-10-13).** **(A) PROBLEM.** `POST /api/auth/refresh` cleared both `sb-*` cookies on ANY failure, and `AuthBackendService.refreshSession` mapped every GoTrue error — a dropped connection and a 5xx alike — to `401`. `useContextSwitch` and `partner-service` called that endpoint straight after a successful switch / unlock, so a GoTrue blip signed the user out at the exact moment they changed persona; the page guards (`ensureSession`) and `apiFetch` had the same reading. And `POST /api/context/switch` / `POST /api/user/freelancer` left the claims stale for any caller other than those two clients. **(B) THE SPLIT.** `services/auth/refresh-outcome.ts` (`isRefreshRejection` / `refreshFailure`, tested against auth-js's own error classes): a refused grant — GoTrue `400`/`401`/`403` (`refresh_token_not_found`, `refresh_token_already_used`, `invalid_grant`, `user_banned`) or `AuthSessionMissingError` — is `401`; everything else (network, `5xx`, `429`, an unparseable body, a session-less success) is `503`. The refresh route clears the cookies on `401` only and answers `503` with them kept; `ensureSession` returns `clearCookies` only on `401` (the guard still bounces, but the session survives the outage); `apiFetch` routes to `/login` only when the refresh answered `401`, and hands the original `401` back on a `503`. **(C) SERVER RE-ISSUE.** Both mutation routes call `session.ts` `respondWithReissuedSession` after a successful service result: `renewSession` mints the rotated cookies onto the same response and the body carries `claimsStale: false`; a failed renewal keeps the old cookies and answers `claimsStale: true` (`WorkspaceResult.claimsStale`). A failed mutation never touches the session. **(D) CLIENTS.** `useContextSwitch` and `partner-service` drop their own `/api/auth/refresh` call, hard-navigate on success as before, and on `claimsStale` report and do not navigate (Decision #150's rule); both mutations are idempotent, so retrying re-issues again. AMENDS #46 (fail-closed now means refusal-only) and #150 (the client-side refresh step). Tests: `refresh-outcome.test.ts`, `apps/web/utils/session-reissue.test.ts`, `apps/web/utils/api-client.test.ts`. **(E) FOLLOW-UPS, BUILT THE SAME DAY (closing flags (b)–(d)).** (b) `BecomePartnerWizard`'s alert action was **Reload**, which re-mints nothing (a reload of a page whose access cookie is live never renews); it is now **Try again**, re-running the idempotent unlock, which re-issues the session. (c) The Settings writes re-issue server-side too: `POST /api/user/handle`, a `freelancer_profile` `POST`/`DELETE /api/user/lifecycle` (account deletion changes no claim), and a `PATCH /api/user/preferences` whose patch touches a claim (`touchesTokenClaims` in `@projective/types/org`: `locale`, `preferredDisplayCurrency`, `dateFormat` — the `CLAIM_PREFERENCE_KEYS` the hook stamps); `serviceBody` split out of `serviceResponse`. `SettingsService.renewSession` is deleted, `ClaimsReissue` types the flag, and on `claimsStale` Settings says the old value may show for up to an hour (the access token's lifetime, after which `ensureSession` renews) and skips the account-type reload. The header currency switcher, which never re-minted at all, is covered by the same route. (d) `ensureSession` now reports `unavailable`; the `(dashboard)` guard throws `HttpError(503)` on it instead of bouncing to `/login`, and `_error.tsx` answers a 503 with "Projective is briefly unavailable … You're still signed in" and a **Try again** link to the same URL. The `[handle]`, `(standalone)` and `/explore` renewals still fall back to the guest view during an outage — correct for public pages, and the cookies are kept. **FLAGS for a human:** (a) `documentation/architecture/MVP_FLOW_AUDIT.md`, which the task cited, does not exist (already #189(b)); (e) `429` is classed as transient — a rate-limited refresh keeps the session; (f) the 503 copy assumes the guard is the only `HttpError(503)` thrower, which is true today — a future thrower would need its own copy. | |
| 195 | **Quick-Init create is one transaction, asks for currency and a baseline price, and the profile-opened modal sends its invitation (2026-10-13).** Human-directed. **(A) ATOMIC CREATE.** `insertProject` (`live-writes.ts`) now makes ONE `projects.create_project` call with the payload `createProjectPayload` builds (title, `format`/`structure_variation`, optional brief, `currency`, the `fixed_price` budget pair with the baseline on a one-off only, and one explicit root stage — `Delivery`/`Stage 1` — priced at the baseline). The three objections its old docblock gave for avoiding the RPC (no id fallback, no budget pair, a stage insert without price/milestone) are all fixed in the current function; its `draft`/`unlisted` pins equal `liveVisibilityFor("draft", …)` and `publish_visibility` keeps its `'public'` default (`CREATED_PUBLISH_VISIBILITY`). The previous direct insert + `create_stage` + `get_or_create_project_channel` sequence only WARNED when the stage or room failed, leaving a stage-less draft; now any failure rolls the create back. No SQL changed. **(B) FIELDS.** The modal asks for **Currency** (`CurrencySelect` over `DISPLAY_CURRENCIES`, seeded from `preferred_display_currency`; the codebase has no narrower "wallet currency" list — wallets open on demand per currency — so this is the set the Rules section and the service already accept) and an optional **baseline price** (`BASELINE_PRICE_LABEL[format]`), in a new `ProjectCreatePricingFields` child. This reverses #117's "not asked" and re-aligns the code with `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Quick-Init, which already listed both fields. **(C) INVITE.** `CreateProjectSchema.inviteeHandle`; after the draft exists `ProjectBackendService.create` reads its hire brief and calls `hire` with `hireForCreatedProject(brief, handle)` (every stage at configured terms, or the whole project on a Task), so the row goes through `projects.invite_to_project` exactly as Add-to-project does — a staged placeholder on a draft. The #103(a) blocker #117(c)/#118(c) cited is gone (#108 identity addressing; the live hire write). A refused invitation never fails the create: `CreatedProjectSchema.invitation = { handle, outcome: "sent" \| "staged" \| "refused", message }`, and on `refused` the modal stays open with the reason and an **Open project** action. SUPERSEDES #117(c) / #118(c); AMENDS #85 (the create path) and #117 (fields). **(D) ADDRESS.** Navigation stays on the minted `prj-` slug (immutable, ROUTING.md; #88); `CreatedProjectSchema.slug` is now validated against `slugPattern("project")` and the docblocks that still said "navigate by id" are corrected. Tests: `live-create.test.ts` (single RPC, refusal mapping, payload, payload-keys-vs-SQL contract), `create_test.ts` (invitation staged / Task / refused-keeps-draft / unknown handle, currency narrowing), `hire.test.ts` (`hireForCreatedProject`). **FLAGS for a human:** (a) the create still ignores `scopeType`/`scopeId` (the modal always sends `personal`), although `create_project` can file a project under a team/business/enterprise with membership checks — wiring it is a product call; (b) a create-with-invite counts against the per-owner hire rate limit, so a burst of creates from profiles can come back `refused` on the invite; (c) `ProjectBackendService.hire`'s docblock still says persistence is "the per-process store on BOTH sides", which the live `insertInvitations` path contradicts; (d) a seller with a required intake question can never be invited from the create modal (it collects no answers) — the modal says so and points to the roster. | |
| 194 | **Approval settles the board and the money together — accepting a submission pays its ticket, and `approve_stage` completes what it pays (2026-10-13).** **(A) THE DEFECT.** `projects.approve_stage` released each held escrow through `finance.fn_release_ticket_escrow` without moving the ticket, so `payment_status` landed `partially_released` (the release reads `completed` to choose `released`) and every card stayed in Review on a `paid` stage; its `total_paid_cents` / `fee_cents` / `splits` summed every released escrow on the stage, earlier releases included; and `stage_approved` reached only ticket assignees, never team-split recipients or the client. `projects.review_submission('accept')` marked the submission accepted and released nothing, though PRODUCT_SPEC (Task-based workflow, Resolution) and PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT §3.6 ("Approve → Released") say approval pays. **(B) THE HUMAN RULING (2026-10-13, asked before any code):** option A — accepting a submission releases that ticket's escrow at once; "Approve Stage" pays out whatever is still held and closes the stage. Option B (acceptance as a non-financial acknowledgement, relabelled) was declined. **(C) ONE SETTLE PATH (00001150 §5c).** `projects.fn_settle_ticket_stage(ticket, stage, reason)` locks the ticket, snapshots its held escrows and, when the ticket stands on that stage, calls `projects.force_complete_stage`: on the final required stage that is the `completed` status write, so `trg_ticket_authority_guard` (review authority), `trg_ticket_escrow_sync` (the release) and `payment_status = 'released'` all happen on the guarded path; a multi-stage ticket advances (and funds) its next stage as `force_complete_stage` already did. It writes a `ticket_history` row carrying the `reason` and, on completion, a `milestone_confirmed` activity row; anything in the snapshot still held (a ticket already completed, or past the stage) is released directly; it returns the escrows THIS call released. `projects.fn_notify_payees` tells each individual payee and every `finance.payout_splits` recipient (members and finder) once. Both are internal, with no grant (00002510). **(D) THE DOORS.** `review_submission` locks the stage row for either verdict and refuses a `paid` / `cancelled` stage (`55000`); on `accept` it settles the ticket and sends `submission.accepted` to the submitter and every payee, and it returns the serialized submission plus `released_count`. `approve_stage` settles each ticket holding escrow on the stage through the same helper; sums `total_paid_cents` / `fee_cents` / `splits` over this call's escrows only; settles a stage whose tickets acceptances already paid (`released_count` 0) and still refuses one nothing was ever funded on; and sends `stage.approved` to the payees, split recipients and remaining assignees, plus a "Stage settled" `stage.approved` to the owner and, when another client member approved, that approver. Lock order is submission → stage → ticket (accept) and stage → ticket (approve), so an acceptance racing an approval waits and is refused, or lands first and the approval finds that ticket already paid. **(E) TYPES, SERVICE, UI.** `SubmissionReviewedSchema.releasedCount`; `liveWrite` takes a message derived from the result ("Submission accepted — payment released.", "Stage approved and closed — every ticket was already paid on acceptance."); the review modal's button reads **Accept & release payment**; the approve dialog says it releases any escrow still held and that accepted submissions were paid on acceptance; the board re-reads the card after an acceptance so it leaves Review. **(F) PROBE** (`supabase/probes/approval_settlement.sql`, 37 checks). On a private stack built from the working tree (every migration and seed): on a team stage, accepting one submission → that ticket `completed` / `released`, credited once (members + vault = net, in `pending_releases`), all three split recipients notified; then approving → `released_count` 1, `total_paid_cents` = the remaining ticket's net only, every ticket `completed` / `released`, nothing held, the accepted escrow not credited again, recipients and owner notified. Approving without accepting → both escrows credited once; every ticket accepted, then approving → 0 / 0 and `paid`; an individual payee accepted → one pending credit; the refusals (a second approval, an acceptance on a paid stage, a freelancer accepting or approving) and the helper grants hold. `function_grants`, `escrow_settlement_authority`, `paid_revisions`, `submission_door` and `escrow_uniqueness` pass; `escrow_release_concurrency` passes all 11 scenarios once it counts `pending_releases` rows (#191 flag (e)). AMENDS PRODUCT_SPEC (Task-based workflow, Resolution) and PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT §3.6 (In review, Released) and §2 (Stage settlement). **FLAGS for a human:** (a) acceptance is one click with no confirmation step although it now moves money irreversibly — the button says so; whether to add a confirm is a UX call; (b) the client notification goes to the project owner and the approving member, not to every billing member of the client business; (c) approving a stage now advances a multi-stage ticket to its next stage and holds that stage's escrow (`force_complete_stage`'s existing behaviour), where the old approval released the installment and left the ticket where it was; (d) the shared local DB has none of this (nor #188 / #189 / #191) — `deno task db:reset` applies it, and until then `releasedCount` reads 0; (e) `supabase/probes/platform_fee_single_source.sql` (#187) reads the `product_sale` credit from `finance.transactions` and FAILs since #191 holds it in `pending_releases` — unrelated to this change; (f) tickets the old approval left `partially_released` in Review are not backfilled. | |
| 197 | **Whole-stage funding made reachable — prepaid, unbound ticket escrow bound at the claim; stages funded while open · assigned · in progress; by card and by wallet; individual and business payers; the card intent and `fund_stage` agree on the priced set (2026-10-13). Product-owner ruling: option (A) of the three put to them, stage scope "open, assigned, in progress".** **(A) THE DEFECT** (MVP flow audit §2.2, reproduced on the shared DB in a rolled-back probe before any edit): `projects.fund_stage` funded only tickets that were assigned AND unfunded — a state the claim trigger makes nearly unreachable (it holds or aborts the moment an assignee is set; a refused claim rolls the assignee back) and that neither acceptance path nor `create_stage` produces. So a hired stage answered "No assigned, unfunded tickets", the card intent the same (`PC409`), `fund_ticket` refused an unclaimed ticket ("Nobody can be paid … yet" — #166(d)'s prepayment was never built), a refused claim left the ticket `needs_funding` with no door that could fund it, the wallet's Fund escrow refused every non-business wallet, `POST /api/finance/escrow/intent` had no UI caller (#169(a)), and `begin_card_payment` skipped unpriced tickets while `fund_stage` raised `no_price` on them — a settled card payment landed in the wallet unheld (probe: 10,000 charged, `lock_status = failed`, no escrow). **(B) OPTIONS PUT TO THE OWNER:** (A) prepaid unbound escrow per priced ticket, payee bound at claim — **chosen**; (B) a stage-level pool drawn down per ticket at claim — rejected (breaks one-escrow-per-ticket, which release, chargeback freezes, the wallet views and `deriveFundingState` all rely on); (C) assign-at-accept — rejected (contradicts Pipeline pull claims, multi-seat stages and capacity, and tickets created after the hire still dead-end). **(C) SCHEMA** (`00000017`, edited in place; no column or FK dropped on `finance.escrows`): `payee_type` / `payee_id` nullable; `ck_escrows_payee_pair` (both or neither); `ck_escrows_unbound` (an unbound escrow is a held base ticket escrow, or refunded — never released). **(D) DOORS:** `finance.fn_ticket_payee` (the one payee rule); `fn_hold_ticket_escrow` holds with no payee (only the client is told: "It is paid to whoever claims it."); `finance.fn_bind_ticket_escrow` names the payee from `fn_ticket_escrow_sync` at a claim or assignment before it would hold — no second debit, the payee told they can start; `fn_release_ticket_escrow` and `fn_fair_exit_release` refund an unbound escrow instead of paying nobody; `projects.fn_stage_funding_need` is THE priced set `fund_stage` holds and `begin_card_payment` charges (in the stage, `unpaid`/`needs_funding`, not finished, no held escrow, claimed or not), and both refuse `PC409` while any ticket is unpriced — the card intent before any charge; `fund_stage` accepts open · assigned · in_progress (an assigned stage still moves to in_progress), returns `awaiting_claim_count`, and "nothing fundable" is `PC409`; `fund_ticket` and the ticket card intent drop their payee refusal; `projects.get_stage_funding_quote` (`authenticated`) is the payer's read. **(E) APP:** `wallet-actions.fundEscrow` funds from a business vault or a person's own wallet and re-quotes through the database at the press; `fundableStages` lists both (a person's on `PersonalExtras.fundable`, Fund a stage offered only while one waits) with `projectRowId` / `unpricedCount`; the wallet's Fund a stage gains **From this wallet · By card** (escrow intent → Payment Element → `PaymentsService.waitForLock`); new `GET /api/finance/escrow/quote` (`StageFundingBackendService`), the tested `payments/core/stage-funding.ts`, and `FundStageDialog` opened by **Fund stage** in the stage board's money sentence (the project board keeps Fund all, the basket hand-off). `deriveFundingState` reads a prepaid ticket as Funded (`paidAhead` now means held); the board applies a stage card payment to every ticket in that stage. **Docs:** PRODUCT_SPEC :54, the §2 lifetimes table and §Escrow Lifecycle for Tickets (audit §2.6(a) resolved by this ruling: escrow is held before work, at the latest at the claim, never at In Progress); PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT §3.6 (Draft → Funded by prepaying; the Needs funding → Draft edges retired); `database/finance/{Tables,Functions}.md`, `database/projects/Functions.md`; DESIGN_SYSTEM §C.1 roster + §D.7.10 (a project workspace is not an entity view, so the conversion-lane gate does not place this control); SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE's Stripe route table. **VERIFIED** on a private stack (#189's commit plus only these edits, own `project_id`/ports): migrations and seeds apply; `supabase/probes/stage_funding_prepaid.sql` 8/8 PASS (open stage prepaid, double-funding refused, claim binds without a second debit and notifies, unbound release refunds and `ck_escrows_unbound` refuses released, unpriced refused by both doors then quote = card charge = escrows locked, in-progress stage funds a late ticket and a paid stage refuses, `fund_ticket` prepays, an individual owner funds and a stranger cannot); `single_ticket_purchase` (updated: an unclaimed ticket is held prepaid), `escrow_uniqueness` (updated refusal text), `paid_revisions`, `escrow_settlement_authority`, `cohort_session_isolation`, `comms_column_grants` pass; touched TS type-checks and lints; 107 related unit tests pass. **FLAGGED — for a human:** (a) the shared local DB was NOT reset or patched (several peer sessions live on it): until it is, its `fund_stage` still refuses open stages, and the quote read — and so the wallet's Fund a stage, which now re-quotes through it — fails there; (b) the team-seat payee rule reads `stage_assignments.status = 'accepted'` but both live accept paths write `assigned` (only seeds write `accepted`), so a team hired live is never resolved as the payee and a member's claim binds to that member — unchanged here; (c) a prepaid escrow stays on the stage it was funded in, so a ticket moved to another stage before its claim keeps its escrow there; (d) an unclaimed prepaid ticket in a stage settled by `approve_stage` (#194) is refunded, not paid, and its `payment_status` returns to `unpaid` on a completed ticket; | 206 | **Freelancers claim `open_pull` tickets from the board (2026-10-13).** A provider dragging a Ready card into In Progress called `projects.move_ticket`, which writes a status and no assignee, so `trg_ticket_escrow_sync` always raised `PF402`; the real claim (`projects.claim_ticket` → `fn_assign_ticket_core`: capacity, payout readiness, the escrow hold or prepaid bind) had no app caller. **(A) THE DOOR.** `POST /api/projects/ticket/claim` (`ClaimTicketSchema` `{ projectId, ticketId }`, age and dev payout mirrors) → `ProjectBackendService.claimTicket` → `live-claim.ts` `claimTicketRow`, with `p_assignee_id` always the session's own user. `claimRefusal` words every refusal (`NULL` → 402 `needs_funding`, `PK403 payout_not_ready` → 403 with the payout gate's `details`, `PM403`, cap breach 409 `over_capacity` in the database's sentence, lost race 409, undescribed 422, not `open_pull` 422). The stub mirrors the board-visible rules and moves no money. **(B) THE CONTROL.** Eligibility is one pure rule, `isSelfClaimable` (Ready · unclaimed · described · not personal · `open_pull`), shared with the workspace Overview's Claimable list; the board adds the seat (`viewerCanClaim`). The card root is the kanban's `role="button"`, so a nested button would be interactive-in-interactive: `KanbanBoard` gains `renderItemActions`, drawn by `KanbanColumn` as a sibling in the same `listitem` (own tab stop, no drag, no activation; `Button`'s `scale()` press). The ticket modal gains a filled Claim ticket (`TicketView` `onClaim?` · `claimBusy?`). Not optimistic: the card moves when the server's claimed card returns. A payout refusal for the viewer keeps an InlineNotice with "Verify and set up payouts" on the board; a needs-funding answer re-reads the card. **(C) THE DRAG.** `isClaimDrop` (unclaimed card → In Progress lane) routes a provider's drop to the claim; the client's identical drop is told a freelancer starts it by claiming; a personal project's owner keeps the ordinary move. **(D) AMENDS #169.** `FundingStatus`'s Start work is offered only on a CLAIMED ticket: on an unassigned one it was a `move_ticket` the escrow sync must refuse, and the viewer there is a would-be claimant, not the payee. **FLAGS:** (a) the board cannot pre-check capacity — `ProjectParty` carries no user id, so the viewer's held load is unknown client-side and a cap breach is learnt from the refusal; (b) the payout fix link shows on the board, not inside the ticket modal (the modal toasts the sentence); (c) not verified in a browser or against the live database in this change — unit tests cover the mapper, the stub and the pure rules. | root `CLAUDE.md` §2/§3 gates 11–12 · `packages/types/projects/{board,workspace}.ts` · `packages/backend/services/projects/{live-claim,claim.test,ProjectBackendService,write-store}.ts` · `apps/web/routes/api/projects/ticket/claim.ts` · `packages/ui/kanban/**` · `apps/web/features/projects/{islands/ProjectBoard.island.tsx,components/ticket/TicketView.tsx,core/{BoardService,board-model,board-model.test}.ts}` · `documentation/{design-system/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md,database/projects/Functions.md}` · Decisions #94 / #165 / #169 / #190 / #196 / #197 |

| 198 | **Fair Exit reaches the UI — a reviewer-only "End Stage (Fair Exit)" with a server-computed tier preview; the exit closes the stage's remaining tickets and tells both sides (2026-10-13).** **(A) THE GAP.** `POST /api/projects/:id/stages/:stageId/cancel` → `projects.cancel_stage_fair_exit` → `finance.fn_fair_exit_release` existed (25/50/75 tiers, the fee only on the earned share, the remainder refunded as `fair_exit_refund`, review authority only) but nothing in the app called it (`SettlementService.cancelStageFairExit` had no island caller). After an exit every ticket kept its status (cards stayed In Progress / Review on a `cancelled` stage), only ticket assignees were notified (not team-split recipients, not the client), and the reported `freelancer_paid_cents` summed `finance.transactions` — which has no row for a payout still in its pending window since #191 — so it read 0 for every payout, and missed a prepaid refund (`escrow_refund`). **(B) ONE QUOTE (00001150 / 00001200).** `finance.fn_fair_exit_split(amount, bp, fee_bp)` (IMMUTABLE) is the per-escrow arithmetic, now used by `fn_fair_exit_release` (its prepaid-refund block, #197, untouched). `projects.fn_fair_exit_quote(stage, tier)` (internal) applies it to every escrow the door would settle — a ticket holding a prepaid escrow refunded whole — and answers `freelancer_paid_cents` / `fee_cents` / `client_refunded_cents` / `prepaid_cents` / `held_cents`. `projects.preview_stage_fair_exit(project, stage)` (`authenticated`, review authority) returns the quote at all three tiers plus `exitable` / `blocked` (`settled` · `nothing_held`, the two refusals) and `tickets_to_cancel`. `cancel_stage_fair_exit` locks the stage, then the tickets it will settle in id order, takes the same quote, runs the split, and reports the quote — so the confirmation's figures are exactly what moves. **(C) THE EXIT CLOSES THE WORK (PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT §2 Stage settlement, §3.6 In progress).** Each ticket still standing on the stage (not `completed` / `cancelled` / `reported_hidden`) lands `cancelled` — the existing terminal `ticket_status`, no bespoke status — with its assignee kept for the record and a `ticket_history` row (`reason: fair_exit`, `tier`); no escrow moves on that write (`trg_ticket_escrow_sync` does nothing on `cancelled`, and the split already settled every held escrow). A paid revision round still `awaiting_payment` on the stage is cancelled as moot. **(D) BOTH SIDES NOTIFIED.** `stage_cancelled` (`stage.cancelled`): every payee and team-split recipient the split paid plus the stage's assignees (`fn_notify_payees`), and the project owner plus the reviewer who ended it, with the quote, currency and tickets closed in the payload. **(E) API, SERVICE, UI.** `GET /api/projects/:id/stages/:stageId/fair-exit` → `ProjectBackendService.previewStageFairExit` → `previewFairExitRow`; `FairExitPreviewSchema` / `FairExitQuoteSchema` carry minor units plus server-formatted labels (`formatMoney`), so the island does no money math; live only (`503` on the stub — fixture figures would promise a settlement nothing could match). `StageExitedSchema` gains `ticketsCancelled`, `feeCents`, `currency`; the success notice names the money that moved. In the Submissions explorer — the stage settlement surface; no stage Finance UI exists, and §3 gate 10 (conversion lane) governs entity-view routes only — `WorkflowActions.endStage` offers **End Stage (Fair Exit)** to a reviewer whenever a stage is in scope (not tied to a unit: ending early is for a stage that never reached an acceptance), opening `FairExitDialog` (DESIGN_SYSTEM §C.1): tier switch, three figures, consequences, a disabled confirm with the reason when the door would refuse. **(F) VERIFICATION.** `supabase/probes/fair_exit_settlement.sql` (38 checks) on a private stack built from the working tree (every migration and seed): the split arithmetic and its truncation; the preview's three tiers on the seeded team stage (25 % → 1,068,750 / 56,250 / 3,375,000; 50 % → 2,137,500 / 112,500 / 2,250,000; 75 % → 3,206,250 / 168,750 / 1,125,000) moving nothing; the 50 % exit reporting exactly the previewed figures, each escrow's net share credited once (pending) and its unearned half refunded, the stage and both tickets `cancelled` with history rows and assignees kept, split recipients, both assignees and the owner notified, a second exit refused; a payee refused preview and exit; a prepaid ticket refunded whole at 75 %; `nothing_held` blocking; tier 40 refused; grants. `approval_settlement` (37), `escrow_settlement_authority` (25), `stage_funding_prepaid` (8) and `function_grants` (10) still pass; `escrow_release_concurrency` passes every Fair Exit race and fails the 4 release / approve scenarios on its own ledger-only count (#191 flag (e), unrelated). Unit: `live-settlement.test.ts` (mappers + SQL key contract + grants), `fair-exit-model.test.ts`, `submission-access.test.ts` (`endStage`). **FLAGS for a human:** (a) the shared local DB has none of this (nor #188 / #191 / #194 / #197): the DDL was not applied to it — the new door depends on #194's `fn_notify_payees` — so the dialog answers with an error there until `deno task db:reset`; (b) a multi-stage ticket standing on the ended stage is cancelled outright, its later required stages never run — whether it should instead move on unfunded is a product call; (c) a stage with nothing held cannot be ended this way (the door refuses, the dialog says so) — closing an unfunded stage stays with the stage tools; (d) the control renders on any stage in scope, settled ones included (the explorer carries no stage status outside an open review, and Approve Stage has the same shape); the preview says "already settled" — gating it on stage status would be a new Dev Context axis; (e) the client-side notification reaches the owner and the acting reviewer, not every billing member of the client business (as #194 (b)); (f) the time-based tier of `finance-model.md` §3 is still not wired — the reviewer chooses; (g) the brief's "depends on M1" names no milestone the docs define — taken as #194's settlement path, which is in the tree; (h) the dialog was not exercised in a browser (the preview has no stub, and the shared DB lacks the DDL). AMENDS PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT §2 (Stage settlement) and §3.6 (In progress). | |
