| 201 | **Session cookies carry `Secure` on any HTTPS request, and "Keep me signed in" decides the refresh cookie's lifetime (2026-10-13).** MVP flow audit rows 1.2 and 1.6. **(A) THE DEFECTS.** `apps/web/utils/auth-cookies.ts` appended `Secure` only when `DENO_ENV === "production"`, so an HTTPS staging deploy without that variable shipped the `sb-*` session cookies, the PKCE verifier and the pending-verification cookie without it. Separately, `LoginSchema.remember` (the sign-in form's "Keep me signed in") reached `AuthBackendService.authenticate` and was dropped, so every session got a 30-day refresh cookie. Reproduced before the fix: a login over `https://` with `DENO_ENV` unset and `remember: false` answered `sb-refresh-token=…; Max-Age=2592000` with no `Secure`. **(B) SECURE.** Every cookie builder now takes the request it answers (`sessionSetCookies(req, tokens)`, `sessionClearCookies(req)`, `pendingVerificationSetCookie(req, …)`, `oauthStoreCookies(req, …)`, and `toAuthResponse(req, result)` in `features/auth/core/respond.ts`). `isSecureRequest(req)` is true in production, for an `https:` request URL, or when the first hop of `X-Forwarded-Proto` is `https` (a TLS-terminating proxy). Plain `http://localhost` stays insecure, so local development keeps working. **(C) LIFETIME.** `SessionTokens.remember?: boolean` (`ServiceResult.ts`): `authenticate` sets it from the input (default `false`, matching `LoginSchema`). `true` gives the 30-day refresh cookie. `false` gives a refresh cookie with no `Max-Age` (browser-session lifetime) plus an HttpOnly, browser-session `pj_session_transient=1` flag. A renewal (`POST /api/auth/refresh`, `ensureSession`, `renewSession`) leaves `remember` unset and inherits the request's flag, so a transient session stays transient and a remembered one keeps its 30 days. A sign-in with `remember: true` clears a flag still present, and sign-out clears it. A browser with no flag is a remembered session, so sessions minted before this change keep their 30 days. The access cookie keeps its `expires_in` `Max-Age` in both modes, because the `(dashboard)` guard renews when it lapses. **(D) TESTS.** `apps/web/utils/auth-cookies.test.ts` (15): `Secure` by scheme, forwarded proto and production; both lifetimes; inheritance across the refresh route and `ensureSession`; the flag cleared on a remembered sign-in and on logout; the login route forwarding `remember`. `respond.test.ts` updated for the new signature. AMENDS SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE §Sessions & Google OAuth and PRODUCT_SPEC §Authentication surfaces. **FLAGS for a human:** (a) `X-Forwarded-Proto` is trusted from any client, with no trusted-proxy list. A forged `https` can only ADD `Secure`, so the sender only loses its own cookies over plain http. (b) Browsers that restore the previous session ("continue where you left off") also restore session cookies, so `remember: false` is best-effort. (c) After a restart the access cookie can outlive the browser session by up to its `expires_in` (about an hour). With no refresh cookie, the session then ends at the first renewal. (d) Google OAuth, SSO, the confirmation link and `/verify` have no remember choice: they inherit the browser's current flag, normally remembered. (e) An API caller that omits `remember` gets a transient session (the `LoginSchema` default). The form's checkbox defaults to on. (f) `documentation/architecture/MVP_FLOW_AUDIT.md`, which the brief cites, exists only on `origin/claude/adoring-bohr-hdy9qt`, not on `main`. |
| 208 | **Age policy v2: a tiered classification grounded in COPPA, UK DPA 2018 / the ICO Children's Code, GDPR Art. 8, minors' contract law and Stripe's 18+ terms — under 13 blocked with nothing retained, 13–17 restricted under one rule set, 18+ full; personal projects open to every account; the guardian model replaced by an 18+ proxy model that stays disabled (2026-10-13, at the owner's direction).** SUPERSEDES #192(F) (21+ guardian ID check and consent form). RESOLVES #192 flags (a) scheduling, (b) Teams in the database, (c) the Personal label and (f) the guardian age band. **(A) UNDER 13.** No record: email sign-ups are refused before any write (`JoinSchema` + `AuthBackendService.refuseUnderMinimum`), and an OAuth identity — which exists before `/join` asks — is deleted by `public.discard_underage_signup(dob, zone)` (the caller's own, profile-less identity, under-13 date only), after which `/api/auth/join` clears its session cookies; nothing about the attempt is logged. The browser session is locked: `SessionKeys.JOIN_AGE_LOCK` (the join island shows only the minimum-age message) and the data-less HttpOnly `pj_join_age_lock` cookie (the route refuses further individual sign-ups). **(B) 13–17, ONE RULE SET** (the mid-minor 13–15 / upper-minor 16–17 tiers are labels only; EU national consent ages are not applied separately). No commerce (#190). No payout account (`finance.record_payout_account` asserts an adult) and no wallet: `org.trg_workspace_member_adult` refuses a minor on `team_members`, `business_members`, `enterprise_members` and `org_invitations`, and `create_workspace` / `respond_to_workspace_invitation` assert an adult — the owner chose to block Teams and Businesses entirely over the proxy model. Scheduling: `scheduling.trg_schedule_age_gate` refuses published hours and accepted calls, `fn_schedule_is_public` never answers true for a minor, `request_discovery_call` and `projects.reserve_seat` assert an adult; the app drops the hours links, the Availability tab and route, Settings → Scheduling & calls (`adultOnly`) and booking on availability pages, and mirrors the booking routes. Privacy by default: `search.record_view` records nothing, `trg_interest_events_minor` drops interest events and `fn_log_query` logs a minor's search anonymously; `get_profile_view` and `org.profiles_index` mask a minor's city, country and time zone from all but the owner. DM shielding: `comms.trg_dm_shield` on `dm_participants` and `dm_messages` lets a minor share a direct thread only with mutual follows (an unfollow ends it; `DM_SHIELD_REFUSAL`). Unlisted only: `trg_users_public_minor_visibility` refuses `public`, and both visibility controls drop the option. The setup ring marks `working_hours` not applicable (`not_applicable_keys`, score over 80) and never suggests `publish_hours` or `become_partner` to a minor. **(C) PERSONAL PROJECTS FOR EVERYONE.** `CreateProject.personal` → `create_project` reads `is_personal`; the create modal offers "Personal project" to any account (forced and explained for a minor, withheld from the hire-from-profile flow) and drops the price fields when chosen; a personal draft reads **Personal** in the portfolio, the card's accessible name and the overview header (`statusLabelFor`, `ProjectSummary.personal`). **(D) PROXY MODEL — DISABLED.** PRODUCT_SPEC records that a minor working within a Team or Business needs an adult (18+, not 21) holding the verified vault, executing the contracts and carrying the liability, and that raw guardian IDs are never stored for child freelancing outside an enterprise entity proxy; none of it is built. Probe: `supabase/probes/age_policy_v2.sql` (18 checks), with `personal_projects.sql` and `age_guardrails.sql` still green. FLAGS (a) EU national digital-consent ages (14 Austria/Italy, 15 France, 16 Germany/Netherlands) are not applied — every 13–15-year-old is treated alike, which a lawyer should confirm for EU sign-ups; (b) web-vitals telemetry still carries `user_id` (performance, not behaviour) and `analytics.events` records business events by actor — a human should decide whether either counts as profiling; (c) the location and local-time switches in Settings still render for a minor though the database masks both — they could be hidden; (d) an existing direct thread between two adults is unaffected, and the shield only bites once a participant is a minor, which today cannot happen; (e) the 13-year-old birthday lock relies on a session cookie and session storage, so a new browser or private window reopens the form — by design, since retaining anything longer would breach the no-retention rule. | `supabase/migrations/{00001010,00001020,00001040,00001100,00001155,00001230,00001300,00001400,00001510,00001520,00001800,00001810,00001840,00001860,00002510,00003001}` · `supabase/probes/age_policy_v2.sql` · `packages/types/{auth/age,org/onboarding,projects/{create,summary}}.ts` · `packages/backend/{core/age-gate,services/{auth/AuthBackendService,messaging/{live-writes,live-conversation-writes},projects/{live-writes,live-queries,live-overview}}}.ts` · `apps/web/features/{auth/{core/{age-lock,wizardStore},components/StepForm,islands/JoinExperience.island},shell/{core/{restricted-surfaces,setup-actions},islands/UserActions.island},settings/*,profile/{components/OwnerNav,islands/ProfileEditor.island},projects/{components/{ProjectCreateModal,ProjectCard,portfolio/ProjectPortfolio},core/portfolio-model}}` · `apps/web/routes/{api/auth/join,api/services/{contact-action,book-session,reserve-seat},api/profile/[handle]/availability,[handle]/{_layout,availability,edit/index,edit/availability}}` · PRODUCT_SPEC §Age guardrails (rewritten) · PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT §3.5 · SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE §Authentication & Identity · `documentation/database/{org,projects,comms,scheduling,search,finance}/*` |
| 205 | **The `sb-access-token` cookie is verified, not trusted by presence (2026-10-13). CLOSES the verification TODO of Decisions #14 / #16 / #46; RESOLVES #14's `pj_auth_session` flag.** **(A) THE DEFECT.** The global middleware set `isAuthenticated` from cookie presence and `ensureSession` admitted any present access cookie, so a tampered, foreign or expired token reached `(dashboard)` pages (then failed every RLS read for up to an hour) and was bounced off `/login` to `/home` — reproduced against `HEAD` 14937cd with a payload-rewritten ES256 token: the dashboard rendered, `/login` answered 302 → `/home`. **(B) THE VERIFIER** (`packages/backend/core/access-token.ts`, `createAccessTokenVerifier`). The key is chosen strictly by the token's `alg`, so neither can stand in for the other: `ES256` / `RS256` against the project's public JWKS (`{SUPABASE_URL}/auth/v1/.well-known/jwks.json`, by `kid`); `HS256` against `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET` (placeholder = absent); `none` and anything else refused. Then `exp` (required, 30 s leeway) and a user session's claims — `sub` and `aud` `authenticated` — which refuses a hosted project's legacy anon / service-role key, itself an HS256 JWT signed with the same secret. The local CLI stack signs user tokens `ES256` (verified against the running stack: a real token passes, a payload-rewritten one fails on signature). JWKS cached 10 min (single-flight); an unknown `kid` refetches at most once per 30 s, so a rotation is picked up and a spray of made-up kids costs one fetch; a failed refetch keeps serving the keys held; a verifier that never obtained keys answers `unavailable`; each fetch is capped at 3 s. No new dependency (`djwt` + WebCrypto). **(C) THE SEAM.** `AuthBackendService.verifyAccessToken(token)` → `ServiceResult<{ verified }>`: `ok` admits, `401` refuses (`details.reason`), `503` = keys unobtainable — the `refreshSession` convention (#193). Stub mode (auth backend not live, outside `DENO_ENV=production`) answers `ok` with `verified: false`: `dev:mock` and stub sessions are opaque or hand-made tokens with no issuer to check. Production always verifies, whatever the gate says. **(D) THE MIDDLEWARES.** `session.ts` `verifyAccessCookie(req)` runs the check once per request (a `WeakMap` on the `Request`). The global middleware stamps `isAuthenticated` / `userContext` through `resolveRequestViewer`: signed in only on a valid token, otherwise the guest context — so the `(public)/(auth)` guard bounces only a VALID session. `ensureSession`: valid → fast path; invalid or expired + refresh cookie → the existing renewal (refused → clear + `/login?redirectTo=`; outage → `503`, cookies kept); invalid + no refresh cookie → clear both cookies, unauthenticated (the dashboard redirects, `[handle]` / `(standalone)` / `/explore` append the clears and render as a guest); keys unobtainable → `unavailable` (`503` in the dashboard). No loop: the auth-screen guard bounces exactly what the dashboard admits, and `safeRedirect` refuses auth paths. `/api/auth/verification-status` reads the verified `ctx.state.isAuthenticated`; `hasSessionCookie` and `CookieKeys.AUTH_SESSION_TOKEN` (`pj_auth_session`, never set) are deleted. **(E) ENV.** The contract's unread `JWT_SECRET` is renamed `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET` (`serverEnv().supabaseJwtSecret`, `.env.example`). No schema change. **(F) TESTS.** `core/access-token.test.ts` (13: ES256 · RS256 · HS256 with and without the secret · rewritten payload · foreign key · `none` / unknown alg / garbage · HS256 signed with the public key · expiry + leeway · anon-key and subject-less tokens · JWKS reuse, rotation, kid-spray throttle, outage, stale keys on a failed refetch) and `utils/session-verify.test.ts` (10, the real guards with a stubbed JWKS `fetch`: valid · tampered without / with a refresh cookie · expired with a refresh cookie (renewed / refused) · expired without · keys unobtainable → 503 · the auth-screen guard on valid vs tampered / expired · a stub-mode opaque session still admitted). **FLAGS for a human:** (a) local verification cannot see revocation — an access token whose session was revoked elsewhere passes until its `exp` (≤ 1 h); its refresh token is dead, and RLS is the backstop in between; closing it needs a per-request GoTrue `getUser` or a session-id check against `auth.sessions`; (b) `deno task dev` with `AUTH_BACKEND_LIVE=true` now refuses forged or opaque cookies — hand-made JWT probes work only under `dev:mock` or with the auth backend off; (c) `/api/*` routes that fall back to `readCookies(req)[SB_ACCESS_COOKIE]` (`api-session.ts`, `user/me.ts`, `user/setup.ts`, `user/preferences.ts`, `view/core/booking-actor.ts`) still hand the raw cookie to services rather than the verified state — PostgREST re-verifies it, so data stays safe, but they do not consult the verifier; (d) `CookieKeys.AUTH_REFRESH_TOKEN` (`pj_auth_refresh`) is equally unused and misnamed — left in place, outside this brief; (e) the brief named `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET` "per the env contract", which listed `JWT_SECRET` — renamed rather than duplicated, since nothing read it; (f) the brief's `documentation/architecture/MVP_FLOW_AUDIT.md` exists only on `origin/claude/adoring-bohr-hdy9qt` (5c6dfa97), not on this branch; its row 1.7 "Residual" is what this closes. No new Dev Context Switcher axis: the mode derives from the existing `USE_MOCKS` / `AUTH_BACKEND_LIVE` gates. | `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §Backend (core) · §Sessions & Google OAuth · §Security · Environment Variable Contract · `ROUTING.md` · `packages/backend/core/{access-token,env}.ts` · `packages/backend/services/auth/AuthBackendService.ts` · `apps/web/utils/{session,auth-cookies,user-context,storage-keys}.ts` · `apps/web/routes/{_middleware.ts,(dashboard)/_middleware.ts,(public)/(auth)/_middleware.ts,api/auth/verification-status.ts}` · `packages/types/auth/user-context.ts` · `.env.example` |
| 200 | **A succeeded card payment always lands in a ledger row, or is flagged (2026-10-13).** MVP audit 3.3 / §2.5, work order R4 (#186 flag (f)), built on M3 (#186). **(A) THE DEFECTS.** `finance.settle_card_payment` already ran the escrow lock and the checkout order in subtransactions after the credit (a refusal rolled back only that step), but: a lock or order refused after the credit left the payment `succeeded`, indistinguishable from an applied one; an `unmatched` settlement (no payment bound to the PaymentIntent yet, or an amount / currency that disagreed) COMPLETED its `fn_claim_stripe_event` claim and the webhook answered 200, so every Stripe retry was replayed and never credited — reachable in practice, because a recurring deposit's off-session intent is confirmed before `bind_scheduled_payment` binds it; a replay answered without saying whether a ledger row existed; and the table allowed `status = 'succeeded'` with a NULL `transaction_id`. Reproduced first: `supabase/probes/card_settlement_ledger.sql` failed 9 of its 13 checks on the unfixed schema (the early success and the mismatch kept their claims, a pre-existing completed claim swallowed its settlement, the NULL-ledger `succeeded` row stored, `settled_unapplied` / the view / the release function absent). **(B) STATUS.** `finance.inbound_payment_status` gains `settled_unapplied` (between `succeeded` and `failed`, edited in place in 00000004): the money arrived and is credited, the lock or order it was for was refused after the credit. `inbound_payments.unapplied_reason` ("The escrow could not be held: …" / "The order could not be placed: …"; the raw detail stays in `lock_error` / `order_error`). CHECKs: `succeeded_at` ⇔ settled (`succeeded` or `settled_unapplied`); `transaction_id IS NOT NULL` ⇔ settled (was one-directional — a settled payment without its ledger row is now unrepresentable); `settled_unapplied` ⇔ `unapplied_reason`. **(C) SETTLEMENT.** The wallet is read `FOR UPDATE` and confirmed by `fn_ensure_wallet` (its owner, the payment's currency) before `fn_wallet_credit`; a missing wallet or a credit that left no ledger row raises (`PW404` / `PL404`) and rolls back with the claim. A refused lock or order sets `settled_unapplied` and the payer's `wallet.topup_succeeded` notification is titled "Payment received — the funds are in your wallet". An `unmatched` settlement releases its claim (`finance.fn_release_stripe_event`, internal, revoked from every role) instead of completing it; a mismatch also writes an `ops.log_entries` `error` row (`scope = 'finance.settle_card_payment'`) and a WARNING per delivery. A completed claim is answered with the payment's `transaction_id`; one whose payment has no ledger row is released and re-processed (the payment row's own status, not the claim, prevents a second credit). Every completed outcome carries `transaction_id`, `status`, `unapplied_reason`. `record_card_payment_failure` and `bind_scheduled_payment` treat `settled_unapplied` as settled. **(D) WEBHOOK.** `requireLedgerRow` (`stripe-webhook.ts`) throws unless an `applied` / `replayed` settlement carries a `transaction_id`, so `handleStripeWebhook` answers 500 and Stripe redelivers; a `payment_intent.succeeded` without a uuid `metadata.projective_payment_id` (every Projective intent carries one, `paymentIntentParams`) is planned `ignore` and acknowledged. AMENDS the "2xx-on-unknown" rule (SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE §Stripe) for settlements only. **(E) OPS VIEW.** `finance.v_ops_unledgered_payments` (00003004, service role only, 00003005): every settled payment with no ledger row (`no_ledger_row`) or a linked row that is not its `credit` of `amount_received_cents` into its wallet referencing it (`ledger_row_mismatch`). **(F) TYPES & UI.** `InboundPaymentStatus` + `SETTLED_PAYMENT_STATUSES` / `isSettledPaymentStatus`, `InboundPaymentSchema.unappliedReason`; `funding-state.ts`, `PaymentsService.waitForSettlement` / `waitForLock` and `PaymentBackendService`'s replayed-key guard read settled through the helper, so the browser behaves exactly as it did for `succeeded` + a failed lock (the existing `fundingState` dev axis already renders that Failed state — no new simulatable axis). Tests: `payments.contract.test.ts` (SQL settled set = SSOT, reason CHECK, release grant), `stripe-webhook.test.ts` (foreign intent ignored; `requireLedgerRow`), `funding-state.test.ts` (`settled_unapplied` → Failed). **(G) VERIFIED.** A private Supabase stack built from `fix/ledger-append-only` + this change applied every migration and seed from scratch; on it `card_settlement_ledger.sql` passed all 13 checks and `ledger_immutability`, `single_ticket_purchase`, `escrow_settlement_authority`, `paid_revisions`, `cohort_session_isolation` and `comms_column_grants` passed. **FLAGS for a human:** (a) **the shared local DB is NOT migrated** — an enum value cannot be added inside a rolled-back dry run and the shared DB is not reset by an agent; nothing breaks meanwhile (the TS reads `unapplied_reason` from `select("*")` with a null fallback), but the new behaviour is live only after `deno task db:reset`. (b) A mismatched settlement now 5xxs for Stripe's whole retry window (about three days, one ops row per delivery), then Stripe stops and the money sits in the platform balance uncredited; there is no alert channel beyond `ops.log_entries` (as #186(b)), and an intent Stripe gave up on is invisible to the view (no payment row settled) — a sweep reconciling `processing` payments against Stripe is the follow-up. (c) `settled_unapplied` records what SETTLEMENT did; it does not flip back when the browser later places the same order under its key or the payer funds the stage from the wallet. (d) `fn_ensure_wallet` in settlement is defensive: `wallet_id` is `NOT NULL … ON DELETE RESTRICT`, so the wallet always exists; the call refuses a payment whose wallet's currency disagrees. (e) The foreign-intent `ignore` is a deliberate exception to "2xx only with a ledger row"; a future Projective intent created without `paymentIntentParams` would be ignored, not retried. (f) The view is empty by construction once the CHECK holds; it is not yet read by the nightly reconciliation (#186 / R2). (g) Not from this change: at the time, `deno task check` failed on `PaymentBackendService.ts:764` (a peer's in-flight `destinationMethodId`) and `deno task lint` on an unused `plural` in `BookingBackendService.ts`. | `supabase/migrations/{00000004_enums_domains,00000017_tables_finance,00001230_functions_finance_stripe,00001240_functions_finance_money_movement,00002510_permissions_function_grants,00003004_views_finance_integrations_analytics,00003005_permissions_view_grants}.sql` · `supabase/probes/card_settlement_ledger.sql` · `packages/types/finance/{payments.ts,payments.contract.test.ts}` · `packages/backend/services/finance/{stripe-webhook.ts + test,funding-state.ts + test,live-payments.ts,PaymentBackendService.ts}` · `apps/web/features/payments/core/PaymentsService.ts` · `documentation/database/finance/{Tables,Functions,Policies}.md` · `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` §Inbound card payment · `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §Integration Blueprints → Stripe · `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Money in and out · Decisions #125 / #126 / #153 / #166 / #186 / #199 |
| 207 | **Sign-up seeds meaningful preferences, and enterprise owners get theirs (2026-10-13).** MVP audit rows 1.12 / 1.15 (`documentation/architecture/MVP_FLOW_AUDIT.md`, present only on `origin/claude/adoring-bohr-hdy9qt`). `org.seed_user_preferences` inserted `user_id` only, so every account began `en-GB` · `GBP` · `system` · `auto` and every `comms.notification_prefs` row `UTC` with no locale, whatever the person's browser said; and an enterprise owner — admin-created with only `objective: "enterprise"`, provisioned by `public.create_enterprise`, which never writes `org.users_public` — triggered neither seeder and had no preferences row at all. Reproduced first by `supabase/probes/signup_preferences.sql` (7 failures against the shared DB). **(A) THE HINTS.** `/join` adds `locale` and `displayCurrency` to both payloads from `navigator.languages` through `preferenceHints` (new `packages/types/org/locales.ts`): the first OFFERED locale (exact, or through its likely region — `fr` → `fr-FR`), and the currency of the first tag that STATES a region (`displayCurrencyForRegion` in `finance/fx.ts`: the offered currency of that territory, `EUR` across the euro area); a bare language never picks a currency. The join schema re-validates both and DROPS an unsupported value rather than refusing the sign-up — a hint is never a reason to fail. `AuthBackendService.provisionAccount` writes them as `locale` / `display_currency` into the individual metadata and into the enterprise owner's `user_metadata`. The browser zone is #192's `timeZone` → `timezone` (projective-com-17, not part of this change). **(B) THE DATABASE KEEPS ONLY WHAT IT OFFERS.** New reference table `org.supported_locales` (23 tags, `00000011`, seeded in `00005050`, RLS on, readable by everyone, writes revoked) — the brief's "reference tables in 00005050" had no locale table, only `finance.fx_rates`. `org.fn_supported_locale` matches ignoring case and `_`/`-`; `org.fn_supported_display_currency` accepts the platform base or a code with seeded rates both ways (the `DISPLAY_CURRENCIES` rule). `org.fn_seed_user_preferences` inserts the row and, only when it is new, applies the supported hints, so the column defaults stay the one source of the fallbacks and a row already chosen is never touched. `comms.fn_seed_notification_prefs` does the same for `timezone` (the profile's zone, else the hint, and only one Postgres resolves — quiet hours and digests run `AT TIME ZONE` it) and `locale`. **(C) THE OAUTH PAYLOAD.** `complete_onboarding` passes its payload as an argument and it never reaches `auth.users`, so a trigger on `org.users_public` could not see it. `provision_user_profile` now publishes the payload for the transaction (`set_config('projective.signup_meta', {user_id, meta}, true)`) and `org.fn_signup_meta(user)` reads it when the user id matches, else `raw_user_meta_data`. **(D) ENTERPRISE OWNERS.** New trigger `on_enterprise_owner_created` (`AFTER INSERT ON org.enterprise_members WHEN role = 'owner'`) → `org.seed_owner_preferences()` seeds the owner's `org.user_preferences` (keyed on `auth.users`). Human ruling (2026-10-13): no personal profile is created for the owner (#87 flag (b) stays open) and the `notification_prefs` FK is not retargeted — it is seeded only for an owner who already has a profile. An existing person's chosen preferences survive creating an enterprise. Tests: `org/locales.test.ts`, `org/locales.contract.test.ts` (the seed list and the fx seed pairs against the TS lists), `auth/join-hints.test.ts`, `settings/core/locales.test.ts` (Settings `LOCALES` = `SUPPORTED_LOCALES`). No new simulatable axis, so no Dev Context Switcher control; no lifecycle change. **FLAGGED:** (a) **a profile-less enterprise owner has no `comms.notification_prefs` row** — `notification_prefs`, `notifications`, `notification_deliveries` and `device_tokens` all key onto `org.users_public`, so such an owner can receive no notification at all; resolving it is #87(b). (b) `org.user_preferences.locale` has no FK onto `org.supported_locales` and `PATCH /api/user/preferences` accepts any string ≤ 20, so a Settings write can still store an unoffered locale. (c) The supported list now lives in three places (SQL seed, `SUPPORTED_LOCALES`, the Settings `LOCALES` labels) held together by two tests; the Settings list could derive its tags from the types list. (d) The zone check in `comms.fn_seed_notification_prefs` duplicates #192's `org.fn_valid_time_zone` (uncommitted when this landed); switch to it once #192 merges. (e) A bare `en` resolves to `en-US` (its likely region), so a UK browser that sends only `en` starts in US English while keeping GBP. | `supabase/migrations/{00000011,00001010,00001310,00001810,00002001,00002010,00002520,00005050}` · `supabase/probes/signup_preferences.sql` · `packages/types/org/{locales,mod}.ts` · `packages/types/finance/fx.ts` · `packages/types/auth/join.ts` · `apps/web/features/auth/core/wizardValidation.ts` · `apps/web/routes/api/auth/join.ts` · `AuthBackendService.ts` · `documentation/database/{org,comms}/*` · `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Settings · Decisions #47 / #87 / #192 / #204 |
| 203 | **Scheduled work runs in every environment — one job registry, loud skips, pg_cron + pg_net with Vault bearers, and `deno task cron:run` (2026-10-13).** MVP audit work order R2. RESOLVES #186 flag (a) and #156 flag (h). **(A) THE DEFECT.** Five `cron.schedule` calls sat in guarded `DO` blocks beside their functions (`archive-stale-service-drafts`, `auto-release-session-escrow`, `finance-billing-cycle`, and on the unmerged M3 / M4 branches `finance-reconcile-wallets`, `finance-mature-pending-releases`); a failed registration left only a `NOTICE` in the migration output. The two HTTP sweeps (`POST /api/finance/cron/deposits`, `POST /api/user/cron/erasures`) had no caller at all, and there is no CI. Reproduced first: `supabase/probes/scheduled_jobs.sql` on the shared DB failed check 1 with five sweeps unregistered. The brief's premise that local `supabase start` lacks pg_cron is stale: the shared DB runs pg_cron 1.6.4 (three jobs, 24 succeeded runs), pg_net 0.20.4 is available, and Vault is installed. **(B) THE HUMAN RULING (2026-10-13):** hosted pg_cron + pg_net calling the routes with the bearer from Vault, not an external runner (no hosting doc says otherwise); deposits hourly and erasures daily, with the cadence configurable; pg_net may be installed in `00000002` under the same guard. **(C) THE REGISTRY.** `ops.scheduled_jobs` (00000013) holds every job: `sql` (one `SELECT`) or `http` (`path` + `secret_name`). An AFTER INSERT / UPDATE / DELETE trigger (00001897) applies the row to pg_cron in the same transaction, so **the cadence is configurable in one place**: the seed (`00005060`) sets the default, and `UPDATE ops.scheduled_jobs SET schedule = … / enabled = false` reschedules or pauses a running database (pg_cron refuses an invalid expression with the write, `22023`). The five DO blocks are gone; their functions now point at the registry. M3 / M4 are seeded only `WHERE to_regprocedure(…) IS NOT NULL`, so the seed holds whichever order those branches merge in. **(D) HTTP DISPATCH.** `ops.fn_dispatch_http_job` reads `CRON_APP_URL` and the row's `secret_name` from `vault.decrypted_secrets` (the secret name is also the app's env key) and queues `net.http_post` (120 s timeout); `ops.http_job_dispatches` records each request, and `ops-watch-jobs` (every 10 min) settles it from `net._http_response` (6 h TTL; unanswered after 7 h is reported). Cadences: `finance-recurring-deposits` hourly at :15 (`limit=50`), `account-erasures` daily 04:10 UTC (`limit=100`). **(E) NOTHING SILENT.** Every skip or failure raises a `WARNING` and writes an `ops.log_entries` row (`scope = 'ops.scheduler'`, `client_id = 'scheduler:<job>:<reason>'`): `pg_cron_unavailable` / `schedule_failed` at registration; `pg_net_unavailable` / `vault_unavailable` / `app_url_missing` / `secret_missing` at dispatch (one row a day per job and reason); `dispatch_failed`, `http_failed`, `no_response`, and `run_failed` (a sql job that raised, read from `cron.job_run_details` once per run). `00000002` can only warn, because the `ops` tables do not exist there yet. **(F) MANUAL RUNS.** `deno task cron:run <job>` / `--list` (`scripts/cron-run.ts` → `ScheduledJobsBackendService`): a sql job through `ops.run_job` (service role only, returns the result as jsonb), an http job POSTed to `APP_URL` + path with the bearer from `.env.local`. Types: `@projective/types/ops`. **(G) VERIFIED** on a private stack reset from `main` + this change (the shared DB was not reset): probe checks 1–7 pass (registration, registry ↔ `cron.job`, reschedule / disable / invalid refused, loud unconfigured skips, the queued request and its bearer, settlement of a 404 and a lost response, grants, failed-run reporting); end to end, pg_net reached a host stub from the container with the Vault bearer (deposits 200, a wrong erasures bearer 404 → one `http_failed` row), pg_cron fired the dispatch itself on a one-minute schedule, and `cron:run` ran `finance-billing-cycle` (14 statements), POSTed deposits (200) and refused a placeholder bearer and an unknown job. **FLAGS for a human:** (a) **implemented sweeps that no migration has ever scheduled**: comms `fn_process_queue`, `fn_escalate_unread`, `fn_build_digests` (daily, weekly), `fn_sweep_expired`, `fn_reap_dead_devices`, `fn_compact_delivery_events` (`database/comms/Functions.md` documented them as registered; now marked not registered), `projects.fn_release_expired_claims` (PRODUCT_SPEC's claim-TTL auto-release depends on it) and `projects.fn_resolve_expired_workload_reports`. Registering them starts sending reminders, refunding claims and writing penalties, so it is left for a ruling; each is one registry row. (b) The Vault secrets are provisioned nowhere: until `CRON_APP_URL` and the two bearers exist in an environment, each http job skips and logs once a day. (c) The shared local DB was not migrated (additive DDL only, nothing reads it but `cron:run`); it picks this up on the next `deno task db:reset`. (d) When `fix/ledger-append-only` (M3) and `feat/pending-window` (M4) merge, their own guarded `DO` blocks come back with them; they re-register the same name and cadence (harmless) but should be deleted (the shared working tree already has them removed). (e) A sql job run through `cron:run` goes via PostgREST, so a long job is subject to the gateway's statement timeout; pg_cron's own runs are not. (f) Alerting is still `ops.log_entries` + `WARNING` only, as #186(b); `ops.http_job_dispatches` is never pruned (~30 rows a day). (g) Decision #202's stale-payout sweep registers itself as `finance-sweep-stale-payouts` in its own change (same pattern, guarded on its function). | `supabase/migrations/{00000002_extensions_core,00000013_tables_ops,00001130_functions_projects_stages,00001155_functions_projects_sessions,00001250_functions_finance_auto_fund_billing,00001650_functions_ops_scheduler,00001897_triggers_ops,00002001_policies_enable_rls,00002019_policies_security_ops,00002510_permissions_function_grants,00002520_permissions_table_grants,00004002_indexes_security,00005060_seed_scheduled_jobs}.sql` · `supabase/probes/scheduled_jobs.sql` · `packages/types/ops` · `packages/backend/services/ops` · `scripts/cron-run.ts` · `deno.json` · `.env.example` · `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §Scheduled jobs + Environment Variable Contract · `documentation/database/{ops/*,comms/Functions,projects/Functions,finance/Functions,security/Functions,README}.md` |
| 202 | **The withdrawal lifecycle after the Stripe Transfer (2026-10-13).** MVP audit row 3.8, §2.5 and Phase 0.6 (`documentation/architecture/MVP_FLOW_AUDIT.md`, present only on `origin/claude/adoring-bohr-hdy9qt`). **(A) THE GAPS.** Only `transfer.created` was handled; the seeded `payout.failed` notification had no sender; a transient Stripe error answered 202 and left the payout `pending` and debited with nothing to close it; `destinationId` was accepted by `WithdrawInputSchema`, sent by `WalletDialogs` and dropped in `wallet-actions.ts`; there was no minimum. Reproduced first: `supabase/probes/payout_lifecycle.sql` aborted on the unfixed schema (no minimum), and the planner tests for `transfer.reversed` / `payout.*` did not type-check. **(B) REVERSAL.** `finance.record_transfer_reversed` (processor door) finds the payout by its bound transfer or metadata id, checks the transfer agrees with it, and credits only what is newly reversed (`LEAST(amount_reversed, amount) − reversed_cents`, reason `payout_reversal`), so a replay, a duplicate or an out-of-order lower total credits nothing; a full reversal is the new `payout_status` value `reversed`, a partial one stays `paid`. Person owners get `payout.reversed`. A reversal of, or a `transfer.created` for, a payout already `failed` moves nothing and writes an `ops.log_entries` error (the money left twice). **(C) BANK PAYOUT.** Connect `payout.paid` / `payout.failed` are planned only with `event.account` (a payout without it is the platform's own); the service reads the bank payout's balance transactions on the connected account into the transfers it carried (`payment` → `source_transfer`) and the earlier failed payouts it re-pays (`payout_failure`), and `finance.record_bank_payout` records `bank_status` / `bank_payout_ref` / `bank_failure_code` / `bank_status_at` on the owner's sent payouts. A failure is final for its own bank payout; a later payout that re-pays it marks it paid; nothing moves in the ledger; person owners get `payout.failed` / `payout.paid` (new key). `STRIPE_CONNECT_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (new, optional) signs the Connect destination; the snapshot endpoint verifies against either secret (`verifyStripeEventWithAny`). **(D) STALE SWEEP.** `finance.fn_claim_stale_payouts(p_limit)` leases `pending` payouts older than `payout_stale_minutes` (15) under `SKIP LOCKED` (`swept_at`, `sweep_attempts`); `PaymentBackendService.sweepStalePayouts` finds each transfer by its new group `projective_payout_<id>`, then by scanning the recorded `destination_account`'s transfers for the metadata id, and completes the payout; with none it re-sends under the same idempotency key inside 23 h (Stripe keeps a key 24 h) and calls `fail_payout` after (money back; `fail_payout` now tells person owners `payout.failed`). The decision is the pure `planStalePayout` (`payout-sweep.ts`). `POST /api/finance/cron/payouts` (bearer `FINANCE_CRON_SECRET`, 404 otherwise) is registered as the R2 (#203) job `finance-sweep-stale-payouts`, every 10 minutes, seeded only where the claim function exists. **(E) DESTINATION.** `begin_payout` gains `p_destination_method_id DEFAULT NULL` (one signature): it must be one of the wallet owner's `payout`/`both`, `active` methods by the person-type rule (someone else's reads as missing, `42501`; inactive `PA403`), is recorded on the payout, hashed into the attempt key and stamped on the transfer's metadata. `WithdrawInputSchema.destinationId` is now a uuid. **(F) MINIMUM.** `min_withdrawal_minor` is a per-currency object with a `default` (≈ £10 each, `finance-model.md` §13), read by `finance.fn_min_withdrawal_minor`; `begin_payout` refuses below it (`22023`, "amountMinor: the smallest withdrawal is 10.00 GBP"). **VERIFIED** on a private Supabase stack reset from `14937cd` + this SQL: the probe 32/32 PASS; `function_grants`, `ledger_immutability` and `pending_window` probes clean; `deno task smoke:stripe` all green against stripe-mock and that stack; `deno task check` passes; unit tests pass bar the four pre-existing `theme-engine` failures. **FLAGGED — for a human:** (a) **NEW CONFLICT.** `finance-model.md` §8 "Failed Withdrawal" says the funds "bounce back to their Personal Wallet (Available state)"; with Separate Charges & Transfers a connected account's failed bank payout returns to that account's Stripe balance, not the platform, and the brief asked for a status and a notification. Built as briefed; §8 carries a flag, not a rewrite. Choose: leave the money in the Connect balance (Stripe pays it again once the bank details are fixed), or reverse the transfer on `payout.failed` so (B) credits the wallet. (b) A Transfer cannot target a bank account: the connected account's DEFAULT external account receives Stripe's payout, so `destinationId` is validated, recorded and stamped but does not route money; that needs manual payouts on the connected account. No live path creates payout methods yet (they are seeded only). (c) Only person owners are notified (as `complete_payout` already did); team and business withdrawers are not told of a bank failure or a reversal. (d) Stripe lists balance transactions by payout for AUTOMATIC payouts only; on a manual-payout account `payout.paid` is `unmatched` and `payout.failed` only notifies. (e) The sweep returns the money after 23 h when its scan (≤ 500 of the destination's transfers, from an hour before the payout) finds nothing; a transfer it missed would then mean the money left twice — `record_transfer_created` / `record_transfer_reversed` log that case to ops, but nothing alerts. (f) The minimum figures are placeholders for product sign-off, and no surface shows the minimum before the press (the refusal names it). (g) No Dev Context Switcher axis: no surface renders a payout's status or bank status (the payout history page was retired); the smoke script simulates the lifecycle. Add an axis with the first surface that reads them. (h) The shared local DB has none of this DDL until `deno task db:reset`. (i) The user's commit `d7359f66` ("Bug fixes & gap fills") captured this work mid-flight, including a `record_bank_payout` whose guards were NULL-blind (no fresh payout ever matched) and a catalog mirror missing `payout.paid` / `payout.reversed` (`catalog.contract.test.ts` fails there); branch `fix/payout-lifecycle` carries those fixes, the docs and this row. (j) The audit file is still missing from this branch (#189(b)). | `supabase/migrations/{00000004_enums_domains,00000017_tables_finance,00001230_functions_finance_stripe,00001240_functions_finance_money_movement,00002510_permissions_function_grants,00004005_indexes_finance,00005001_seed_platform_params,00005010_seed_notification_catalog,00005060_seed_scheduled_jobs}.sql` · `supabase/probes/{payout_lifecycle,scheduled_jobs}.sql` · `packages/types/finance/{payments,wallet}.ts` · `packages/types/comms/catalog.ts` · `packages/backend/core/{env,stripe}.ts` · `packages/backend/services/finance/{PaymentBackendService,stripe-webhook,stripe-rails,live-payments,live-wallet,wallet-actions,payout-sweep}.ts` · `apps/web/routes/api/finance/cron/payouts.ts` · `scripts/stripe-smoke.{ts,sql}` · `.env.example` · `documentation/database/{finance/{Tables,Functions,Policies},comms/Tables,security/Tables}.md` · `finance-model.md` §8 / §13 · `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` · `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` · `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` |
| 209 | **"Add to Projects" on a Pipeline listing is one real transaction — `marketplace.instantiate_service_blueprint` — and both booking services read the listing live (2026-10-13).** MVP audit row 2.17 / roadmap 2.3 (`documentation/architecture/MVP_FLOW_AUDIT.md`). AMENDS #80(C) and #108 where they describe the live path. **(A) ONE DOOR, ONE TRANSACTION.** `marketplace.instantiate_service_blueprint(p_blueprint_id, p_intake, p_currency, p_idempotency_key, p_title)` (`00001175`, SECURITY DEFINER) writes, in the caller's personal workspace: the project (`format = 'pipeline'`, `status = 'draft'`, `visibility = 'unlisted'`, `source_blueprint_id`, the listing's brief and currency); one stage per `stage_template` entry, priced at the stage's STANDARD per-ticket price (`priceCents`, else the listing's `ticket_price_cents`, else NULL — never the 0.5× range floor the card prints, which the stub had been copying and now no longer does); one backlog, unassigned ticket per stage at the same price, its checklist seeded from the stage's deliverables; every stage room and the Discussion room; and a `projects.service_instantiations` request row (key, normalised answers, currency). **No escrow, no seat** — a seat only ever comes from an accepted invitation or application (`PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` §Stage assignment), which REPLACES #80(C)'s "assignments parked at `pending_funding`" as a description of instantiation itself: the seller is hired in through the ordinary hire path. **(B) IDEMPOTENT TWICE, UNDER A PER-BUYER ADVISORY LOCK:** the caller's own key first (a replay returns what it created, whatever its status; the same key for another listing is refused 22023), then the caller's live (`draft`/`active`, personal) draft of the listing. `get_service_draft` reads with the same predicate, so the listing CTA and the write agree. **(C) CALLER CHECKS IN THE DOOR:** signed in 28000 · adult (`org.fn_assert_adult`) PM403 · published P0002 · pipeline with stages 22023 · not your own or your team's PU422 · currency PC409 · intake an object ≤ 32 KB, reduced to the listing's field ids. **(D) "REMOVE DRAFT" GOES LIVE TOO** as `marketplace.archive_service_draft` — check-no-escrow and `set_project_status(…, 'archived')` in one transaction — because a live instantiation with a stub archive would be a control that does nothing (§3.11). **(E) LISTINGS READ LIVE.** `resolveListing` (`packages/backend/services/explore/listing-source.ts`) is the one resolver `BookingBackendService` (offer, slots, bookSession, reserveSeat, configure, contact, askQuestion, requestQuote) and `ProjectBackendService.instantiateService` use: with `EXPLORE_BACKEND_LIVE` on it is a fresh anon read of the published listing, blueprint/product, gallery and skills (`readListing`, `live-catalog.ts`) composed through the page's own `composeViewPage`; the process-wide snapshot (`findItem`/`peekCatalog`, up to a TTL stale and served indefinitely on failed reloads) stays only behind `EXPLORE_BACKEND_LIVE=false`, and the in-memory draft store only behind `PROJECTS_BACKEND_LIVE=false`. **VERIFIED** — `supabase/probes/service_instantiation.sql`, 32 checks in one rolled-back transaction against the seed; the live listing read run against the local stack; `live-instantiate.test.ts` pins the projection keys, door parameters and SQLSTATE mapping against the migration text; `hire-from-profile.test.ts` covers the stub path (standard prices, currency refusal, guest, unknown listing, replay, archive). **APPLIED** by the 2026-10-13 reset and re-probed live (32/32); the TS live path (`instantiateBlueprintRow` → PostgREST) has not yet run end to end with a real session. **FLAGGED — a human decides:** (a) a buyer currency other than the listing's is REFUSED, not converted — who carries FX on a seller's committed prices is a business rule; (b) required intake answers are stored, not enforced, at instantiation (the `/view` CTA collects none and the draft commits nobody) — decide whether funding or the hire should enforce them; (c) the seller cannot read the request's answers (owner-only RLS); (d) team/business workspaces are refused (`workspaceId` → 422); (e) `ProjectBackendService`'s feed comment saying `marketplace` is not exposed to PostgREST is stale (`config.toml` exposes it). | `supabase/migrations/{00000015_tables_projects,00001175_functions_marketplace_instantiate,00002001_policies_enable_rls,00002011_policies_projects,00002510_permissions_function_grants,00004003_indexes_projects}.sql` · `supabase/probes/service_instantiation.sql` · `packages/types/services/pipeline.ts` · `packages/backend/services/{explore/listing-source.ts,explore/live-catalog.ts,projects/live-instantiate.ts,projects/ProjectBackendService.ts,booking/BookingBackendService.ts}` · `apps/web/routes/api/services/{instantiate-pipeline,archive-draft}.ts` · `apps/web/features/{view/core/BookingService.ts,profile/components/hire/ServiceDetailModal.tsx}` · `documentation/{flows/ServiceCreation.md,flows/Projects.md,database/marketplace/Functions.md,database/projects/{Tables,Policies}.md,database/README.md}` |
| 210 | **Pipeline escrow semantics — the Chain Reaction holds the same ticket's next stage on approval, from the live balance, per-stage switchable; the column lock is a derived board rule (2026-10-13). Product-owner ruling on audit 2.17 / §2.6(e) (`MVP_FIX_PROMPTS.md` P2).** **(A) THE RULINGS.** Asked four questions, the product owner chose: a "column lock" is *no entry into a work column without held escrow* (not a frozen column while in review); "Ticket B" is *the same ticket's next required stage* (not the next backlog ticket); the hold draws on the *live wallet balance*, short → Needs funding + notify (no pre-authorised budget, no wallet reservation — Decision #165 had already declined a reservation concept); and the chain is a *per-stage toggle, default on*. PRODUCT_SPEC's "Approval of Ticket A … locks Escrow for Ticket B" is re-worded to say so. **(B) WHAT ALREADY EXISTED.** Every approval — an accepted submission and `approve_stage` — already settles through `fn_settle_ticket_stage` → `projects.force_complete_stage`, which released the stage, advanced the ticket and re-held (`PF402` → `todo` / Needs funding). The audit missed it because nothing named it a chain. **(C) THE TOGGLE.** `projects.project_stages.chain_reaction boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true`, read on the RECEIVING stage (the hold is that stage's money and its lock trigger). Off, `force_complete_stage` moves the ticket there `todo` with `payment_status = 'unpaid'` (Draft) — NOT `partially_released`, because every "fundable" gate (`fund_ticket`, `fund_stage`, `fn_stage_funding_need`) accepts only `unpaid` / `needs_funding`, and the payment axis describes the CURRENT stage, exactly as `fn_mark_needs_funding` already overwrites it on a refusal. Edited in the stage's Advanced settings ("Fund on arrival", `SetupStageFields`, not offered on the first stage, a session or a personal project) through the existing setup PATCH (owner RLS on `project_stages`, no new RPC); read-only in the StageInspector. `StageSetupSchema.chainReaction` has NO Zod default on purpose: `UpdateProjectSchema` is it `.partial()`, and Zod 4 applies a default through `.partial()`, so a save that omitted the field would have switched it back on. **(D) OUTCOMES + NOTICES.** One `ticket_history` row per outcome (`chain_held` · `chain_needs_funding` · `chain_off`, read by the board as `funding` entries naming the stage); the refused path tags `fn_mark_needs_funding`'s DETAIL `trigger: chain_reaction`; a wait (refused or off) tells the assignee a new catalog key `ticket.awaiting_funding` (work · high · in_app/push/email); a hold already tells them "Funded — you can start". **(E) THE COLUMN LOCK IS DERIVED, NOT STORED.** No `board_columns` table exists (the audit's `projects.board_columns.locked_by_escrow` was invented) and board columns map 1:1 to `ticket_status`, so the lock is `workColumnLocked(card, toStatus)` in `@projective/types/projects`: a CLAIMED ticket, entering `claimed`/`in_progress`/`in_review` from outside work, not personal, whose `funding.canStart` is false — the #165 gate on `trg_ticket_escrow_sync` shown before the drop. An unclaimed ticket is never locked: its drop is the claim, which is the lock trigger. `@projective/ui/kanban` gains `itemDropRefusal(item, column) → reason | null` (locked column styling, reason line, announcement); the board island also re-checks in `onItemMove`. **(F) DEV CONTEXT SWITCHER — NO NEW AXIS.** The lock is derived from the funding state, so the existing **Funding state** axis now drives it too (the island reads it through `previewFunding`); the toggle is real stage data edited in the UI, not a simulated axis. **VERIFIED** — `supabase/probes/chain_reaction.sql`, 23 checks rolled back (held / refused / off / gate / `fund_ticket` / `approve_stage`), plus `approval_settlement`, `escrow_settlement_authority`, `fair_exit_settlement`, `stage_funding_prepaid` unchanged (108 checks); APPLIED by the user-approved 2026-10-13 reset and re-probed live (23/23). `column-lock.test.ts` (7) and `live-board.test.ts` (chain history, stage flag). **FLAGGED — for a human:** (a) the chained hold keeps the ticket's CURRENT assignee as payee in the next stage unless a team holds that stage — a different freelancer staffing stage 2 is not re-routed (pre-existing in `force_complete_stage`); (b) `live-board.ts` `escrowHeldOf` reads `partially_released` as held — harmless to the lock (it reads `funding.canStart`) but wrong on the card's `escrowHeld` flag. |
| 211 | **Session settlement beyond cohort seats — 1-1 sessions and set-session blocks are payable, hold ONE ESCROW PER SITTING, release per sitting by negative consent, cancel per sitting, and a block's n-1 sittings are agreed through 72-hour scheduling handshakes (2026-10-13). Product-owner rulings, the same day: wire 1-1 payment here; one escrow per sitting; provisional weekly sittings + a 72h handshake whose lapse keeps the pencilled-in time.** MVP audit rows 2.18 / 2.19, work order X2. **(A) THE DEFECTS.** `finance.auto_release_session_escrow` released only cohort SEAT escrows; the provider-cancel → auto-refund and the late-cancel forfeit existed only for seats; a set-session block booked one slot and nothing scheduled the other n-1. Under all three: `finance.place_wallet_order` refused a `service_session` / `set_session` line (PS501), and the checkout said so before Pay — a 1-1 session could not be paid for at all, so it had no escrow to release or refund. Two adjacent defects surfaced on the way: a reschedule round that carried moved `scheduling.events` but never the sitting (`projects.session_events`), which is what the join window, the 15-minute rule and the payout clock read. **(B) 1-1 PAYMENT.** A 1-1 session is a cohort of one seat (the session model in `00001155`), so it adds no parallel structure: `place_wallet_order` prices the line from the listing NOW (session price, × `session_count` for a block) at the line's `scheduled_at` (must lie ahead) and calls `projects.fn_book_session_slot`, which finds or makes the listing's ONE session engagement (`format = 'session'`, `session_kind = 'normal'`, `unlisted`, `active`, one "Sessions" stage; advisory-locked per listing), seats the buyer on a fresh one-seat `active` cohort, writes the sittings (the first at the picked time, each later one a week after the one before), holds one escrow per sitting from the paying wallet (`finance.escrows.session_event_id`, new nullable FK, `ck_escrows_sitting_on_seat`, unique per sitting), and mirrors every sitting onto the provider's calendar (`scheduling.events.source_session_event_id`, both people on the roster). The first slot is re-tested against the provider's schedule under a row lock (conflict + blackout), so two buyers paying for one slot cannot both be seated — the second is refused PS409 and charged nothing. The checkout's `walletSettles` accepts a session line that carries its time. **(C) NEGATIVE CONSENT, GENERALISED.** The sweep releases every session escrow with no open dispute (`finance.fn_escrow_has_open_dispute`, the dispute work order's predicate) `session_payout_hours` after its sitting ended: a 1-1 sitting's escrow after ITS sitting, a cohort seat's after the cohort's last (unchanged). A provider no-show refunds the escrow its sitting stands for, at ANY age — the 24h window on that path is dropped, since the escrow still being `held` is the only guard needed and a missed sweep must not forfeit a refund. A per-sitting move ends the seat only when nothing is left held (`projects.fn_settle_membership_state`: `completed` if any sitting paid the provider, else `cancelled`); a sitting that happened still pays out on a cancelled booking. **(D) CANCELLATION.** PRODUCT_SPEC §Session-Based C already rules the money (>24h refund · <24h forfeit to the freelancer · freelancer cancel = full refund any time; no partial fee — the 50% figure was retired earlier), so nothing was asked: `cancel_cohort_membership` applies it PER SITTING against each sitting's own start on a 1-1 — a client cancelling a block across the notice line comes out `partial` (far sittings refunded, near ones forfeited) — and leaves a sitting that has already started to settle on its own window or dispute. Each cancelled sitting is marked `cancelled_by_freelancer` / `cancelled_by_client`, leaves the calendar, has its open handshake withdrawn, and the one-seat cohort ends. The other party is notified (`session.cancelled`, with refunded / forfeited minor units). **(E) THE N-1 HANDSHAKES.** Each provisional sitting gets a 1-on-1 reschedule round (round 0, `counterparty`, `collecting` — the provider proposes, the client accepts or counters, the Proactive Calendar's existing flow) due `set_session_schedule_hours` (72, new param) after payment and never later than `session_cancel_notice_hours` before the provisional start. `projects.fn_lapse_session_handshakes` (job `session-scheduling-handshakes`, every 15 min) lapses each round still open at its deadline through `scheduling.close_reschedule_round` — `lapsed` already means "the original time stands" — and tells both people (`session.scheduling_lapsed`). Both are told at payment (`session.scheduling_requested`); the same sweep reminds the provider ONCE, 24h before the booking's earliest open deadline (`session.scheduling_reminder` — the notification row is the "sent" mark). The reminder is deliberately NOT routed through `comms.notification_queue`: its worker `comms.fn_process_queue` is unscheduled pending Decision #203 flag (a), and registering it would start every queued reminder platform-wide — that ruling stays with a human. Two triggers (00001860): `trg_sitting_follows_calendar` moves a sitting with its calendar mirror (which also fixes the adjacent defect for group sittings), and `trg_session_handshake_closed` confirms a provisional sitting when its round rests. **(F) DEV CONTEXT SWITCHER — NO NEW AXIS.** Nothing here is a simulated persona, capability or flag: per-sitting funding and handshakes are real data. **VERIFIED** — `supabase/probes/session_settlement.sql` in one rolled-back transaction over the on-disk `00001155` / `00001210` / `00001860` (end to end through `place_wallet_order` as the buyer: payment, slot clash, stale price, past time, handshake carried and lapsed, per-sitting release / dispute hold / 100h no-show refund, partial client cancel, provider cancel inside the window, cohort-seat regression, grants, job registry — 17 checks), and on the same in-flight definitions `cohort_session_isolation`, `single_ticket_purchase`, `scheduled_jobs`, `function_grants`, `paid_revisions` unchanged; `catalog.contract.test.ts`, `payments.contract.test.ts`. **NOT APPLIED** to the shared local DB (needs a user-approved reset or definitions apply). **FLAGGED — for a human:** (a) no UI calls the cancel door (`SessionService.cancelSeat`) — the provider-cancel → refund path is reachable by API only; (b) the handshake reminder reaches the provider only — the client is told at payment and at lapse; and `session.reminder_60m/15m/5m` before each sitting still have no producer (and their queue no worker — #203 (a)); (c) the 12h reschedule lockout vs the 24h cancel window (`PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md`'s standing flag) now also applies to block sittings; (d) the session engagement gets no Discussion room — `comms.get_or_create_project_channel` authorises the caller, who at payment is the buyer; (e) `get_session_workspace` (the dispute work order's region) still shows one escrow per seat — a block's per-sitting money is not yet rendered. | `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Session-Based C–D · `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` §Domain lifecycles · `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §Scheduled jobs · `database/finance/Tables.md` · `database/projects/Functions.md` · `00000017` · `00001155` · `00001210` · `00001860` · `00002510` · `00004005` · `00005001` · `00005010` · `00005060` · Decisions #165 / #188 / #191 / #203 |
| 212 | **A dispute is resolvable — the platform dispute lifecycle `open → awaiting_response → under_review → resolved_release \| resolved_refund \| resolved_split` (never deleted, terminal once resolved), the session attendance audit that rules a no-show claim on the room's logs, a 48-hour respond-or-escalate deadline, an operator-only ruling through the existing seat release/refund primitives, `DisputeBackendService` + routes, and a Report no-show action with a status panel and Evidence Vault upload in the session sidebar (2026-10-13).** MVP audit work order X1 (audit 2.19, §2.6 f, roadmap 0.8). **(A) THE DEFECT.** `projects.claim_session_no_show` inserted an open `finance.disputes` row that stopped the seat's auto-release, and nothing anywhere moved one: no `UPDATE finance.disputes`, no `DisputeService`, no route, no UI caller of `SessionService.claimNoShow` — a claim froze its escrow forever. The provider-no-show auto-refund also looked only at sittings that ended inside the payout window, so a missed sweep never refunded (dropped by #211 at our request). **(B) THE LIFECYCLE (PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT §3.8, `dispute_status` edited in place).** `open` — raised; the room's logs are complete only when the sitting ends. At the end the attendance audit (`finance.fn_dispute_attendance_audit`) is PRODUCT_SPEC §Dispute Resolution 3's deterministic ruling: `fn_session_presence` = `provider_no_show` → **instant refund**; a tracked room with the host AND the claimant each in it ≥ `dispute_dismiss_presence_pct` (80) % of the sitting → **dismissed** (webhook rows only — a host attestation cannot dismiss a claim against the host); anything else, an untracked room included → `awaiting_response` with `respond_by` = now + `dispute_response_hours` (48, the spec's cooling-off) and `dispute.response_requested` to the provider. The respondent's statement, either party's escalation, or the deadline (the sweep — the auto-escalation) → `under_review`. An operator (`security.is_admin()`, `ops.admin_users`) rules release · refund · split with reasons. The old `resolved` / `refunded` had no writer and are gone. **(C) SCHEMA (00000017).** `finance.disputes` gains `session_event_id`, `audit`, `respond_by`, `responded_at`, `escalated_at`, `resolved_by`, `payee_share_bp`, `settlement`, `updated_at` and three CHECKs; `escrow_id` CASCADE → RESTRICT (a dispute outlives anything it is about). `finance.dispute_messages` gains `party`; new `finance.dispute_evidence` (statement ↔ `files.items`, RESTRICT). One live dispute per escrow (`uq_disputes_escrow_live`). Never deleted, never rewritten once resolved, statements/evidence append-only — `finance.fn_dispute_records_immutable` for every role (00001830); a file filed in a live case cannot be soft-deleted (`trg_files_items_guard_evidence`); `files.fn_can_read` gains the evidence arm so the counterparty and the auditor can open it. RLS: `dispute_evidence` readable through its dispute; no client writes to any dispute table. **(D) FUNCTIONS (new 00001260).** `fn_escrow_has_open_dispute` (THE live predicate — #211's release paths, the claim and the workspace call it), `fn_settle_dispute` (the one writer of a resolved status; release → `fn_release_seat_escrow`, refund → `fn_refund_seat_escrow`, split → new `fn_split_seat_escrow` on `fn_fair_exit_split`'s arithmetic; the seat follows through #211's `fn_settle_membership_state`; an escrow already settled elsewhere is recorded `by_fact`; a chargeback-frozen one waits), `fn_advance_dispute`, `respond_to_dispute` (statement + ≤ 10 own uploaded files), `escalate_dispute`, `get_dispute`, `resolve_dispute` (operator), `sweep_disputes` (`finance-dispute-sweep`, :07/:22/:37/:52). `claim_session_no_show` now names the sitting, picks the escrow that pays for THIS sitting (per-sitting first, #211), notifies `dispute.opened` instead of the chargeback's `escrow.disputed`, and audits at once after the end; `get_session_workspace` adds `disputes` and `claimable_sitting_id`. New catalog keys `dispute.response_requested`, `dispute.escalated`. **(E) SERVICE, ROUTES, UI.** `DisputeBackendService` (`packages/backend/services/finance/` — PRODUCT_SPEC's `DisputeService`, named by the folder's `*BackendService` convention) + `dispute-model.ts`; `GET /api/finance/disputes/:id`, `POST …/respond`, `…/escalate`, `…/resolve`; the no-show route gains Zod (`NoShowClaimInputSchema`). Zod SSOT `packages/types/finance/disputes.ts` (the stale `DisputeSchema` leaves `billing.ts`). `SessionDisputes` + `DisputeCaseDialog` (DESIGN_SYSTEM §C.1) in the session sidebar: Report no-show for an attendee on the reportable sitting, the claim's status and sentence, the case with evidence upload through the files pipeline. Dev Context Switcher axis `disputeState` (none · reportable · each status). **(F) VERIFICATION.** `supabase/probes/dispute_resolution.sql`, 59/59 PASS in a rolled-back run over the shared DB with the on-disk DDL rebuilt inside the transaction (#211's 00001155 included); `scheduled_jobs.sql` expects the new job. Unit: `dispute-model.test.ts`, `workspace-disputes.test.ts`, `dispute-view.test.ts` (12). UI exercised headless on a live dev server through the dev axis (every state, both dialogs; writes blocked). **FLAGS for a human:** (a) the shared local DB has NONE of this — the enum and table changes need `deno task db:reset` (a definitions-only apply cannot change the enum); (b) there is no operator console: a ruling is `POST /api/finance/disputes/:id/resolve` with an admin's session, and PRODUCT_SPEC's `/disputes` pages are unbuilt (a page route would need an opaque dispute slug, Decision #88); (c) an `under_review` case has NO deadline — the platform never rules on money without a human, so a case nobody reviews still holds its escrow; an ops alert / SLA is unbuilt; (d) PRODUCT_SPEC Phase 1's "Refund Offer" (the parties agreeing a percentage between themselves) is not built — the cooling-off is the respond window, and a split comes only from an auditor; (e) the Evidence Vault holds the audit snapshot and the filed statements and files, not a frozen copy of the board, chats and stage files, and there is no "Dispute Summary PDF"; (f) reputation does not see platform disputes — `org` standing counts escrows `disputed` (the chargeback state), and the bad-faith-reporting penalty is unwired; (g) a dismissed claim on a whole-cohort seat with sittings ahead defers the payout to its normal release rather than paying early — a product call; (h) a provider cannot open a claim (the spec gives the claim to the client; an attendee no-show already pays the provider); (i) presence is measured first-join → last-leave, so time out of the room between a leave and a rejoin still counts towards the 80 %; (j) only seat escrows settle — no door opens any other dispute. | `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Dispute Resolution · §Session-Based B–D · `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` §3.5 / §3.8 · `database/finance/{Tables,Functions,Policies}.md` · `database/files/Functions.md` · `database/projects/Functions.md` · `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §Scheduled jobs · `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` §C.1 · `00000003` · `00000017` · `00001155` · `00001160` · `00001260` · `00001830` · `00001880` · `00002001` · `00002013` · `00002510` · `00002520` · `00004005` · `00005001` · `00005010` · `00005060` · Decisions #171 / #198 / #211 |
| 213 | **Approval never settles a ticket it has nobody to pay — a stage nobody was hired on is refused, an unclaimed prepaid ticket on a hired stage is refunded and left open, and the counts say which; plus the post-wave-7 small gaps: a confirm before Accept & release payment, one live flag for listing + instantiation, pre-settlement assignee notices, the wallet Standing's effective fee, and the two probes that predate the pending window (2026-10-13).** Post-wave-7 gap closure (`MVP_FLOW_AUDIT.md` / `MVP_FIX_PROMPTS.md`), CLOSES #197 flag (d), #194 flags (a) and (e), #191 flag (e). **(A) THE DEFECT** (reproduced on the shared DB in a rolled-back run before any edit): funding an OPEN stage through `fund_stage` holds one prepaid escrow per ticket with no payee (#197); `approve_stage` then pushed every such ticket through `fn_settle_ticket_stage` → `force_complete_stage`, so both tickets landed `completed` while `fn_release_ticket_escrow` refunded their escrows (`payment_status` back to `unpaid` on a completed ticket), the stage went `paid`, and the result said `released_count` 2 with `total_paid_cents` 0 — work recorded as done and paid that nobody was hired to do. **(B) THE RULE (00001150 §6, edited in place).** Approval pays whoever was hired. With no BOUND escrow on the stage — held or already released (an acceptance) — `approve_stage` refuses before anything moves (`55000`, "Nobody has been hired on this stage — its prepaid escrow waits for a freelancer to claim a ticket, so there is no one to pay yet."; the old "No funded (held) escrow" refusal stays for a stage nothing was funded on). On a stage that did hire, a ticket whose held escrow on the stage is still unbound is refunded through `finance.fn_refund_ticket_escrow` and NOT settled — its status stays where it stands, `payment_status` → `unpaid` — and the bound tickets settle as #194. `released_count` now counts tickets this call actually paid (it counted every ticket looped), and the result gains `refunded_count` / `refunded_cents`; the owner's "Stage settled" notice appends the refund sentence and carries both in its payload. The choice between the two options offered (refuse vs skip-and-refund) is BOTH, by case: refusing a hired stage over one unclaimed ticket would leave the client unable to close it. **(C) PIPELINE NOTICES.** `approve_stage` reads the stage's assignees BEFORE settling: a Pipeline ticket advances to its next stage when this one completes (#210), so the old post-settlement read (`current_stage_id = p_stage_id`) missed exactly those assignees. **(D) TYPES/SERVICE.** `StageApprovedSchema.refundedCount` / `refundedCents`; `live-settlement` parses them; `approveStage`'s message adds "n prepaid ticket(s) nobody claimed went back to your wallet." **(E) CONFIRM** (#194 flag (a)): **Accept & release payment** in `SubmissionReviewModal` (the one review surface the explorer and the board share) is now the trigger of a `ConfirmPopup` — "Accepting releases this ticket's escrow to the freelancer now. This cannot be undone." · **Accept & release** / **Not yet**; dismissal is rejection. **(F) ONE LIVE FLAG.** `resolveListing(itemId, live = isExploreBackendLive())`: `instantiateService` passes `isProjectsBackendLive()`, the flag its write runs under, so the listing it validates against and the instantiation are both live or both stubbed (a live listing could drive a stub draft, or a snapshot drive the live RPC). **(G) FEE.** `wallet-standing.ts` shows `platformFeeBp` from `finance.get_purchase_owner` (`fn_effective_platform_fee_bp`: a negotiated rate, else the platform param), `PLATFORM_FEE_BP` only as the fallback when the caller cannot see the subject's finances. **(H) PROBES/HYGIENE.** `escrow_release_concurrency.sql`'s `probe_moves()` and `platform_fee_single_source.sql`'s seller credit count `finance.pending_releases` as well as `finance.transactions` (a matured row once, by its ledger row); the unused `plural` import leaves `BookingBackendService.ts`; DESIGN_SYSTEM §C.1 gains `ProjectCreatePricingFields` (#195) and the accept confirmation. **VERIFIED:** `approval_settlement.sql` gains section F (fund an open stage → approval refused, nothing moves; claim one ticket → approval pays it, refunds the other at 50,000 without completing it, counts 1 / 1, claimant and client notified) — all A–F checks pass with the edited function applied in a rolled-back transaction, then again after `deno task db:reset`; `platform_fee_single_source.sql` 8/8 PASS; `escrow_release_concurrency.sql` 11/11 PASS on a disposable copy of the reset DB (dumped WITH grants — a `--no-acl` copy fails the stage doors on schema `USAGE`); `stage_funding_prepaid`, `escrow_settlement_authority`, `chain_reaction`, `paid_revisions`, `submission_door`, `single_ticket_purchase` pass; in the browser the review modal's Accept opens the confirmation with focus on **Not yet**, which sends nothing, and **Accept & release** sends exactly one review POST (intercepted, nothing moved); `deno task check` / `lint` clean, unit tests green but for the four known `theme-engine` failures. `escrow_uniqueness.sql` was not run: its dblink dials `dbname=postgres`, so it cannot run on a copy. **FLAGS for a human:** (a) a refunded unclaimed ticket stays open on a `paid` stage — a later claim would hold fresh escrow on a closed stage; whether such tickets should instead be cancelled (as Fair Exit does) is a product call; (b) `cancel_stage_fair_exit` was not re-examined for unbound escrow beyond what `fn_fair_exit_release` already refunds; | 214 | **Capacity ($W_i$) is not checked when an application or invitation is accepted — acceptance seats a freelancer and lands no ticket; the caps bite at the claim or assignment (2026-10-13).** MVP work order M6 item 3 / audit rows 2.9–2.11. **(A) THE QUESTION.** `projects.check_ticket_capacity` runs only inside `fn_assign_ticket_core` (claim, manual assignment, round robin, parallel stream); the audit asked whether `assign_from_application` and the invitation-accept path (`fn_apply_invitation_decision`) should call it or a stage analogue. **(B) THE RULING, from the spec.** PRODUCT_SPEC §Concurrency Limits defines both caps as sums of $W_i$ — a per-TICKET figure (category weight × difficulty) — over the tickets a freelancer holds, validated "before any claim or assignment". The spec does not cover seats, so nothing is added: capacity stays enforced, unchanged, at the door that hands over a ticket. The rest of M6 already holds (seat and application `FOR UPDATE`, the conditional seat flip, the per-candidate advisory lock and `fn_assignee_slot_conflict` on both accept paths). **(C) DOCS.** PRODUCT_SPEC §Concurrency Limits gains the sentence ("$W_i$ is a sum over tickets held, not seats …"). No code change.
