Recorded as #238 because BB5 and BB4 took #236 and #237 the same day. **OWNER RULINGS (question → answer → what it settles).** **(a) What does a co-owner grant carry?** [1] all three reserved rights · [2] all but archive · [3] all but withdraw → "All three (Recommended)" → a co-owner is an admin seat holding `withdraw_funds`, `manage_billing` and `archive_entity` on top of the admin preset: the owner's exact set. Only transferring ownership and the owner seat itself stay the owner's. **(b) May a co-owner hand the reserved rights on?** [1] owner only · [2] co-owners too → "Owner only (Recommended)" → only the owner grants billing, withdrawal, archiving or co-ownership, to anyone; a holder cannot pass one on, even to a member they outrank. **(c) Who may revoke?** [1] the owner, or the holder gives it up · [2] owner only · [3] owner or any co-owner → "Owner, or holder gives up (Recommended)" → the owner revokes at any time, the holder may give up their own grant (`relinquish`), nobody else may; leaving or removal clears it as before. **(d) On an ownership transfer?** [1] cleared · [2] kept → "Cleared (Recommended)" → every reserved grant on the business ends with the transfer; the new owner grants afresh. **BUILT (BB6).** (A) `org.workspace_capability` gains `manage_billing` (`00000004`, edited in place; `WorkspaceCapability` in `@projective/types/workspace`), business-only (`fn_kind_capabilities` / `capabilitiesForKind`); the owner preset holds it, the admin preset does not (`PRESET_GRANTS` unchanged). `org.fn_vault_capabilities_for` projects a business member's `manage_billing` onto the vault's, so every billing write (`fn_owner_capability(…, 'manage_billing')`) accepts a granted admin; a team's stays the owner's. (B) §7b of `00001020`: `org.fn_reserved_capabilities(kind)` and `org.fn_assert_reserved_change` — owner only (`42501 reserved:`), a new grant needs the typed `CONFIRM` (`22023 confirm:`; `RESERVED_GRANT_CONFIRMATION`). `update_workspace_member` gains `confirm` and `relinquish`, keeps the billing right on admin seats only, and checks the patch's whole effect; `upsert_workspace_role` refuses a business role carrying any reserved right (they are a named person's, never a role's — so an invitation cannot carry one either); `transfer_workspace_ownership` clears them. `isCoOwner` / `OWNER_RESERVED` / `reservedCapabilities` in `common.ts`. (C) Module gates are predicates (`packages/types/workspace/access.ts`: `canOpenFinance` · `canOpenSpend` · `canOpenBilling` · `canOpenVerification` · `spendAccess`), read by `module-registry.tsx` (`ModuleGate`, `gateAllows`) and exported for the Overview bands (BU6) and the console read (BB9): Billing and a business's Verification on `manage_billing`; Spend on any of `spend_funds` · `manage_finances` · `approve_spend`; a business's Finance on any of `contribute_funds` · `spend_funds` · `manage_finances`. (D) `finance.settle_card_payment` writes a shared wallet's card top-up as `ledger_audit` `add_funds` (the payer, what arrived, `{kind: 'card_topup'}`) in the settling transaction. | 239 | **Spend approvals bind to what they approve and are spent exactly once — `finance.fn_redeem_spend_approval` (BB4, 2026-10-17).** Builds BUSINESSES_AUDIT BF20 / BF44 / §4.3 on the spend gate of #240. **BUILT.** (A) `finance.spend_approvals` (edited in place, `00000017`): `ref_table`/`ref_id` NOT NULL with a CHECK on `baskets` · `tickets` · `project_stages` · `revisions` · `invoices` · `transfers` (a transfer names its destination wallet); `consumed_at` + `consumed_ref_table`/`consumed_ref_id`, set together and only on an `approved` row; a unique partial index allows one `pending` request per `(wallet, ref)` and a partial index serves the redeemer (`00004005`). (B) `request_spend_approval` (`00001210`): business wallets only (a team has nobody holding `approve_spend` — BF44); the ref is required and must name a row the business pays for; a stale `pending` row past its expiry is closed and a live one refuses a second (`PC409 refId:`); the threshold reads `fn_business_threshold`. (C) `fn_redeem_spend_approval(wallet, actor, amount, ref_table, ref_id, consume = true)`: the smallest approved, unexpired, unspent request for that exact ref covering the amount, filed by the actor, locked and stamped spent in the charging transaction; else `PA403 needs_approval` with the ref in DETAIL. The gate (#240) calls it on both its over-cap and threshold paths, so one approval clears one spend of both. (D) `decide_spend_approval`: a spent, rejected or expired request is never re-decided; approving moves no money except that an approved ticket request retries the auto-fund queue. (E) `fn_auto_fund_drain` checks the redeemer instead of scanning `spend_approvals` itself, files a request when the hold is refused `PF402` for `spending_limit` / `needs_approval`, and no longer lets an owner who holds `approve_spend` skip the threshold. (F) App: `SpendRequestInputSchema` gains `refTable` + `refId` (`SpendRefTable`, `SpendApprovalRefSchema` in `packages/types/finance/vault.ts`); the checkout pre-flight (`spendLimitFor`) binds `needs_approval` to the basket (`requestRef`) and reads an approved, unspent request covering the total as `allowed` (`approved: true`); a checkout `spend_limit` refusal carries `approvalRef`. **Rulings taken in the build (no source doc decided them):** (a) a `tickets` approval may be spent by any spender, because automatic holds act as the project owner; every other ref only by its requester; (b) `expires_at` (7 days from filing) bounds the whole request, deciding and spending included, so an approval given on day 6 must be spent within a day; (c) the wallet's free-form "Request spend" quick action is gone. It was offered only to members without `spend`, whom the database always refused, and a request now needs a ref the wallet page cannot name. The checkout dialog bound to the basket is BP2; (d) a team checkout's `needs_approval` verdict reads `blocked`, since no team request can be filed. A §C.3 convention (px everywhere, or rem everywhere incl. `packages/ui`) needs a ruling. (b) The bespoke 1100px tablet step had no ui token and was snapped to `--bp-xl` (1279px), so windows 1101–1279px now take the tablet adaptation. | 241 | **The business bench — Talent built per #230 (i): `org.get_business_bench`, the Talent screen, and Add to project through the profile's own picker (BU5, 2026-10-17).** BUSINESSES_AUDIT BF43, the Talent half of BD26; work order BU5 of #229. **BUILT.** (A) `org.get_business_bench(handle_or_id)` (`00001020` §17, `authenticated` in `00002510`, `STABLE`): `not_found` · `forbidden` (a non-member, or a member without `hire` — the capability the registry's Talent gate asks) · `ok` entries (`kind`, `handle`, `name`, public avatar object, `contact_handle`/`contact_name`, `engagements`, `live_now`, `last_worked_at`, `rating_average`, `rating_count`, and for the caller `profile_visible` = `org.fn_profile_visible` and `viewer_manages` = `org.fn_profile_manages`), ordered live first, then the latest delivery, then name; no row id is a field of the answer — an avatar object path keeps the storage bucket's owner-id prefix, the platform-wide avatar layout, so a public avatar URL carries it as every avatar URL does; indexes `idx_projects_client_business`, `idx_stage_assignments_stage` (`00004003`); probe `supabase/probes/business_bench.sql`. (B) `BenchEntrySchema`/`BusinessBenchSchema` (`@projective/types/workspace`), `WorkspaceBackendService.bench` (`live-bench.ts`, one RPC, a drifted row dropped), `GET /api/workspace/bench?ref=` (the screen's retry); the business `[module]` handler reads it through `resolveBench` (`workspace-ssr.ts`) for `talent` only, so `workspace-route.tsx` is untouched. (C) `TalentScreen`: a `DataTable` preset `BenchTable` (Name with the person's avatar or the team's entity mark and `@handle` · Kind as text · Engagements · Last worked · Rating), the row links to the profile, the row menu (`bench-model.ts`) is View profile · Message · Add to project; the summary is a MetaLine ("3 on the bench · 3 working now"); the empty state is one sentence + a tier-4 "Find talent on Explore"; no filled control on the page. (D) `/[handle]?add=1` opens the profile hero's Add-to-project popover for a signed-in, unrestricted visitor of a seller and is stripped on read (ROUTING.md). (E) Seed: Juno accepted on Helia's Meridian documentation stage (s3 `assigned`, an accepted invitation), so Helia's bench holds two freelancers and a team; s3 opens on a fixed day after her Haddad Studio deadline (`STAGE_STARTS` in `gen/schedules.ts`, emitted as `start_trigger_type = 'fixed_date'` + `fixed_start_date`), so the two live seats pass the accept door's double-booking guard (AC6, `projects.fn_assignee_slot_conflict`). **BUILD RULINGS.** (a) An engagement is a PROJECT billed to the business (`client_business_id`) on which the party holds a `stage_assignments` seat that is not declined / cancelled / released, on a non-draft project, either completed (stage approved or paid, assignment completed, or project completed with work begun on the stage — `in_progress` / `submitted` / `revisions`; a seat on a stage nobody started or funded is a cancelled booking, not a past engagement) or live (the live assignment set, project active or on hold, stage not approved / paid / cancelled); counted once per project; a freelancer whose account was erased (a completed account deletion request) is left out. (b) Last worked is the latest paid stage's `completed_at` (the one instant a delivery is dated by); "Now" while live; the table's own empty dash when nothing is dated (an approved, not yet paid stage); a date renders as a tabular `<time>`. (c) The rating is the mean of the `reviews.entity_reviews` the BUSINESS gave the party — written by one of its members (any membership status) on one of its own projects; a co-worker's or outsider's review on the same project is not the business's (2 dp; `freelancer`/`user` targets for a person, `team` for a team), "No reviews" at none. (d) A team's Message reaches its OWNER (a DM is person-to-person — `comms.get_or_create_dm_thread` takes a user), the menu row saying "Reaches {owner}, who runs {team}". (e) The re-hire (#230 (i)) is the profile's own Add to project, reached by `/@handle?add=1`; refused with its reason when `viewerCan.hire` is false (#236); the viewer's own row, or their own team's, refuses Message and Add to project ("That's you." · "You run {team}."); a team the viewer administers (`viewer_manages` — the profile hero offers its manager no picker) refuses Add to project ("You manage {team}.") but keeps Message (its owner is another person); a party whose profile does not open for the viewer (`profile_visible` false — every team is created invite-only, a person may go private, an archived team is hidden) stays on the bench as history, its row is no link and View profile and Add to project are refused ("{name}'s profile isn't public."), Message kept. (f) The RPC takes a handle or id (not the work order's uuid — #88, parity with `get_workspace_detail`) and is its own read, not folded into the detail (BB9's one-RPC console read stays). **FLAGS.** (g) No live door writes `stage_assignments.status = 'completed'` (only the seed does), so `org.profiles_index.delivered_count` stays 0 in production; should paying a stage stamp its assignment completed? The bench reads completion from the stage instead. (h) No seeded business member lacks `hire`; "a member without hire is 303'd" is proven by `talent-gate.test.ts` (the real `routeConsole` through the real `resolveWorkspaceConsole`, only the detail read stubbed) and the probe (a rolled-back plain member is `forbidden`), not a persona — seeding one would serve BU2 / BU6 / BU7 / BZ2 but changes that persona's chrome; owner's call. (i) A freelancer who left (`released`) drops off the bench even if they delivered earlier stages under that seat; recommended for now. (j) The profile's Add-to-project picker lists the projects the VIEWER owns or participates in (`resolveHireProjects`, the feed's `involvement: "owner"`), not every business project they may hire into: Priya (Helia's owner) sees her own Verdant project but not Hannah's Meridian or wallet projects. Recommended: the picker also lists the business projects the viewer holds `hire` on (#237). |
| 242 | **The business console's Verification module is built — where KYB stands, what is still due, what verification unlocks and the billing right's start of Stripe Connect onboarding; every KYB link lands on it, and nobody is linked to a module they cannot open (BU4, 2026-10-17).** BUSINESSES_AUDIT BF28 / BD26; work order BU4 of #229, on BB9's server-decided read and #238's billing gate. **BUILT.** (A) `BusinessVerificationScreen` renders BB9's `kyb` and `viewerCan.startKyb` and never recomputes policy: a lifecycle Tag (Unverified · In review · Verified · Action needed — a `provider_restricted` / `kyb_rejected` / `kyb_expired` code reads Action needed whatever the stored status, and `connect_account` reads Unverified whatever it, since nothing is with Stripe to review), the `keystone-check` crest through `ownerVerificationStamp` once verified (#225 (C), #227), a MetaLine ("Verified on 4 Feb 2026", in UTC, or "N requirements due"), the requirement codes as plain hairline rows, ONE tier-3 `severity="primary"` action (#225 (A)) — **Verify {business}** with the registration country when the business has no Connect account, **Continue verification** through BB9's refresh-door URL when it has one (**Verify again** after `kyb_rejected` / `kyb_expired`); where the processor is not connected there is no control, only Settings' own sentence (#225 (B), §3.11); `pageshow` clears the in-flight state when Back restores the page from Stripe — and the three things verification unlocks, each sourced (finance-model §10's Business-owner row; PRODUCT_SPEC §Identity Verification Level 3, "Paying on account", "Money leaves only to a verified payout account"). The wording is pure (`core/kyb-model.ts` + test); `ModuleScreen` routes a business's `verification` to the screen, a team's stays the placeholder with its verified note corrected from business wording. (B) No SQL or route change, and one read field: `BusinessKybSchema.processorConnected` (`toKyb` takes `isPaymentsLive()` from `live-detail.ts`; `false` by default, so the fixture read offers nothing). The start reuses `POST /api/finance/verify/kyb` → `PaymentBackendService.startKybOnboarding` (#126), whose `finance.payout_account_for` refuses anyone without the billing right (42501 → 403) before any Stripe call; `supabase/probes/business_kyb_surface.sql` pins the flag and the door agreeing per persona, the requirement codes as the Connect account moves, and a billing-granted admin (#238) reaching both. (C) No dead-end link (§3.11): `VerificationLock` on Overview and Spend and the lane's Finish link ask `canOpenVerification` — the module's own gate — instead of `manage_settings`, and the setup checklist's verification step is unlinked for a viewer who may not open it (`SetupFacts.canVerify`, set in `live-detail.ts`); a team is unchanged, its predicate being `manage_settings`. (E) Dev Context Switcher axis `businessKyb` (Auto · Unverified · In review · Action needed · Verified; In review always shows `finish_onboarding`) substitutes the module's picture after mount — `DevOverrides` + `DEV_DEFAULTS`, `DEV_BUSINESS_KYBS`, the panel's Business KYB control, `reflect()` set and delete, `readDevSeam`. **BUILD RULING (a).** BU4's acceptance asks for an admin journey with "no action, explanation", written before #230 (d) / #238 gated Verification on `manage_billing`: an admin without the grant is 303'd from the module and never linked to it, and the explanation is the KYB lock's "Ask the owner to verify the business." on Overview and Spend; the screen's own ask branch serves any viewer the server says cannot start (and the dev axis). **FLAGS for a human.** (b) Stripe returns a business's onboarding to `/settings/verification?kyb=<status>` (`onboardingReturnPath`, kept as built by #225 (D)), not to the console module; the screen says so beside the action — a `back=console` target would change the return and refresh doors. (c) Settings → Verification & payouts offers no action while a business's KYB is pending (`kybCopy`), while the module offers Verify / Continue whenever something is due (seeded Atlas Labs: pending with no Connect account) — left as #225 built it. (d) A team's Verification module is still the "Not built yet" placeholder (the Teams series). (e) The checklist's other steps (Invite → Invitations, approval threshold → Spend) still link viewers to modules they may not open (Teams C1 / BU6). | 243 | **A business checkout asks for approval in place, bound to its basket, and every business refusal is worded from one map (BP2 + BU6 step 4, 2026-10-17).** BUSINESSES_AUDIT BF20 / BF24; work order BP2 of #229, on #239's redemption and #240's gate; #230 (c) [1]. **BUILT.** (A) Contract (`packages/types/finance/checkout.ts`): `SpendLimitBlock.requestHref` — the `/businesses/{slug}/spend` link, where nothing could redeem an approval for the basket — is gone; the block gains `gate` (the #240 sub-code) and `request` (the member's latest unspent request for this basket: pending · approved · declined · expired, its amount, whether it covers the charge, "Expires 24 Oct", who decided); a blocker gains `gate` (`CheckoutBusinessGate`: purchase · kyb_required · over_limit · needs_approval · business_not_active); the session gains `businessAccount` (approvers owner-first, `canAddFunds`, `canStartKyb`, `canManageMembers`, `kybStatus`, read from `finance.vault_permissions`); `CheckoutApprovalRequestSchema` (basket, lines, reason 1–400, the page's `preselect`, no amount). `evaluateSpend`'s verdict carries the sub-code the database would raise (`policy.test.ts`). (B) Server: `POST /api/checkout/approval` → `CheckoutBackendService.requestApproval` narrows the lines as `create` does, computes the charge in the listings' own currency (the business wallet's) and files through `wallet-actions.fileSpendRequest`, the one door `/wallet` files through too, answering the fresh session in the page's deep-link scope with a sentence the payment step shows; "already waiting" (PC409) answers the session when it shows the member's own request pending, else a 409 naming another member's request on the shared basket, and "needs no approval" (22023) answers the session when it agrees, else a 409 — the dialog never closes on nothing (§3.11). `live-buyer.spendPositionFor` reads the wallet in the charge currency, its envelope rolled at `resets_at` as `fn_check_spending_limit` rolls it, its threshold with the platform's `vault_approval_threshold_cents` behind a NULL column (`fn_business_threshold`, closing #239 (f) / #240 (b)), and the member's request through RLS; it matches an approval against the charge in its own currency, and a request bears on Pay only while the order needs approval — a pending one holds it, an approved covering one clears it, and one left over for an order that has since dropped under every gate does neither. Only a stored basket binds: an owner with no basket row yet reads the synthetic `default` basket, which binds no request (the review's service smoke caught Atlas Labs' checkout session answering 503 on `ref_id = 'default'`). `checkout-refusal.ts` (pure, tested) maps refusals at Pay: PA403 / PK403 carry their gate, and `55000 business_not_active:` is a `not_authorised` refusal instead of a 503 (closes #236 flag (e)); a business's KYB refusal is said once (the spend-limit blocker steps aside for `verification_required`). (C) BU6 step 4: `apps/web/features/workspaces/core/refusal-copy.ts` — `BUSINESS_REFUSAL_COPY` (sentence, action label, ask) for hire · purchase · manage_projects · not_funder · kyb_required · over_limit · needs_approval · business_not_active · entitlement · pool_short, and `SPEND_REQUEST_COPY` for a member's own request; re-exported from `workspace-model.ts`; tested over every code and context. (D) UI: `core/business-gate.ts` (pure, tested — no href it can produce reaches `/spend`) and `BusinessSpendGate`: the map's sentence as an InlineNotice, a MetaLine, ONE control — tier-3 **Request approval** opening `RequestApprovalDialog` (FormControl + Textarea, tier-3 Send request); "Waiting for approval from …" with the amount, the expiry and Check again; "Approved by … Placing this order uses the approval."; tier-1 **Add money** to `/wallet?w=business:<id>&do=top_up&back=<checkout>` (`wallet/core/wallet-intent.ts`; `/wallet` opens the action once and its done step offers Back to checkout); a tier-4 link to `/businesses/:h/verification` for a startKyb holder, else "Ask the owner to verify the business."; the blocker list, the rail's "Pay is off…" line, a refused attempt's headline and reasons (`refusalLines`: a gated refusal is the headline, once) and Buy Now word gated blockers from the map; while the gate offers a fill (Request approval, Add money) the rail's Buy Now is not rendered (`gateTakesCommit`, §B.8.2's card-step precedent); the dialog says a refusal of the reason at the field and any other refusal for the form (an assertive InlineNotice); `SpendLimitNotice` keeps only the envelope's figures on a business. Dev Context Switcher axis **`checkoutGate`** (auto · needs approval · over limit · pending · approved · no approver · pool short · KYB): `checkout/core/dev-gate.ts` substitutes the payment step's view of a real business session, client-side, writes untouched. No SQL change (the BB3 / BB4 probes and `supabase/probes/checkout_approval_reads.sql` run green); `do` / `back` are URL intents (ROUTING). **BUILD RULINGS (no source settled them).** (a) The map lives in the leaf `refusal-copy.ts`, re-exported from `workspace-model.ts`: the prompt names `workspace-model.ts`, whose registry → glyph → stylesheet chain blocks `deno test` and would put every console glyph in the checkout bundle. (b) `over_limit` also offers Request approval beside the remaining envelope: #240 (C) has one approval clear the envelope and the threshold, and `request_spend_approval` files over-cap requests. (c) The remaining envelope comes from the session's `spendLimit` (the `finance.spending_limits` row the gate counts), not the refusal DETAIL, which carries none (`00001210`); it is named only when the cap, not the per-transaction ceiling, is what the order outruns. (d) A short pool is said before the spend gate (the database checks the balance first) and only on the wallet route — a business card or Invoice pays without the pool; #230 (c) [2] / [3] add no on-account fallback. (e) The gate is a row — InlineNotice + MetaLine + one control — because InlineNotice hosts only a text action. (f) PRODUCT_SPEC's "Contribute" ships as **Add money**; it opens the wallet's top-up through a URL intent because `MoveDialog` is private to `/wallet`, the way back allowlisted to `/checkout`. (g) The request amount is server-derived; the client sends lines and a reason. **FLAGS for a human.** (h) **RULED by the owner (2026-10-17): tier 3 stands** — Request approval files a request and moves no money, so the amber stays Pay's; DESIGN_SYSTEM §B.8.1 states the exception. Tier: Request approval and Send request are tier 3 (workflow commits that move no money, like Approve, #225 (A); DESIGN_SYSTEM already lists "spend request" among the commits that stay tier 3) against §B.8.1's "every commit of the checkout" — open with #157 (j). (i) **Closed in review:** #239 (f) / #240 (b) — the pre-flight now reads the platform threshold behind a NULL wallet threshold (`security.platform_params`, readable by any signed-in member), so a platform-only threshold is pre-flighted like a wallet's own. (j) #239 (e): with no other approver the member reads "Ask the owner to add an approver", and no request nobody could decide is offered; the owner (`manage_members`) reads "Give a member approval rights in Members." instead of being told to ask themselves. (k) No withdraw door: a pending request on a basket that has since GROWN waits for its decision or its 7-day expiry (BU2's planned Withdraw); one on a basket that has dropped under every gate no longer holds Pay. (l) Add money is the card top-up only — no transfer from the member's own wallet — and is locked with its reason where the processor is not connected. (m) #230 (c) at the other funding doors (`FundStageDialog`, the board's Checkout N) is not built here. (n) `purchase` is not pre-flighted (the vault grants do not carry it, and no client-callable read answers the caller's workspace capabilities short of the console's detail read): a spender without it learns at Pay, worded by the map — and can meanwhile FILE a basket request (`request_spend_approval` checks vault `spend` only) that, once approved, no Pay of theirs can spend; closing it needs `can_purchase` on `finance.get_purchase_owner` (SQL) so the checkout and `requestApproval` refuse first. (o) The approver names read `finance.vault_permissions` (#238's projection); a lagging sync could name a stale approver — display only, the database decides. (p) BU4's lock and screen word "Ask the owner to verify the business." themselves (#242); they should take it from `BUSINESS_REFUSAL_COPY.kyb_required.ask` (the same words today). (q) The running dev server (port 13041) served stale `packages/*` modules during this build (the route's Zod schema read as undefined; peers' `WorkspaceBackendService.bench` likewise), so the route was smoke-tested against the service directly. | 244 | **A business's `/:handle` is a buyer profile — Message leads (to the owner), Manage business for its editors, Corporate verified only on KYB, the client rating as the trust readout, no Standing, and its visibility honoured (BP1, 2026-10-17).** Builds BUSINESSES_AUDIT BF45 / BF46 / BF47 / BF48; work order BP1 of #229, on BB5's visibility (#236 (C)). **BUILT.** **(A) Visibility, decided in SQL.** `org.fn_profile_visible`'s business arm now takes the team rule — an outsider sees a business only while it is `active` AND `public`/`unlisted`; an active member always does — so a draft, archived or `invite_only` business is NULL to every stranger-facing reader at once (`get_profile_view`, `get_profile_owner`, every section read, `get_profile_cards` in saved / follow lists, the showcase / settings RLS and `scheduling.fn_schedule_is_public`), and `/@handle` answers the existing 404 "Profile not found" worded exactly as an unknown handle (no route change). `org.get_profile_view`'s business arm returns its real `visibility` (was hard-coded `public`), a `workspace` object — `hidden_reason` (`archived` › `draft` › `invite_only`; NULL while anybody may open it, so only a member is ever told) and `viewer_edits` / `viewer_settings` (the viewer's OWN seat's `edit_profile` / `manage_settings` via `fn_member_can`, never fn_profile_manages' platform-admin arm) — and `contact` (the owner's handle and name). Zod: `ProfileView.workspace` (`ProfileWorkspaceFacts`: `hiddenReason`, `consoleHref`, `settingsHref`) and `ProfileView.messageTo`, both optional and business-only; `MemberEntry.since`. A member reading a hidden business sees `ProfileMemberNotice` above the page (InlineNotice, lock glyph, "{name} is a draft." / "is archived." / "is invite-only." + "Only members can see this page." — a fact, never a promise of what one step would change: a business is created `draft` AND `invite_only`, so publishing alone would not open it). **BP1's tier-4 link to Settings is WITHHELD (review ruling):** the Settings module is still a `ModulePlaceholder` whose copy sends visibility to a profile editor that offers a business none, so the link would land on no surface that can act (root CLAUDE.md §3.11 outranks the spec line); the read still carries `settingsHref` (`manage_settings` holders) so the link returns in one line once Settings can publish or change visibility — FLAG (d). **(B) The rig.** `rigFor` gives a business its own `layout: "business"`: no Hire, no Add to project, whatever the viewer; **Message** is the one filled control (tier 3, the `severity="primary"` class set Hire uses) and Follow the icon-only tier-4 circle; a member holding `edit_profile` (`manages`, from `consoleHref`) sees **Manage business** (a tier-4 `<a>` to `/businesses/:h`) in Message's place, and the owner nav's Preview note says so ("They see Message where you see Manage business"). The layout resolves no services or products for a business, so no price or purchase surface can render on its page (root CLAUDE.md §3.10). Client and enterprise buyer rigs are unchanged. **(C) Message reaches a person.** `dm-{business slug}` resolved nobody (both messaging doors resolve a DM handle through `org.users_public` only), so the old Message was a dead control (§3.11); a business's Message now opens the DM with its **owner** (`messageTo`), the popout titled "{Business} · {Owner}" and the phone drawer "Message {Owner} at {Business}". A read that names no owner (`contact.handle` absent) withholds Message outright (`rigFor` `messageable: false` → `primary: "none"`, Follow alone) and the popover opens nothing — a business never falls back to `dm-{business slug}`. The mapper's `invite_only` → `private` reading is business-only; a team's visibility maps exactly as before (Teams P1's). **(D) Trust.** The crest is `corporate_verified` only while `kyb_status = 'verified'` — there is no `verification_tier` column on `org.business_profiles`; tier 3 is `org.profiles_index`'s derived column over the same `kyb_status` — and a business carries no Standing and no adornments (SQL NULL / `[]` and the mapper's seller gate), both pinned by the probe and the mapper test. No Client Trust Score is computed anywhere, so the hero's rating metric on a business reads **"Client rating · N reviews from freelancers"** — the freelancer-given `rating_average` / `rating_count`, the score's one computed input — and no figure is invented. **(E) Work.** A business's completed projects ARE its client proof (`get_profile_past_projects`' existing arm: commissioned by it, `completed`, `public`, portfolio display `allowed`) and lead Hiring now; a roster row's second line is a MetaLine — title or role · "Since 2026" (`tabular`), no chips; `get_profile_roster`'s business arm answers the title, else the BASE PRESET's label (`Owner` · `Admin` · `Member`) — a custom role's name is member-only under `org.business_roles` RLS and no longer reaches the public roster — plus `since` (`joined_at`). **(F) Reviews.** Unchanged in SQL (a read-side dedupe would make the list disagree with `rating_count` and the hero figure); the stance filter is dropped on a business, which only ever holds the client stance (`ReviewsList` `stances={false}`), and the empty states read business sentences. **(G) Editor wording.** PRODUCT_SPEC's owner-views sentence names "a business's owner or an admin (whoever holds its profile right)", as the database grants `edit_profile`; the team clause is #250 (b)'s, corrected by Teams P1. **Conflicts settled here (reversible; each is a FLAG).** BP1's acceptance says "unlisted business is 404 to a stranger"; #236 (C) as built, its FLAG (a) and the platform meaning for people and teams resolve `unlisted` by handle — #236 is followed, so the outsider 404s are `draft` · `archived` · `invite_only` and the acceptance runs against `invite_only` — which means BP1's acceptance line "unlisted business is 404 to a stranger" is **NOT MET as written**: it is the one acceptance item awaiting the owner (FLAG a). Kept rather than flipped on review because #230 (h) ruled "visibility like teams", the committed BB5 directory view documents `unlisted` as "resolve by handle — the profile page", and a flip would leave the console's counterparty faces (`org.profiles_index`) linking to a 404. #119 (B) "Preview = exactly what a visitor sees" is AMENDED for business editors only (Manage business replaces Message, the note says so), mirroring Teams P1's "Manage team … instead of Hire". **Dev Context Switcher (added on review, root CLAUDE.md §5):** `businessPage` (Auto · Draft · Archived · Invite-only · Missing) rides `pj.dev.business-page`, which the `/[handle]` middleware lays over a business's read in development only (`features/profile/core/business-page-sim.ts`) — a member's hidden reason, or the outsider's 404 — the only route to those states, since both seeded businesses are active + public; it grants nothing. Verified by `supabase/probes/business_public_profile.sql` (A: the 3 × 3 status × visibility matrix for a stranger, a guest, a member and the owner across six readers; B: viewer facts per seat and the owner contact; C: crest on/off with KYB, no Standing; D: roster title / base preset / custom role / `since`; E: client proof gates; F: every business review is by a provider on a project the business commissioned, one per (project, reviewer), and — a seed tripwire, not a guarantee — one per (project, provider entity); G: draft team, public person, no new keys on a team) — failing 41 checks before the SQL change, passing after; `live-profile.test.ts` and `profile-model.test.ts`. **FLAGS for a human.** (a) **RULED by the owner (2026-10-17): an unlisted business opens by link**, as a team's (#230 (h)); only `draft` · `archived` · `invite_only` are missing to an outsider, and BP1's "unlisted business is 404 to a stranger" acceptance line is superseded. Unlisted — was an unmet acceptance line: BP1 says "unlisted business is 404 to a stranger"; as built it resolves by link. Confirm, or flip it: the business arm of `fn_profile_visible` to `= 'public'`, an `unlisted` hidden reason (the SQL CASE, `ProfileHiddenReason`, `hiddenNoticeText`), probe A's `v_open`, PRODUCT_SPEC §Businesses "Visibility" — and decide `org.profiles_index`'s by-handle row with it (#236 C). (b) Message recipient: the owner's personal DM — the alternatives are a designated contact member or an entity inbox. (c) The Client Trust Score has no definition or weighting (PRODUCT_SPEC names it; finance-model §16.3 has no formula; `system_penalty_score` and `security.penalties` of type `trust_score` exist unpublished) — define it in finance-model §16 in a later work order; until then the client rating stands in. (d) No UI publishes a business (draft → active) or changes its visibility: the Settings module is still a `ModulePlaceholder` whose copy sends visibility to the profile editor, which offers visibility to a person only — so every UI-created business stays `draft` + `invite_only` and missing to outsiders; the business Settings screen should gain Publish and Visibility on the existing doors (`org.set_workspace_status`, `POST /api/workspace/update`) — Teams C6 builds that screen for a team only — and the member notice's Settings link (BP1 SPEC 1) renders from `settingsHref` only then. `ModuleScreen.tsx` was not edited (a sibling work order owned it). (e) "One aggregate review per business–freelancer engagement" is a WRITE rule with no writer (reviews come only from the service role and the seed); `UNIQUE (target, reviewer, project)` would let a team engagement yield one review per member — enforce one per (project, provider entity) in the future review RPC. Today the read is unaggregated (`get_profile_reviews` returns every row), and probe F's per-engagement check holds over the seed only. Note the two scopes: PRODUCT_SPEC §Reviews "Entity Consolidation" puts the one-aggregate rule on reviews written BY a business's members ("individual members within a business cannot spam reviews"), BP1 on reviews written ABOUT a business by freelancers — neither is enforced; decide both in the review-writer work order. (f) Client and enterprise buyer rigs keep the neutral Message + Follow text pair (BP1 is business-gated) — align every buyer rig on the business treatment in a follow-up? (g) Two leaks remain for a hidden business: `search.sync_business_to_index` still indexes draft / invite-only businesses (#236 FLAG b), so a search hit can lead to the 404, and a public project brief still names its poster with a link to the now-missing `/@handle`. (h) RESOLVED: the roster imports U1's `MetaLine`, which landed on main in b71da633 — no merge-order constraint remains. | `supabase/migrations/00001040_functions_org_profiles.sql` · `supabase/probes/business_public_profile.sql` · `packages/types/profile/{profile,tabs}.ts` · `packages/backend/services/profile/{live-profile,live-profile.test}.ts` · `apps/web/features/profile/core/{profile-model,profile-model.test}.ts` · `apps/web/features/profile/components/{ProfileRig,ProfileMetrics,ProfileMemberNotice,OwnerNav,ProfileTabContent}.tsx` · `apps/web/features/profile/components/tabs/{WorkTab,ReviewsTab}.tsx` · `apps/web/features/profile/components/work/WorkRoster.tsx` · `apps/web/features/profile/islands/{ReviewsList,ProfileMessagePopover}.island.tsx` · `apps/web/features/profile/styles/{profile-base,profile-work}.css` · `apps/web/features/profile/core/{business-page-sim,business-page-sim.test}.ts` · `apps/web/routes/[handle]/{_layout.tsx,_middleware.ts}` · `apps/web/features/devtools/{core/dev-context.ts,components/DevContextPanel.tsx}` · `database/org/{Functions,Tables}.md` · `business/PRODUCT_SPEC.md` · `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` · `design-system/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` (§B.8.1, §D.7.7) · `architecture/{BUSINESSES_AUDIT,ROUTING}.md` · Decisions #119 / #183 / #226 / #227 / #229 / #230 / #236 / #250 |
| 252 | **Subscriptions, agency scale and the public `/pricing` page — audit, canonical patches and work orders; thirteen questions for a human (2026-10-17).** Source: `architecture/SUBSCRIPTIONS_AUDIT.md` (read-only, findings `L*` · `M*` · `G*` · `R*` · `D*`) and `architecture/SUBSCRIPTIONS_BUILD_PROMPTS.md` (S0–S8, X1–X2, Z1), commissioned by a brief to add a public pricing page and an agency team tier. **Recorded now (no ruling needed — each follows the existing rules):** (A) `/pricing` is a planned `(public)` route (`PRODUCT_SPEC.md` sitemap, `ROUTING.md`), lane-less, guest and signed-in alike, its handle already reserved (`packages/types/profile/reserved.ts`); it shows **three audience tabs over the two payment planes** (personal · entity), not a third plane. (B) Its figures are `finance.plans` GBP minor units rendered through `MoneyView` from the request's `AsyncLocalStorage` currency scope, re-projected in place from their origin; the base is never converted at rest, and the GBP charge is disclosed. (C) An agency tier may monetise **footprint and tools only** (seats, public projects, storage, audit retention, export) — never execution capacity, never Standing (it must not carry `discovery_boost`), never an unlimited or seat-scaled proposal pool, never a fee discount outside `finance.negotiated_rates`. These are written into `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §7 "Public pricing" and "Agency scale" (marked **Planned**). (D) The proposed `finance` schema (`plan_prices`, the agency seed rows, `custom_treasury_export`) is **not** written into `database/finance/Tables.md`, because that folder documents migrated schema only (`database/CLAUDE.md`); prompt S2 carries the exact Tables.md patch to land with the migration. **Corrections to the brief** (audit §0): there is no `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §D.5 (the shell is `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` Part D); only three switches fail open — `business_plan_gates_enforced` is on and split templates are always enforced, and plan refusals are `PK402` → 402, never 403; tickets are not a public pool, so agency fairness is a proposal-pool and ranking question; the chart tier is Decision #1, already in `packages/ui/display/charts`; `/teams/:teamHandle/finance` and `/wallet/analytics?w=team:{id}` already exist; no plan checkout, Stripe Billing, `/api/finance/plans` or `SubscriptionBackendService` exists to extend. **Defects found (no ruling needed):** guests cannot read the plan catalogue (both policies and the grant are `authenticated`-only, L13); `PlanBlock` renders prices with `formatMoney`, not `MoneyView` (D11); invite does not check payout readiness, so an agency owner learns of an unverified invitee only at accept (G4, #196 (b)). **FLAGS for a human:** (a) **Tier name** — `team_enterprise` collides with Enterprise, the buyer entity kind since #231; recommend code `team_agency`, label "Agency". (b) **Prices** — the agency price (flat vs per seat), every annual price and its discount, and Business Pro's still-`NULL` price (FM §1.3); recommend a `finance.plan_prices` child table (one row per plan × interval) over second plan codes, and "Price coming soon" with no button on `/pricing` until a price is set. (c) **Agency proposal pool** — a fixed finite ceiling per team, never seat-scaled; recommend 300/wk + 8 per 10 h as starting dials. (d) **Hierarchical treasury** (master vault → department → squad) has no schema, no spec entry, and teams have no departments; recommend per-engagement contribution agreements first and no `nested_split_rules` key until specified (X1). (e) **Slots** — recommend `pricingHeaderFor` (the plane switcher band, no figure, no purchase control) and **no** `pricingLaneFor`. (f) **How a plan is paid** — recommend Stripe Billing Checkout, card-only, never a wallet debit; who may buy for an entity (owner / `manage_billing`, #238); downgrades at period end. (g) **Agency importers** (Jira/ClickUp → stages, attachments mounted read-only) — no spec entry; recommend CSV + one tracker first, specified before any build (X2). (h) **Enforcement family** of the new agency levers — recommend public-project caps stay fail-open with the footprint family and `custom_treasury_export` is enforced like split templates. (i) Whether invite should refuse an unverified invitee or only say so — recommend say so (readiness shown, refusal stays at accept). (j) **Entitlement keys** — recommend reusing `team_seats` (+ `is_unlimited`), `weekly_proposals`, `audit_log_retention_days`; adding only `custom_treasury_export`; no `agency_seats`. (k) The brief's "Team / Agency" and "Business / Enterprise" tab grouping puts the enterprise buyer tier on the business tab; recommend that grouping, with Enterprise as its custom-priced column. (l) Whether `/pricing` should show the 5% service fee and the Standing commission taper — recommend the fee as one sentence (same on every plan), the taper as one line linking to help, so the page never implies a plan buys a lower fee. (m) **Which subscription states keep a plan** — SQL `fn_active_plan` honours trialing · active · past_due; `PlanBackendService` also honours `paused`; `subscription_grace_days` (7) is read by nothing; recommend trialing · active, past_due inside the grace window, not paused. Prompt S0 puts (a)–(m) to the owner; S1 stops on (m), S2 on (a)–(c) and (h), S4 on (f), X1 on (d), X2 on (g). |
| 290 | **A workload report suspends the ticket and moves no money — the assignee could release their own escrow with one INSERT (2026-10-17).** **(A) THE DEFECT, reproduced by execution** (one rolled-back transaction on the seed; ticket held 800000): the _File workload report_ policy admits a ticket's assignee to INSERT into `projects.ticket_workload_reports` (`projects` is exposed through PostgREST and `authenticated` holds INSERT on the table). `trg_open_workload_report` → `projects.fn_open_workload_report` cleared `current_assignee_id`, and `projects.fn_ticket_escrow_sync`'s "freelancer removed mid-work" branch released the whole held escrow to that same freelancer (`released`, 40000 fee, 760000 into their pending balance, ticket back in the backlog). No client action happened anywhere in that chain. The code called this the "Frozen rule"; no decision sanctioned it, and `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §Dispute Mechanism: Workload Intensity Reporting says a report hides the ticket and suspends work until the client adjusts $W_i$, paying nobody. The spec wins on a business rule (§0). **(B) BUILT.** `fn_open_workload_report` now suspends. It is refused unless the ticket is `claimed` / `in_progress` (which also keeps one report open per ticket). The ticket becomes `reported_hidden` with its assignee and held escrow kept, and `hidden_until` is stamped on the ticket and, newly, on the report: the expiry sweep reads the report's value and nothing used to set it. `projects.fn_resume_reported_ticket(ticket, report)` (internal) ends one report's suspension: the ticket goes back to `in_progress` when it is still suspended on that report and still held, and the countdown is lifted either way. The client answers by raising $W_i$: `fn_ticket_immutability_guard` lets a non-assignee raise (never lower) a `reported_hidden` ticket's `workload_intensity`, and `trg_adjust_workload_report` (`AFTER UPDATE OF workload_intensity`) resolves the report `adjusted` and resumes the work. The same guard refuses the reporter any `status` or $W_i$ change while suspended (`42501`). `fn_resolve_expired_workload_reports` and `fn_flag_bad_faith_report` resume the work instead of leaving the ticket in the backlog. Because a suspended ticket now carries a hold, `cancel_stage_fair_exit` (and the preview's `tickets_to_cancel`) cancels it with its stage and lifts the countdown, and `remove_project_member` settles it like any held ticket (§Freelancer Removal Mid-Ticket). Probe `supabase/probes/workload_report_suspension.sql`: it fails on the old functions and passes on these. `function_grants`, `fair_exit_settlement`, `escrow_settlement_authority`, `business_authority`, `ledger_immutability`, `team_search_workload` and `workspace_detail_payload` pass with the new definitions applied. Docs: `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` (the section above), `PRODUCT_MANAGEMENT.md` §3.6 rule 7, `database/projects/{Functions,Tables,Policies}.md`. No schema or Zod change. **FLAGS for a human:** (a) **The expiry sweep is not scheduled.** No `ops.scheduled_jobs` row runs `fn_resolve_expired_workload_reports` (or `fn_release_expired_claims`), so until one does, a suspended ticket waits for the client's raise, an audit or a settlement. Recommend registering both (Decision #203). (b) **"Both parties incur a penalty"** on non-response, but the sweep penalises only the client. (c) **Ruled here; please confirm.** After the window lapses, or once a report is dismissed, the work resumes at the original $W_i$ (the spec says only that the ticket is hidden for 48 h). A report may be filed only while the ticket is claimed or in progress. (d) **A settlement leaves the report open.** If the client approves, Fair-Exits or removes the freelancer during the window, the report stays `open`, and the sweep later penalises the client for not adjusting a ticket they settled. (e) **Only the owner can answer.** The _View tickets_ arm hides a suspended ticket from every project-access viewer, and _Manage tickets_ admits only the owner and the assignee, so a business's other reviewers can neither see the ticket nor raise its $W_i$. (f) **Seen in passing, not fixed.** `fn_ticket_immutability_guard` exempts the assignee entirely, so a freelancer can rewrite their own claimed ticket's `unit_price_cents` (verified by rolled-back execution). `finance.fn_hold_ticket_escrow` prices the ticket's next-stage hold (the Chain Reaction, #210) from that column, so the client's wallet can be held at a price they never agreed; releasing it still needs approval. |
